Shimmer is US-028a, not a US-028 AC.

### US-029 GPU pipeline: shading + edge pass on the GPU, parity page  [Priority: P0] [Status: testing]
> **Owner hardware gate run (2026-09-23, owner's Chrome, ANGLE Intel 0x7D45 D3D11, window >= 1600x900, fresh port 8802):** `?gpucompare=1` **ALL PASS** on all 5 poses: glyph 100.00% (non-edge), fg/bg outside +-4 = 0, fgMax 1, bgMax 0, depth 100%. `?debug=1` overlay while playing: **fps 60.0, frame 1.56 ms, grid draw 2.10 ms, shade: gpu, upload 0.40 ms, gpu 0.25 ms** (budget <= 4 ms). World tower, farReady 100%. Gate criterion 'runs on the owner's hardware' is MET.
As a player, I want the renderer to run its heavy per-cell work on my graphics card, so that the world can get bigger, sharper and steadier without the game slowing down.
(D-009 stage 1 = architecture section 14 option C. **This story is the D-009 gate.**)
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] **Architect tech notes written before dev** (engine story): texture layout of the G-buffer upload and the material/data textures, pass order, the GLSL port plan of `shadeDetailFast` + the edge pass, how `bindShading` feeds both paths.
- [ ] Render-to-texture pipeline on WebGL2 (`gl2` back-end): the CPU sector caster stays authoritative and writes the US-028 G-buffer; the G-buffer is uploaded once per frame (`texSubImage2D`, zero per-frame JS allocation); a fragment pass (one fragment per cell) computes glyph, fg and bg from it (US-028 v2 shading, texel-class glyphs, fog v2) plus the edge pass, writing the two `cols x rows` RGBA8 cell textures that `present()` (D-005) already samples. `present()` is unchanged.
- [ ] Material table, glyph ramps and LOD/fog data (US-028 `lodGates`, `fog.sparse`) reach the GPU as data textures/uniforms built from the same flattened records as the JS path; nothing tower- or game-specific in GLSL (D-006).
- [ ] Determinism: hashes are `uint` bit-exact with the JS hashes and use the seed; no `gl_FragCoord`-anchored noise; the only time input is the `timeSec` uniform.
- [ ] **Parity page `?gpucompare=1`**: renders the same frame on both paths over the `bench-cast.mjs` pose set, reads the GPU cell textures back with `readPixels` (test only, never in the frame loop), and reports per pose and in total: **glyph match >= 99 %** excluding edge cells (cells whose 4-neighbour kind differs, same rule as `compare-detail-export.mjs`), **fg and bg within +-4 per channel**, depth within 1 %. Shows PASS/FAIL on screen and in the console.
- [ ] Debug views (URL flag or key) render `gbuf.kind`, `planeId` and edge rule as colours, for GLSL debugging.
- [ ] **GPU budget: <= 4 ms per frame** at 160x60 on the owner's laptop, measured via `?bench=1` (`EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2` where available, else frame time); the JS frame time does not rise versus the US-028 baseline (the upload cost, ~0.4 ms, is reported).
- [ ] **Runs on the owner's real hardware** (Chrome, owner's laptop): the game and `?gpucompare=1` run with zero console errors, and the owner (via the main session) confirms the result. A tester run on another machine alone does not satisfy this.
- [ ] (D-017) **The JS path is the correctness reference only, not a playable fallback**: no WebGL2 or context-creation failure -> a clear full-screen "WebGL2 required" message (short how-to: update browser / enable hardware acceleration), no crash. A software renderer (`UNMASKED_RENDERER` contains SwiftShader / llvmpipe / Basic Render) -> run the GPU path with a one-line "performance may be poor" warning. `?gpu=0` stays a dev/debug switch showing the JS reference in-browser (any grid, speed irrelevant). `shadetest`, `bench-cast` and the designer oracles still run headless in Node and pass unchanged. The F3 overlay (or console until US-018) shows `gpu` / `cpu`.
- [ ] Public API unchanged: `renderWorld(fb, world, cam)` picks the GPU pipeline when `rt.backend === 'gl2'` and `opts.gpu !== false`. `node tools/check-deps.mjs` OK.
- [ ] **Gate rule:** if the parity thresholds or the owner-hardware run fail, the programmer gets **one** rework. If it still fails, the story closes as "gate failed", M1 switches to **plan A** (PO writes US-004c: CPU optimisations), US-030 leaves M1, and US-006/US-007/US-016 are built on the CPU. The main session records the outcome in `docs/decisions.md` under D-009.
Design needed: no.
Notes / dependencies: US-028 `done`. Grid stays 160x60 in this story. Tester runs `?gpucompare=1` and records the numbers in `docs/test-reports/US-029.md`.

**Tech notes (architect, 2026-09-23).** Durable parts (texture layouts, module boundaries, GL-state rules, budgets) are in `docs/architecture.md` 14.1; this is the build plan. Scope guard: the CPU caster, `computeDerivatives` and `fillSky` stay exactly as they are; nothing here touches `sectorCaster.js`, `GBuffer.js` fields or the oracles. US-030 deletes the upload (items 2-3), so keep it self-contained.

1. **Where it plugs in.** New folder `engine/render/gpu/` (file list in 14.1). `GpuCellPipeline` is created by the game bootstrap (later by `renderWorld`) only if `rt.backend === 'gl2' && opts.gpu !== false && !isSoftwareRenderer(gl) && DP && table.allV2` (`bindShading` gains `allV2: boolean` = every record has `.v2`; `?detail=0` therefore always means CPU). Per frame, after `computeDerivatives` and instead of `shadeSurfaces` + `edgePass`: `pipeline.frame(fb, light)` (stores refs, sets `pending`). Everything GPU-side then happens inside the present hook (item 4). If `pipeline.ready` is false (compile failure, context lost, disabled) the caller runs the JS passes - decide **before** shading, once per frame: `const gpu = pipeline && pipeline.ready;`.
2. **G-buffer upload = repack + 4 `texSubImage2D`.** One repack loop over N cells into preallocated staging arrays (allocated in the constructor/resize only): `GI` (`Uint32Array` 2N -> RG32UI): `x = planeId >>> 0`, `y = kind | face<<8 | mask<<12 | mat<<16`; `GA` (`Float32Array` 4N -> RGBA32F): u, v, z, aoD; `GD` (4N -> RGBA32F): dudx, dvdx, dudy, dvdy; `fb.depth.depth` uploads directly as R32F (no staging; `Infinity` is a legal float32 texel). After the repack, `rt.cells.mask.fill(0)` (item 4). Budget: repack <= 0.10 ms, uploads <= 0.35 ms (422 KB), draws <= 0.05 ms; report `pipeline.stats.uploadMs` in `?bench=1`.
3. **Data textures** built in `ShadeTextures.js` from the bound `MaterialTable` (pure JS, no `gl` - Node-testable): `MAT_F` RGBA32F, `MAT_I` RGBA32I (`isampler2D`; seeds and `-1` set ids stay signed), `SET_I` RGBA32I, `SET_F` R32F, `GAIN` R32F 256x1; exact slot tables in 14.1. Pack-time asserts (throw, never clamp): tones <= 4, overlay tints <= 8, `maxAlt` <= 8, set levels <= 32, `nDark + 4*nFam` <= 62. Everything scalar (shading, fog incl. the 2+2 fog glyph codes, ao, `faceK[7]`, `TAN22/TAN68`, edge `fogMax` + 8 rule glyph codes/gains, `uLight`, `uTimeSec`) is a plain uniform set once at `bind()`; only `uLight`/`uTimeSec` are set per frame. `bind(table, DP)` is called at start and whenever `bindShading` reruns (resize -> cellAspect).
4. **Composition without touching the presenter shader.** `CellBuffer` gets `mask: Uint8Array(N)`; `setCell`/`setCellRGB` store `mask[i] = 1`, `clear()` zeroes it. On the GPU path the JS layer in `rt.cells` holds only what JS wrote this frame (sky, pause overlay, F3 text), so mask == 1 means "JS cell wins". `RenderTargetGL.present()` keeps its uploads and its draw; between them it calls `this._cellPass?.()` (`rt.setCellPass(fn|null)`), and then re-binds its own program, units 0-2 and `bindFramebuffer(null)` + canvas viewport before drawing (present must not rely on state persisting across the hook). The hook: upload (item 2) -> pass 1 shade -> pass 2 edge -> restore. Pass 1 reads `fgTex/bgTex` (JS layer) + G-buffer + data textures, writes MRT `shadeFg/shadeBg` (RGBA8, owned by the pipeline): if `kind == 0 || mask` it passes the JS cell through with `shadeBg.a = 0`, else shaded cell with `shadeBg.a = 1.0`. Pass 2 reads `shadeFg/shadeBg` + `GI` + depth, writes MRT `rt.fgTex/rt.bgTex` (final, `bg.a = 1.0`): passthrough cells copied, others get the edge rule. No texture is ever read and written in the same pass.
5. **Shade shader = `shadeDetailFast` line for line** (same function names: `hashFast`, `levelFromThresholds`, `orientClassCode`, `bandFactorNum`, `crossLineFast`, `lineGlyphCodeFast`, `pickGlyphCodeFast`, `pickCharCodeFast`, `smoothstepFast`, `coverFast`, `samplePowLUT`). Hash: `uint h = uint(x)*0x27d4eb2du ^ uint(y)*0x165667b1u ^ uint(s)*0x9e3779b1u; h = (h ^ (h>>15)) * 0x85ebca6bu; h = (h ^ (h>>13)) * 0xc2b2ae35u; h ^= h>>16; return float(h >> 8) * (1.0/16777216.0);` - `uint` wrap-around equals `Math.imul`, `uint(int)` equals `x|0`; the `>> 8` makes the uint->float conversion exact (no driver-dependent rounding), deviating from the JS float64 value by < 2^-24 (accepted; see risks). Cell address only from `ivec2(gl_FragCoord.xy)` -> `texelFetch`; no flips (texture row y == grid row y == staging index `y*cols+x`). `samplePowLUT`: `texelFetch(GAIN, ivec2(int(clamp(x,0.,1.)*255.0 + 0.5), 0))`. Byte output: `floor(v + 0.5) / 255.0` in the shader (matches `Math.round` for non-negatives; never rely on the unorm conversion or GLSL `round`). Fog factor is recomputed in pass 2 from depth with the same formula (no float aux target -> no `EXT_color_buffer_float` dependency); `onJoint` is bench-only and is not produced on the GPU. Loops are bounded by constants (`MAX_TONES 4`, `MAX_LEVELS 32`) with an early `break`, mirroring the JS scans. `uTimeSec` is declared and unused (US-028 shading has no time term).
6. **Edge shader = `edgePass` decision block verbatim**, on `GI`/depth of the 4 neighbours (and `i+2`), `farther()` identical incl. `1.18`/`0.35`; gain applies to the pass-1 **byte** values (`floor(min(255, byte*gain) + 0.5)`, min 1), so quantisation happens at the same point as in JS. Rule code goes to a third MRT target `ruleTex` R8UI only when the debug view is on (`?gpudebug=kind|plane|rule`: a `debug.frag` renders the chosen field as colours into `fgTex` in place of pass 2).
7. **`?gpucompare=1`** (`runGpuCompare(pipeline, fb, castFrame, poses, report)` in `engine/render/gpu/gpuCompare.js`; flag parsing, the pose set and the on-screen PASS/FAIL text live in `game/js/main.js`; poses move to `tools/bench-poses.js`, imported by both `bench-cast.mjs` and `main.js`). Per pose: cast once (`castFrame(cam)` = beginFrame + castSectors + computeDerivatives, shared by both paths, so depth parity is by construction 100 % - still reported), JS `shadeSurfaces` + `edgePass` + `fillSky` -> copy `rt.cells.fg/bg` into two preallocated `Uint8Array`s; then `pipeline.frame` + `present()` + `gl.finish()` + `readPixels` of `fgTex`/`bgTex` through a readback FBO (2 x 38 KB, allocated once). `compareCells(jsFg, jsBg, gpuFg, gpuBg, kind, cols, rows, out)` is pure and Node-tested: glyph match over `kind != 0` cells whose 4 neighbours have the same kind (edge cells excluded, counted separately), fg/bg max and mean abs delta and the count outside +-4 over **all** `kind != 0` cells, `mat == 0` count, and per-rule mismatch counts. Print a table per pose + totals; PASS iff glyph >= 99 %, fg/bg outside-tolerance == 0, depth == 100 %.
8. **GPU timer** (`GpuTimer.js`): `EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2`, ring of 4 queries around the hook (upload + both passes + present draw), poll `QUERY_RESULT_AVAILABLE`, drop results after `GPU_DISJOINT_EXT`, keep a 120-entry `Float32Array` ring -> `stats.gpuMsP50/P95`. No extension -> `stats.gpuMs = NaN` and the overlay/bench prints `gpu n/a (frame time N ms)`.
9. **Fallback + switches.** `isSoftwareRenderer(gl)` (`glUtil.js`): `WEBGL_debug_renderer_info` `UNMASKED_RENDERER_WEBGL` if present else `RENDERER`, regex `/SwiftShader|llvmpipe|Basic Render/i`. Shader compile/link errors throw inside the constructor -> caught by the bootstrap -> `console.warn` + CPU path (the game must still run). Context lost -> `ready = false`; restored -> `_initGL()` + `bind()` again. `?gpu=0` -> CPU shading on the gl2 presenter; `?force2d=1` unchanged. F3 overlay: `shade: gpu | cpu`.
10. **Tests.** Node: `ShadeTextures.test.js` (pack the real content pack, unpack every slot, deep-equal against `table.records[i].v2`/`table.sets`; asserts fire on an oversized synthetic set), `gpuCompare.test.js` (edge-cell exclusion, tolerance edges 4 vs 5, PASS/FAIL rule), `glsl.test.js` (shader source strings contain the five hash constants, `1.18`, `0.35`, no `gl_FragCoord` outside the address line, no `round(`), `shadetest`/`bench-cast`/`compare-detail-export`/`check-deps` unchanged and green. Browser (tester, then owner): `?gpucompare=1` numbers into the report; `?gpu=0`, `?force2d=1`, `?gpudebug=...`; `?bench=1` prints JS ms, `uploadMs`, `gpuMsP50`; context loss via `WEBGL_lose_context` from the console (loses -> cpu, restores -> gpu).
11. **Do not**: change `present()`'s shader or draw; read back in the frame loop; use `EXT_color_buffer_float`, UBOs, `round()`, signed shifts, `%` on negatives, `gl_FragCoord` in any hash; add a second copy of the material flattening (only `bindShading` output is packed); add anything level- or tower-specific to GLSL; allocate in `frame()` or the hook.

**Programmer notes (2026-09-23), -> `arch-review`.**

Files added: `engine/render/gpu/{GpuCellPipeline,ShadeTextures,GpuTimer,gpuCompare,glUtil}.js`, `engine/render/gpu/glsl/{common,cell.vert,shade.frag,edge.frag,debug.frag}.js`, `engine/render/gpu/{ShadeTextures,gpuCompare,glsl}.test.js`, `tools/bench-poses.js` (POSES/EYE_H moved out of `bench-cast.mjs` verbatim, item 7). Edited: `engine/render/CellBuffer.js` (`mask`, set in `setCell`/`setCellRGB`/`clear`), `engine/render/RenderTargetGL.js` (`compileShader`/`linkProgram` moved to `gpu/glUtil.js` per the 14.1 file table; added `setCellPass`/hook in `present()`), `engine/render/MaterialTable.js` (`allV2`), `engine/index.js` (exports: `GpuCellPipeline`, `isSoftwareRenderer`, `runGpuCompare`, `compareCells`), `game/js/main.js` (construct + bind the pipeline, one `pipeline.frame()` call in `render()`, `shade: gpu|cpu` overlay line, `?gpucompare=1` mode), `tools/bench-cast.mjs` (imports `POSES`/`EYE_H` from `bench-poses.js` instead of a local copy - behaviour unchanged, verified against the embedded baselines).

**Item 1 deviation (US-025 boundary):** could not add `pipeline.frame(fb, light)` to `compositor.js`'s frame sequence - that file is owned by US-025 (in architect review in parallel). Zero-diff workaround instead: `GpuCellPipeline` sets `rt.gpuActive = true` once ready; `shadeSurfaces` (`detailShade.js`) and `edgePass` (`edgePass.js`) both no-op at their very first line when `rt.gpuActive`/`fb.rt.gpuActive` is true. `compositor.js` keeps calling both exactly as today - they just do nothing while the GPU owns the frame - and `main.js` calls `pipeline.frame(fb, ambientL)` itself right after `renderWorld(...)`, before `rt.present()`. Net effect at runtime is identical to the tech note; please confirm this satisfies the intent or tell me to redo it as a direct `compositor.js` edit once US-025 lands.

**Item 6 deviation:** did not add the `ruleTex` R8UI MRT target - `?gpudebug=kind|plane|rule` is a separate program (`debug.frag.js`) that only needs `GI` + `shadeFg.a` (a "was this cell shaded" indicator) to render the three modes item 6 asks for; the exact per-cell rule code is only ever produced inside `edge.frag.js` itself (not exposed). Flagging in case the architect wants the real rule code visualizable independently.

**Verification run in this sandbox** (`node -v`, `python -m http.server` on a fresh port, Chromium via the Browser pane - never touched port 8000):
- `node tools/check-deps.mjs` -> OK (76 files).
- `node --expose-gc tools/bench-cast.mjs --gc` -> same 2 timing-gate ("escalation trigger 3.5 ms") FAILs as on unmodified `master` in the clean worktree `game_project_test` @ d7723fb (confirmed by diffing the two runs - pre-existing busy-machine flakiness per the US-028 PO decision, not a regression). All correctness/checksum/flicker checks PASS; 0 GC events; heap deltas ~160 B/frame.
- New Node suites: `ShadeTextures.test.js` 764/764, `gpuCompare.test.js` 11/11, `glsl.test.js` 19/19 - all pass.
- Full existing engine suite re-run (`compositor`, `world/*`, `physics/*`, `entities/*`, `check-deps.test.mjs`) - all green, no regressions.
- Browser: `game/index.html` (default `gl2` backend) and `?force2d=1` (falls back to `c2d-capped`) both load with **zero console errors**; overlay/console correctly report `GpuCellPipeline inactive - JS shading` in both cases.
- **Content-pack gate finding (blocks exercising the gate against real gameplay):** `bindShading`'s `allV2` is computed over every non-sky `P.materials` key (existing US-028 behaviour, unchanged by this story) - 4 legacy v1-only materials (`ash`, `iron`, `grate`, `rock`) have no `.v2`/remap in `design/detail-pass.js`, so `allV2` is `false` for the real content pack regardless of level, and the GPU pipeline never activates in normal play or `?gpucompare=1` today. This is a content-completeness gap in `design/`, not an engine bug - flagging per CLAUDE.md rule 3 rather than loosening the `allV2` check myself. **Manager/architect/designer decision needed**: either the designer adds `.v2`/remap entries for those 4 materials, or the gate is explicitly scoped to "materials the bound level actually uses" instead of the whole palette (a spec change to item 1's `allV2` definition).
- **To still exercise the actual pipeline**, I forced `matTable.allV2 = true` in a manual browser console session (real GPU: **ANGLE (Intel, Intel(R) Graphics (0x00007D45) Direct3D11 vs_5_0 ps_5_0, D3D11)** - not a software renderer, so `isSoftwareRenderer` correctly let it through) and ran `runGpuCompare` against `test_room` over the real `tools/bench-poses.js` pose set: **glyph match 100 % on all 5 poses, fg/bg outside-tolerance 0, depth match 100 %, zero `gl.getError()`** - see the session transcript. This is well above the 99 % gate, but it is a forced/synthetic run (patched `allV2`, only 5 frames, this sandbox's software/virtualised GPU), not the required owner-hardware measurement.
- `?bench=1` GPU timing was NOT meaningfully measured here (only a handful of frames were run under the forced-`allV2` smoke test; the first-frame driver warm-up dominated, ~97 ms). Needs a real run.

**Owner-hardware run needed (gate AC "Runs on the owner's real hardware" + the GPU budget AC) - please do, on your own Chrome/laptop, from a repo checkout with this story's changes:**
1. `python -m http.server 8000` from the repo root, open `http://localhost:8000/game/index.html`.
2. Check the console for `[GpuCellPipeline] active (<renderer string>)` or `inactive - ...` and paste that line back, plus any console errors. (Given the content-pack finding above, it will likely say `inactive` - `allV2` is `false` - until that's resolved; if so, please instead run steps 3-4 after locally patching `matTable.allV2 = true` right after the `bindShading(...)` call in `game/js/main.js`, the same forced check I ran here, OR wait for the content fix and re-run unpatched.)
3. `http://localhost:8000/game/index.html?gpucompare=1` - paste back the on-screen PASS/FAIL table (also printed to console) and the `WEBGL_debug_renderer_info` string it logs.
4. `http://localhost:8000/game/index.html?bench=1` after playing for ~10s (so the GPU is warmed up) - or open the F3 overlay in normal play - and paste back `shade: gpu|cpu`, `upload ...ms`, `gpu ...ms` (or `n/a` if the timer extension is unavailable).
5. `http://localhost:8000/game/index.html?force2d=1` - confirm it still loads and plays with zero console errors (the fallback).

**Architect review 1 (2026-09-23, commit 8e84ba0): ARCH CHANGES -> `dev`.** The GLSL port itself is good: `hashFastU` is bit-exact with `hashFast` (uint wrap == `Math.imul`, `uint(int)` == `x|0`; the `>>8` truncation only ever lowers the value by < 2^-24, accepted), texture formats/slots match 14.1, `texelFetch` only, passthrough/mask composition is right, `present()` restores its own state, edge decision block matches `edgePass` line for line, rule order matches `RULE_NAMES`. Deviations accepted: item 1 (`rt.gpuActive` no-op shim in `shadeSurfaces`/`edgePass` - stage-1 only; US-030 moves the choice into the compositor) and item 6 (no `ruleTex`; the `rule` debug mode is a "was shaded" view - rename it `shaded` in the URL flag so nobody reads it as the rule code). Fix these, then back to arch-review:

1. **`?gpucompare=1` is a tautology (blocking).** `shadeSurfaces` writes via `rt.setCellRGB`, which sets `mask[i] = 1` for every world cell; `runGpuCompare` then calls `pipeline.frame` + `present()` with that mask still set, so pass 1 passes the JS cells straight through and the 100 % is the JS result compared with itself. Fix in `gpuCompare.js`: after copying `jsFg/jsBg`, `fb.rt.cells.mask.fill(0)` and **poison** the JS layer of every `kind != 0` cell (e.g. glyph byte 255, fg/bg 0 - values the shader can never produce) before `present()`; sky (`kind == 0`) stays untouched. Any surviving passthrough then shows up as a mismatch. Add `poisonedSurvivors` (count of cells still holding the poison) to the `compareCells` output and to PASS: it must be 0. Re-run the forced-`allV2` smoke test and put the real numbers in the notes.
2. **Context loss (blocking, AC "context failure -> JS shading, fully playable").** Tech note item 9 not implemented: on `webglcontextrestored` `RenderTargetGL._initGL()` creates new `fgTex/bgTex`, but the pipeline keeps `ready = true`, `rt.gpuActive = true` and an `fboFinal` bound to deleted textures, while the CPU passes stay no-oped -> black world. Add `rt.onContextLost/onContextRestored` callbacks (or have the pipeline listen on `rt.canvas`): lost -> `ready = false`, `setEnabled(false)` (which must be allowed when not ready); restored (after `rt._initGL()`) -> `_initGL()` + `bind(table)` again (keep the last table). Verify with `WEBGL_lose_context` from the console: loses -> `shade: cpu`, restores -> `shade: gpu`.
3. **Per-frame allocations (blocking, AC).** `_hook()` allocates every frame: `GpuTimer.stats()` (`Array.from` + `sort` of 120 entries + result object), `Object.assign(this.stats, ...)`, and `_bindCommonTextures` (closure + result object per pass). Fix: write p50/p95 into `stats` in place, recomputed at most every 30 frames (or lazily when the overlay reads them) into a preallocated `Float32Array` scratch with an in-place sort; bind textures with a plain loop over a bind-time array of `[loc, tex]` pairs (locations are stable after link). Confirm with a 300-frame heap-delta check like `bench-cast --gc`.
4. **Minor (do if cheap, not gating):** (a) the ~30 static uniforms in `_passShade`/`_passEdgeOrDebug` belong in `bind()`; only `uLight`/`uTimeSec` per frame (tech note item 3). (b) `edge.frag`: re-quantise before the gain - `floor(sfg.rgb * 255.0 + 0.5) * gain` - per 14.1 section 5 "read bytes back as floor(t*255+0.5)" (removes the 127.49999 half-step cases; today inside tolerance, but the rule exists so US-030 does not inherit it). (c) `bindShading` returns `missingV2: string[]` next to `allV2`, and the bootstrap logs it in the `inactive` line so the content gap is visible without digging.

**Ruling on `allV2` (asked by the programmer): all-or-nothing stays; no per-material fallback in the engine.** Reason: a mixed path would keep the CPU shading loop alive over every cell just to fill a few materials (the exact cost D-009 removes), and doubles the parity/test matrix; US-030 deletes CPU shading from the frame entirely. v2 data for every non-sky material is a **content-pack invariant** (designer adds `ash`, `iron`, `grate`, `rock` now; a designer oracle should assert it). Scoping `allV2` to "materials the bound level uses" is rejected too: `bindShading` runs before `bindLevel`, and a level swap would silently flip the render path.

**Readiness notes for US-030 (no action this story):** (i) `GA`/`GD`/`DEPTH` become GLSL render targets in stage 2 - RGBA32F/R32F are not colour-renderable in core WebGL2; US-030 either requires `EXT_color_buffer_float` (else CPU fallback) or packs floats into RGBA32UI via `floatBitsToUint`. Architect decides in the US-030 tech notes. (ii) Stage 1 upload at 320x120 would be 1.7 MB/frame - fine, because stage 2 removes it; do not ship 320x120 on stage 1. (iii) The `rt.gpuActive` shim and `mask` semantics move into `renderWorld` when US-025 is `done`.

**Programmer rework (2026-09-23), -> `arch-review`.** All four review-1 items fixed.

1. **Tautology fixed.** `runGpuCompare` (`engine/render/gpu/gpuCompare.js`) now calls `poisonNonSky(fb.rt.cells, fb.gbuf.kind, n)` right before `pipeline.frame`/`present()`: clears `cells.mask` and overwrites every `kind != 0` cell's fg/bg with glyph byte 255 + rgb 0 (sky untouched). `compareCells` gained `poisonedSurvivors` (count of cells still showing that signature) and PASS now requires it to be 0. Added a Node unit test (`gpuCompare.test.js` test 6) proving a surviving poison cell is caught; had to change the test file's own default glyph byte from 255 to 50 first, since 255 was already its "don't care" baseline for unrelated cells and would have false-positived once the check existed.
2. **Context loss handled.** `GpuCellPipeline` now listens on `rt.canvas` itself for `webglcontextlost`/`restored` (registered after `RenderTargetGL`'s own listener, so it always runs after `rt._initGL()` on restore, by DOM listener-order). Lost -> `ready = false` + `setEnabled(false)` (relaxed to allow force-disabling even when `!ready`); restored -> rebuilds everything (`_initGL()`, which now re-`bind()`s the last table) + `setEnabled(true)`. `dispose()` also removes the listeners.
3. **Per-frame allocations removed.** `GpuTimer.stats()` (`Array.from`+`sort`+object literal every call) replaced with `writeStats(out)`: writes straight into the caller's preallocated `stats` object, and only re-sorts (into a preallocated `Float32Array` scratch, in place, no comparator closure) every 30 calls. `_hook()` no longer does `Object.assign`. Texture binding no longer builds a closure+object per pass: `_buildBindTable`/`_setSamplerUniforms` run once (init/rebind time) into flat `[loc, tex, unit]` arrays; the per-frame `_bindTextures` is a plain loop doing only `activeTexture`+`bindTexture`.
4. **Minor items done.** (a) shading/fog/ao/faceK/edge-rule/grid uniforms moved into `bind()`/`_bindStaticUniforms()`, set once per bind, not per frame - only `uLight`/`uTimeSec` remain per-frame. (b) `edge.frag.js` now re-quantises to the pass-1 byte first (`floor(sfg.rgb*255+0.5)`), then applies gain and re-quantises again. (c) `bindShading` (`MaterialTable.js`) returns `missingV2: string[]`; `main.js`'s `inactive` log line includes it.
   Also (per review 1's item-6 deviation note): renamed the `?gpudebug=rule` mode to `?gpudebug=shaded` (and `DEBUG_MODE_RULE` -> `DEBUG_MODE_SHADED`) so it isn't misread as the real edge-rule code.

Full existing suite (`compositor`, `world/*`, `physics/*`, `entities/*`, `check-deps.test.mjs`) green. `bench-cast --gc`: ALL CHECKS PASS (correctness/flicker/GC all green; the JS timing-gate line flickered OVER BUDGET on one run under machine load and PASSED on the next two re-runs with nothing else changed - same pre-existing busy-machine noise flagged in the original programmer notes, not a regression, and untouched by this story's files).

Reproduced identically across two separate fresh-port loads, so it's deterministic, not driver jitter.

Reverted the one line, confirmed via `grep` the source is back to `rgbF = clamp(rgbF, 0.0, 255.0)`, reloaded on port 8797 (also never opened before) - back to the same 4 PASS / 1 FAIL (start pose) as before the injection. The parity check is real and catches a real regression; it is not passing by construction.
  - Have **not** root-caused the "start pose" 5-cell gap - out of scope for this rework pass (review 1 was about the harness being trustworthy, not about chasing every gap it might reveal); flagging for the architect to triage, possibly a small US-028a-era edge case that the old tautology was masking on this one pose.
- **Context loss/restore**: from the console, `gl.getExtension('WEBGL_lose_context').loseContext()` -> `rt.gpuActive` flips to `false` immediately, console logs `[GpuCellPipeline] WebGL context lost, falling back to CPU shading`, no crash (canvas goes black while the context is genuinely lost, which is a browser-level WebGL constraint independent of this fix - nothing can draw to a truly lost context). `.restoreContext()` -> console logs `[GpuCellPipeline] WebGL context restored, GPU shading resumed`, `rt.gpuActive` back to `true`, screenshot confirms the world renders again (GPU shading, not black).
- `?force2d=1`: loads and plays, zero console errors, `[RenderTarget] back-end: c2d-capped`, `[GpuCellPipeline] inactive - JS shading`.
- `?bench=1` / F3 overlay after ~7.5s of play: `shade: gpu  upload 0.40ms  gpu 0.22ms`, frame 2.32ms - well inside the 4ms GPU budget; `gpuMsP50` populated (not `n/a`), confirming `GpuTimer.writeStats` works after the allocation rewrite.

Confirmed via multi-point pixel sampling + `gl.getError()===0` (not a driver/permission issue, a real shader-output bug). Spawned a follow-up task (`task_7379f8bf`) rather than touching it under this ticket, since it's unrelated to review 1's four items.

**Architect review 2 (2026-09-23, commit 3f37791): ARCH CHANGES -> `dev` (one item).** Review-1 items 1-4 verified: poisoning + `poisonedSurvivors`, context loss/restore, zero-alloc `_hook`/`writeStats`/bind table, minors. The debug-mode black output goes to the follow-up task, not here.

5. **Start-pose FAIL is a real parity bug, not float tolerance. No AC change.** Probe (instrumented `gpuCompare`, headless Chrome, same ANGLE Intel 0x7D45 D3D11, 1600x900) shows the failing cells are brick wall cells (mat 1, face 3) at the wall top, `v = 2.8f` (2.79999995). JS `Math.floor(v / g.v)` in f64 = floor(6.99999988) = **6**; GLSL f32 `floor(v / gv)` rounds to 7.0 = **7**. So the cell lands in a different course -> a different block hash -> a different tint/tone (fg up to 15, bg 5). The glyph is the same. Proof: with `Math.fround(v / g.v)` in JS, all 5 poses PASS (fgMax 1). The failure only shows when the window is large enough for these cells to exist; at 800x600 all poses pass. That is why it looked pose-specific. **Fix (both paths, same rule; do not use `fround`, because GPU division is not IEEE-exact):** snap-then-floor for every world-anchored grid quotient: `QEPS = 1/256` (exact in f32). JS `q = v / g.v + QEPS; course = floor(q); fv = q - course`, and GLSL uses the identical code. The same applies to `bix = floor(uo / g.u + QEPS)`, to band `pos` (`floor(bcoord / period + QEPS)`) and to `crossLine` `k`. Put it in one helper, `qfloor(x)`, in `detailShade.js` and in `glsl/common.js`, and document the rule in architecture.md 14.1 section 5 (floor of an authored-boundary quotient). Leave the power-of-two `floor(u*ds)` texel floors alone (exact in both). If `?shadetest=1` then diverges from the `design/detail-pass.js` reference at the boundary samples, the designer adds the same `QEPS` there (the reference must stay the oracle). Re-run `?gpucompare=1` in a window of at least 1600x900, plus shadetest, `ShadeTextures`/`glsl` tests and check-deps. Then send it straight to `po-review` (the architect only needs to see the numbers). This counts as the D-009 single rework, not a new failure.

### US-030a GPU raycasting: GLSL DDA + configurable grid  [Priority: P0] [Status: dev]

As a player, I want walls and floors to stay crisp on a bigger grid, so that the world looks solid instead of "lines jumping".
(D-009 stage 2a. Only runs if US-029 passed the gate.)
Acceptance criteria:
- [x] **Architect tech notes written before dev** (engine story): sector grid / structures / spans as integer data textures, the DDA loop and its step cap, the depth texture. Done — see below and `docs/architecture.md` 14.2.
- [x] Sector DDA in GLSL: one fragment per cell casts against the sector grid, including multiple placed structures with per-structure origins (US-025/D-008) and open spans; writes the G-buffer (kind, planeId, material, uv, depth) that the US-029 shading/edge pass consumes. The CPU caster no longer runs on the `gl2` path.
- [x] Parity: with N = 1 the GPU cast matches the JS caster under `?gpucompare=1` (architect ruling: <= 0.5 % cells outside fg/bg +-4, fgMax/bgMax <= 64, 0 depth/uv violations, **holes = 0**). **6/7 poses PASS, holes 0 on all 7.** The 7th (world_m1 spawn) is a CPU oracle defect, **BUG-CPU-001**, and is a known FAIL that does not block this story (architect review 3).
- [x] **Configurable grid**: `createEngine({cols, rows, cpuGrid, gpu, rays})` and URL `?grid=WxH`, allowed range **160x60 to 320x120**, cell aspect preserved, out-of-range values clamped and logged once. **Default on the `gl2` GPU path is 240x90** (D-009 amendment 2; 320x120 selectable via `?grid=`). `engine.setGrid(cols, rows)` added.
- [x] **Fallback**: no WebGL2 / software renderer / `?gpu=0` -> JS path, **grid forced to 160x60**, fully playable; `?grid=` is ignored on the fallback (logged once). `?gpu=0` keeps the gl2 presenter (fast draw) and only drops the cell pipeline + grid, matching 14.1's fallback matrix (fixed a bug where it wrongly forced the whole back-end to Canvas2D).
- [x] The JS caster stays the oracle: `bench-cast` still passes headless in Node (checksums unchanged vs the embedded baseline). `check-deps` OK (89 files); public API unchanged (additive only).
- [x] `footprintEntry` fix (US-025 carry-over): writes `ctx._entryX/_entryY`, returns a bool. Nothing else changes in the CPU caster; `bench-cast` baselines confirmed unchanged.
- [x] **Owner walk-test at the 240x90 default with n = 1** as a *baseline* only. Renders and holds 60 fps (gpu ~0.2-1.2 ms on the owner's Intel ANGLE/D3D11 laptop GPU). The world_m1 see-through bug (BUG-OWN-001) was a GPU DDA bug and is fixed (owner pose shows walls on GPU, matches `?force2d=1`); tester re-confirms, owner walk-through requested at testing.
Design needed: no (designer re-checks existing previews at 320x120 as a follow-up, tracked under US-030b).
Notes / dependencies: US-029 gate PASSED, US-025 `done`. US-006 depends on this story `done` (and on US-030b for the flicker bar).

**Programmer notes (2026-09-23), arch-review:**

**Scope delivered:** `engine/render/gpu/WorldTextures.js` (+ test) packs placed structures' `packed.geom/mats/flags/relief` into one stacked atlas per array (7.2 amendment: `FLAGS` is now RG8UI, `g` = `floorRise|ceilDrop<<4`; `packed.relief` added to `engine/world/packed.js`, computed independently of the CPU caster's own `ensureRelief` cache — CPU path untouched). `engine/render/gpu/glsl/dda.frag.js` is the GLSL DDA (14.2 item 1 pseudocode, ported close to line-for-line from `castColumn`/`footprintEntry`/`primeWallGSample`/`wallAoD`/`planeAoDFast`), writing `GI`/`GA`/`DEPTH` directly (n=1 collapses cast+resolve into one pass, `floatBitsToUint` throughout, no float-color-attachment extension needed). `engine/render/gpu/glsl/deriv.frag.js` ports `computeDerivatives`. `GpuCellPipeline.js` gained the cast/deriv passes, world-atlas upload/dirty-row management (`world.structVersion`, a new, narrower counter than `renderVersion` — see `World.js`), camera-basis computation, and `setSource('dda'|'upload')` (the old 14.1 upload path is kept, test-only, for `?gpucompare=shade`). `engine/render/compositor.js`'s `renderWorld` gained a one-line early-return (`if (fb.gpuDda) return`) — the only touch to US-025 territory, required by the "CPU caster no longer runs" AC. `createEngine`/`RenderTarget.js` do the pre-construction WebGL2 + software-renderer probe and grid clamp (`engine/core/engine.js`: `clampGrid`, `GRID_DEFAULT_COLS`, `setGrid`). `main.js` parses `?grid=`/`?rays=`, wires `fb.gpuDda`, and splits `?gpucompare=1` (new: full DDA-vs-CPU `renderWorld` parity, forced 160x60/n=1, `compareGeometry` new pure function in `gpuCompare.js` + `WorldTextures.test.js`/`gpuCompare.test.js` additions) from `?gpucompare=shade` (old US-029 shading-only test, unchanged, `setSource('upload')`).

**Bonus fix (PO-flagged, moved in from US-029):** `?gpudebug=kind|plane|shaded` rendered solid black — `debug.frag.js` wrote `outFg.a = 0` (glyph 0 = space = zero atlas coverage) regardless of mode. Fixed: non-`kind==0` cells now write a solid-coverage glyph (`'@'`) so the colour is visible; `kind==0` stays glyph-less (correct, not the bug).

**Deviations (flagged for architect review, not silent):**
1. **`rays` ships as 1 only.** 14.2's eventual default is 2 (N-ray coverage), but N-ray resolve is US-030b's job; `rays` is plumbed through `createEngine`/`?rays=`/uniforms but the DDA only implements the n=1 exact-centre sample this story.
2. **GPU sky is a flat gradient, no clouds** (`GpuCellPipeline._bakeSkyLUT`, a 32-sample baked LUT) — a deliberate simplification of `fastShadeSky`'s cloud-noise-texture lookup, since `compareCells` already excludes every `kind==0` cell from the parity bar. `fillSky` (JS) no longer runs on the GPU path.
3. **The CPU's "camera outside a structure's footprint" `VOID_SECTOR` quirk is not reproduced** in the GLSL DDA (documented in `dda.frag.js`'s header) — the shader starts at the real entry cell's own sector, simpler and arguably more correct; not exercised by any in-scope pose (camera always spawns inside a placed structure).
4. **Structure order for the DDA's `t0 >= bestT` early-out is placement order**, not a per-frame camera-distance sort — correctness (nearest-candidate-wins) doesn't depend on order, only the early-out's effectiveness does.
5. **Terrain is not cast by the GPU path** (US-016, unbuilt) — a ray that leaves every placed structure's footprint reports `kind=0` (GPU sky), whereas the CPU path would separately hand it to `castTerrain`. Not a regression (terrain casting doesn't exist on either path in a meaningful way yet outside `world_m1`'s far-hill silhouette), but worth architect confirmation that this is the intended M1 boundary.

**Owner-run verification done this session** (fresh no-cache static server, not 8000/8801/8802; full list run, all green except the one item below):
`check-deps` OK (89 files) · all Node suites green (`playerLook`, `eyeFeel`, `handle`, `jump`, `physics`, `compositor`, `serialize`, `terrain`, `packed`, `world`, `ShadeTextures`, `gpuCompare`, `glsl`, `WorldTextures`) · `tools/bench-cast.mjs --gc` checksums unchanged vs the embedded baseline (the "OVER BUDGET" v1/v2 timing line is the pre-existing US-028 finding, unrelated) · `?shadetest=1` v1 361/361 + v2 2242/2242 ALL PASS · `?gpucompare=1` (new DDA page) 4/5 PASS at 160x60/n=1, see the AC line above · `?debug=1` at the 320x120 default: GPU active, ~0.2-1.2 ms/frame on `test_room` · `?grid=160x60` and `?grid=240x90`: both render, overlay shows the requested grid · `?force2d=1`: falls back to Canvas2D at 160x60 as expected · `?gpu=0`: now correctly stays on the gl2 **presenter** with JS shading at 160x60 (was wrongly forcing Canvas2D before a fix this session) · no console errors on any of the above.

**Found and NOT fixed here (owner/coordinator-reported, investigated in depth, root-caused, follow-up task spawned - `task_840bc1e6`):** at `world_m1`'s default pose, the GPU path shows mostly glyph-less (solid-colour) cells where the CPU shows rich texture. Traced to a **pre-existing `shade.frag` defect** (US-029, not touched by this story's own changes): fed the *exact same* per-cell inputs (u, v, z, aoD, dudx/dvdx/dudy/dvdy, kind, mat, face - verified byte-identical between the DDA's readback and the CPU's `gbuf` for the same cell) the JS reference `shadeDetailFast` renders a visible glyph while the GLSL port renders blank, for the `"stone"` grid material at height values around 3.6-4m (rare in `test_room`'s low ceiling, common in the tower). This likely also explains the one `?gpucompare=1` near-miss above. Root-caused but not line-pinned (needs a GLSL step-debugger this session didn't have); flagged, not blind-fixed, to avoid risking the passing shade tests. **Geometry itself is not affected** - kind/mat/face histograms at the same pose are 99.6% identical between the two paths (the CPU/GPU images "looking different" is downstream of the glyph bug, not a DDA/geometry mismatch).

**Bug fix (Programmer, 2026-09-23) - "GPU path shows only colour blocks, no glyphs" (and the paragraph above is superseded):** root cause is NOT `shade.frag`. `ambientL` (`engine/render/sectorCaster.js`, the per-frame ambient light `main.js` hands the GPU shade pass as `uLight`) was only ever primed inside `castScene`, i.e. by the CPU caster - which `renderWorld`'s new `fb.gpuDda` early-out skips. So on the GPU path it stayed `[0,0,0]`: brightness 0 -> glyph 0 (space) for every shaded cell, fg = tone x `fgMin` (hence the coloured blocks). `?gpucompare=1` passed only because its CPU oracle ran first and primed the light; the "JS 27 vs GLSL 0 for identical inputs" observation had the same cause (the GLSL side was fed `uLight = 0`). Fix: `primeAmbientLight` exported and called on the DDA early-out in `engine/render/compositor.js` (same call, same source of truth, once per frame). `?gpucompare=1` hardened so this class can't pass again: (a) it now runs the **GPU frame first**, from a fully poisoned mask-free JS layer (`poisonAllCells`), and the CPU oracle second - the GPU result can't borrow CPU side effects; (b) the readback is `RenderTargetGL.readbackPresent()` - **exactly the textures bound on present()'s units 0/1 after its draw**, not `rt.fgTex/bgTex` by name (`GpuCellPipeline.readback()` delegates to it; `sampledOwnTextures` is reported and fails the page if false); (c) the **world_m1 player-spawn pose** (tower start, yaw 330, pitch 30) is a sixth compare row alongside the five test_room poses. Files: `engine/render/{sectorCaster,compositor,RenderTargetGL}.js`, `engine/render/gpu/{GpuCellPipeline,gpuCompare}.js`, `engine/index.js`, `game/js/main.js`. The stair-pose 12-cell near-miss above is a separate, real parity gap (still reported by the page), not this bug. Follow-up chip `task_840bc1e6` ("shade.frag defect") is moot.

**How to re-run (owner):** `python -m http.server 8000` (or any static server) from the repo root, then `game/index.html?debug=1` (default 320x120), `?grid=160x60`, `?grid=240x90`, `?force2d=1`, `?gpu=0`, `?gpucompare=1` (new DDA parity page), `?gpucompare=shade` (old US-029 page, unchanged), `?gpudebug=kind|plane|shaded` (now visible). Context loss: `WEBGL_lose_context` from the console, same drill as US-029 (lost -> CPU, restored -> GPU + world atlas rebuilt). Browsers cache ES modules aggressively across reloads on a plain static server - hard-reload (Ctrl+Shift+R) or use a private window if a change doesn't seem to take.

**Architect review 1 (2026-09-23, commit 49050b3, US-030a files only): ARCH CHANGES.**
Method: read of the diff plus a headless probe (a float64 JS port of `dda.frag.js` run cell-for-cell against the CPU `castSectors` G-buffer at 320x120 and 160x60 on world_m1 + test_room). Good: WorldTextures packing/dirty-row plan, all-uint G-buffer, camera basis from JS, `footprintEntry` fix, `?gpu=0` presenter fix, hardened gpucompare (GPU first, `readbackPresent`), `check-deps` OK (88 files), Node suites green. Deviations 1-5 accepted (rays=1 -> US-030b; flat GPU sky -> designer/PO visual follow-up under US-030b; VOID_SECTOR quirk, placement-order early-out, no terrain = M1 boundary until US-016).
Required changes:
1. **BUG-OWN-001 is a GPU DDA bug - fix.** `dda.frag.js`: the sky rule `C.ceilSky && !N.solid && !N.ceilSky && hb > N.ceilH -> break` terminates the ray. `castColumn` never terminates a column on sky (it only sets `skyPending` and keeps walking; sky is painted at column end into rows nothing else claimed). Probe at the owner pose (eye 4.6 = feet 3.0 + 1.6, yaw 236, pitch -29, sector 'L' looking over the closed grate 'G', ceilH 3.0 < eye): CPU = wall/step/floor everywhere, DDA port = 10,751 sky cells (kind parity 73.5 %). With the `break` removed (the ray simply continues with `C = N`): 100.000 % kind parity at that pose, and the 5 test_room poses, the spawn pose and two more tower poses stay at 99.99-100 %. Do exactly that: drop the fourth branch; no other rule change. Add the owner pose as a 7th `?gpucompare=1` row (world_m1, cam (1500.69, 1027.36, 4.6), yaw 236, pitch -29; bar >= 99.5 % kind, 0 depth/uv violations).
2. **Fallback matrix gap (14.2 items 5/7).** `main.js`: when `rt.backend === 'gl2'` but the pipeline is not created (`!candidate.ready`, `!detailPass`, `!matTable.allV2`), the CPU caster runs at 320x120. Call `engine.setGrid(cpuGrid.cols, cpuGrid.rows)` in that case and rebuild `gbuf` from the `grid:changed` event (the F3 overlay must show `path: cpu grid: 160x60`). Also `fb.gpuDda = !!gpuPipeline && rt.gpuActive` (not `!!gpuPipeline`): after a failed context restore `gpuActive` is false but the compositor would still skip the CPU cast -> black world.
3. **Per-frame allocations in the hook (14.2 item 9).** `planFrameUpdate` returns a fresh `{rebuildNeeded, dirtyRanges: []}` every frame and `_computeCamBasis` builds a new object every frame. Give `planFrameUpdate` an `out` parameter with a preallocated ranges array (`count` + `Int32Array(2*MAX_STRUCTS)`) and make `_camBasis` a once-allocated object written in place. Test-only paths (`readbackPresent`, `buildWorldTextures`) may allocate.
4. **`floatBitsToUint(1.0 / 0.0)`** in `dda.frag.js`: a constant division by zero is an unspecified value in GLSL ES 3.00 (it happens to fold to Inf on ANGLE). Write the sentinel literally: `outDepth = 0x7f800000u;`.
5. **Tolerance rulings for the parity page** (no code beyond the threshold): the world_m1 spawn colour gap (138 fg / 111 bg cells > +-4 at kind 99.92 %) and the stair 12-cell near-miss are shading-level (kind/depth/uv parity is 100 % in the probe for both) - float32 `v`/`z`/`aoD` on 6-8.5 m walls flipping a detail band. Ruling: `compareCells` gets an optional `maxOutsideFrac` (default 0 = the US-029 shade page, unchanged); the `?gpucompare=1` page passes with `fgOutside + bgOutside <= 0.5 %` of non-sky cells per pose **and** `fgMax/bgMax <= 64` (a band flip, never a wrong colour). Re-check after item 1: if the spawn row is still > 0.5 % it is a real bug and comes back here.
Notes, no change required: eye exactly at `floorH` (e.g. z = 3.0 literal on the ledge) makes the DDA hit its own floor at t = 0 where the CPU draws nothing - unreachable in play (eye >= floor + 0.3). `engine.setGrid` recreates the RenderTarget on the same canvas without disposing the old one (fine for the one startup use; the in-game option story must add `dispose()`). Structure order for the early-out: acceptable, revisit only if the `cast` pass exceeds 1 ms at 320x120 with 2+ structures.
**Grid recommendation for the PO/owner (item 4 of the review brief):** 320x120 is only legible when a cell is >= ~8x12 CSS px, i.e. a >= 2560 px wide canvas. Recommend **auto-grid-by-window at startup**: `cols = clamp(floor(canvasCssWidth * devicePixelRatio / 8), 160, 320)` rounded down to a multiple of 8 (keeps `rows = cols*3/8` integral), `?grid=` still overrides; 1280 px -> 160, 1600 px -> 200, 1920 px -> 240, 2560+ -> 320. No engine change needed (`createEngine({cols})` + `clampGrid` already do it; `main.js` computes `cols` from the canvas size once; no live resize in M1). A fixed 240x90 default is the acceptable simpler alternative; keeping 320x120 as the default is not recommended for normal laptops. The US-030b coverage pass, not grid density, is the anti-shimmer lever, so a smaller grid costs no stability.

**Programmer, ARCH CHANGES fix (2026-09-23) -> `arch-review`.** All 5 items done, exactly as specified, no other rule change:
1. **BUG-OWN-001 fixed**: dropped the fourth branch (the sky `break`) in `dda.frag.js`; the ray now just continues with `C = N`. Added the owner pose as a 7th `?gpucompare=1` row (world_m1, cam (1500.69, 1027.36, eye 4.60 = feet 3.00 + eyeHeight 1.60), yaw 236, pitch -29) - **PASS, kind/glyph 100%**. Verified live in-browser too: teleported the player to the exact pose on a fresh port (8964, stopped after) - GPU path now renders the wall/step/floor identically to `?force2d=1` (CPU); previously the GPU showed sky through it.
2. **Fallback gap**: `main.js` now forces `engine.setGrid(cpuGrid.cols, cpuGrid.rows)` when `rt.backend === 'gl2'` but no `gpuPipeline` was created, and rebuilds `gbuf` (`rt`/`depthBuffer`/`openSpans` re-read off `engine` - all four were promoted from `const` to `let`). `fb.gpuDda = !!gpuPipeline && rt.gpuActive`.
3. **Per-frame allocations removed**: `planFrameUpdate(world, atlas, out)` now writes into a caller-owned `{rebuildNeeded, count, ranges: Int32Array(2*MAX_STRUCTS)}` (`makeFrameUpdatePlan()`, one per `GpuCellPipeline`); `_computeCamBasis` writes into a once-allocated `_camBasis` in place. `WorldTextures.test.js` updated to the new `out`/`count`/`ranges` shape (allocates one `out` per call - test-only, fine per item 3).
4. **Infinity sentinel**: `outDepth = 0x7f800000u;` literal, no `floatBitsToUint(1.0/0.0)`.
5. **Tolerance ruling wired**: `compareCells` takes an optional `maxOutsideFrac` (default 0, unchanged for `?gpucompare=shade`); `?gpucompare=1` passes `0.005` and also requires `fgMax/bgMax <= 64`. **Re-check result (the point of this item): the spawn/stair rows are still failing, and worse than documented.** At 160x60 post-fix: `world_m1 player spawn` outside 1.91% (was ~2.6% - kind parity 99.93%, unchanged), `test_room facing stair + 1.0m platform` outside 2.08% (was a 12-cell/0.047% near-miss), and a previously-clean `test_room start pose` now sits at 0.501% (just over the bar). Kind/depth/uv parity is still 100% (start pose) or 99.93%/100% (spawn/stair) in all three - this is the same pre-existing shading-level float32 boundary issue the architect already root-caused (item 5's note), just touched by more cells now that rays that used to stop at the sky `break` keep going into real geometry. **Per the architect's own ruling, this comes back to architect review, not silently patched further** - flagging, not fixing blind.

**Architect review 2 (2026-09-23, commit 38e92bc): ARCH CHANGES.** Items 1-4 and the 240x90 default are OK: the sky `break` is gone, the 7th row passes, the fallback forces `setGrid` and `gpuDda` checks `gpuActive`, the plan and cam basis are preallocated, and the Inf literal is in. Item 5: I probed with headless Chrome (ANGLE D3D11, port 8977, raw buffers intercepted). The gap is **not** in `shade.frag`, the light priming or the sprites (no-sprite run is identical). At every non-hole outlier cell the GPU inputs (u/v/z/aoD) match the CPU. Real outlier cells are 27/33/43 (0.30/0.34/0.49 %), but max delta is 176-225, so these are wrong colours, not band flips. Causes:
1. **Metric bug.** `outsideFrac` counts channels (up to 6 per cell), not cells. That gives the programmer's 1.9-2.1 %. Count a cell once if any fg/bg channel is > 4, and report `cellsOutside`.
2. **DDA holes at exact boundaries (main cause, test_room).** At test_room start (67,10) the CPU has a ceiling with v = 2.000 and the GPU has kind 0. At (27,2) the CPU has a wall top with v = 3.000 = ceilH and the GPU has kind 0. The ray reaches a wall whose hit height lies a float step outside [floorH, ceilH], so no rule claims the cell. The hole then makes row 9's ceiling derivatives one-sided (dudy 0.1805 vs 0.1896), and the mat-13 joint glyphs flip. That is the 16/27 non-edge ceiling cells there and 27/33 on the stair pose. Fix in `dda.frag.js`: when the ray enters a solid or higher neighbour and no wall/step/upper claims the hit, close the gap. If hb >= C.ceilH and !C.ceilSky, write C's ceiling sample at t1; if hb <= C.floorH, write C's floor sample at t1 (same u/v/planeId formulas as the span hits). Add a "holes" count to `compareGeometry`: GPU kind 0 where the CPU kind is not 0, counted even on edge cells. It must be 0 on all 7 poses.
3. **Wall face tag differs** at test_room start (27,3-4): CPU planeId 50331649, GPU 50331650, same u/v. When sideDistX == sideDistY, the GPU side choice must use the same comparison as `castColumn` (strict vs non-strict).
4. **world_m1 spawn cluster** (x 55-67, y 12-14, 34 cells). The CPU sees a mat-8 TOP at z 0 (pid 92281688). The GPU hits a wall plane (pid 50331654) at z 0.22-0.39. So the DDA draws a low lip that the CPU does not. Likely the relief `FLAGS.g` (floorRise) is being used as geometry, or a step-rule mismatch. Dump the ray at (56,12) against `castColumn` and make the DDA match the CPU.
5. **Bar after 1-4 (unchanged):** <= 0.5 % cells outside and fgMax/bgMax <= 64 per pose, plus holes = 0. All 7 rows must PASS before `po-review`.

2. **Holes counter added, gap-closure fix NOT applied.** `compareGeometry` now counts `holes` (GPU kind 0 where CPU kind != 0, counted even on edge cells) and requires it 0 to pass. I tried the specified gap-closure branch anyway (write C's own floor/ceiling sample at t1 when no wall/step/upper claims a boundary, restricted to "entering solid/higher neighbour" per your wording so it wouldn't fire on ordinary open-to-open boundaries) - it fixed 0 real holes and regressed kind parity on 2 test_room poses from 100% to 44-55% (sky-adjacent boundaries where BUG-OWN-001's fix now lets the ray walk on). Reverted; not in the diff.
3. **Tie-break/coord bug fixed - this was the real cause of the planeId diff, not a strict-vs-non-strict comparison.** `dda.frag.js`'s wall `coord` used `cMapX`/`cMapY` (the NEAR cell, pre-step) where `castColumn` uses `ray.mapX`/`ray.mapY` (the FAR cell, already advanced by `ddaStep` before `primeWallGSample` reads it) - an off-by-one at the coord field, exactly matching the reported 50331649 vs 50331650. Changed to use `mapX`/`mapY` (already the post-step value in this shader). Fixed test_room start from 99.93%/95.99%-ish shading down to **100.00%/100.00%** and also fixed the owner-repro row (94% -> 100%).
4. **Lip wall: investigated, not fixed - reverted after regression.** Tried bounding the WALL/STEP claims below by the near cell's own floor height (`hb > C.floorH`, mirroring `castColumn`'s row-range clip `[nearSector.floorH, farSector.floorH]`) - this fixed the world_m1 spawn lip but broke legitimate walls elsewhere (the "facing stair" pose went from 100% to `depthViol`/`uvViol` 5, i.e. it excluded real wall rows whose visible base sits at/below the near floor). Reverted; not in the diff. A Node repro at the exact screen cell (56,12)/world_m1 spawn pose (`beginFrame`+`castSectors` only, no browser) gave `kind=1` (WALL) uniformly for x53-68,y10-15, not the reported `TOP z=0` cluster - my repro's camera/grid setup likely doesn't match the live pipeline's exactly (couldn't get an independent numeric trace matching your headless-Chrome probe), so I could not pin the exact rule difference with confidence. Per your own item-5 wording ("comes back to architect review, not silently patched further"), flagging rather than guessing again.

**Architect review 3 (2026-09-23, commit 2b448bb, diff only): ARCH OK -> `po-review`.** Item 1 (cell metric) and item 3 (the wall `coord` uses the far cell, as in `castColumn`) are correct.
1. **Item 2 is dropped.** Holes = 0 on all 7 poses without it. The `holes` counter in `compareGeometry` now guards against them. Leave the reverted gap-closure branch out.
2. **World_m1 spawn row: the CPU is wrong, the DDA is right.** The CPU planeId 92281688 decodes to TOP at floorH 7.0. The GPU planeId 50331654 is a wall face at z 0.22-0.39 above the near cell's floor. So the ray crosses a 7.0 m broken-wall top (`$`/`P`) into a higher solid (`&` 7.5 / `%` 8.0, tower.js). In `castColumn`'s solid branch (sectorCaster.js ~l.618-636), the inner `ddaStep` consumes the solid cell's exit boundary. It then sets `nearSector` to the next cell without testing solid-to-next for a wall or step. The riser between two stacked solid tops is never drawn, and a lower plane fills its rows. The DDA tests every boundary, which is correct geometry. The programmer's `hb > C.floorH` bound copied the CPU defect and broke real walls, so do not apply it.
   **Filed: BUG-CPU-001 (engine, CPU caster, own story, not US-030a).** After the inner `ddaStep` in the solid branch, run the same transition checks for `solidSector -> nextSector` at `exitDist`: next solid with a higher `floorH` gives a WALL face, rows `[rowAtHeight(next.floorH), rowAtHeight(solid.floorH)]`; next non-solid with a higher `floorH` gives a STEP. Clip both with `openTop/openBottom/floorFilledTo`, like the existing branches. Re-baseline `bench-cast` checksums (an intended change). Acceptance: the spawn row of `?gpucompare=1` PASSES, and 7/7.
3. Until BUG-CPU-001 lands, the tester records the spawn row as a **known FAIL (BUG-CPU-001)**. It does not block US-030a. The other 6 rows must PASS.

**BUG-CPU-001 status: fixed, pending test (2026-09-23, programmer).** Added the missing `solidSector -> nearSector` transition check in `castColumn`'s solid branch (sectorCaster.js, after the inner `ddaStep`, ~l.636-666): WALL face when `nearSector.solid`, STEP when not, rows `[rowAtHeight(nearSector.floorH), rowAtHeight(solidSector.floorH)]` at `exitDist`, clipped by `openTop/openBottom` only (no `floorFilledTo` clamp - mirrors the non-solid branch's `higher === farSector` case, since the far side is always the higher one here). `?gpucompare=1` on a fresh port (8918): world_m1 spawn now **PASS** (was the only intended target). **ARCH OK (architect re-review, 2026-09-23, 980a405 `dda.frag.js` only) -> `po-review` (PO OK already given; tester re-tests directly).** The descending branch mirrors `castColumn`'s `floorH !== floorH` riser: near-higher case, `higher.wallMat` = `C.wallMat`, band bounded `(N.floorH, C.floorH)`, non-solid only. The `floorFilledTo` clip maps to `t1 < bestT`. `<=` on WALL only changes the bit-exact row, so it can't overfire.

**Tooling fix, `?gpucompare=1`/`?gpucompare=shade` window-independence (2026-09-23, not a story-status change).** The tester's stair/start hole FAILs (see the re-test row above and `docs/test-reports/US-030a.md`) were suspected to be window-size-sensitive: `rt.pxCellW`/`rt.pxCellH` (real measured glyph-metrics device pixels, `glyphMetrics.js`) are derived from `window.innerWidth`/`innerHeight` in `RenderTargetGL.resize()`/`RenderTargetCanvas2D.resize()`, and `screenAspect = (cols*pxCellW)/(rows*pxCellH)` (read from `rt` in `sectorCaster.js`, `GpuCellPipeline.js`, `sprites.js`, and `MaterialTable.bindShading`'s `cellAspect` arg) fed straight off those two fields - so the same commit gave 7/7 PASS at 1024x768 but FAILed extra cells at other window sizes, purely from camera-plane geometry differing by viewport, not from the DDA itself. `HFOV_DEG` (`sectorCaster.js`, 75°) was already a fixed constant, not window-derived. Fix: `resize()` on both back-ends now takes optional `(refAvailW, refAvailH, refDpr)` args that override the window read (default/no-args behaviour, i.e. every real gameplay call site, is byte-for-byte unchanged); `game/js/main.js` calls `rt.resize(1280, 720, 1)` right after engine construction, before `bindShading`, whenever `?gpucompare=1` or `?gpucompare=shade` is set, so the GPU pipeline and the CPU/JS oracle (which share the same `rt`) both cast against an identical, window-size-independent box. The window `resize`/`visibilitychange`/`pageshow` listeners re-apply the same fixed box in compare mode instead of reverting to the live window size. Both panel headers now print the fixed setup, e.g. `ref: 1280x720 @dpr 1  cell: 8x13px  aspect=1.6410  fov=75 deg`. **Verified (fresh port 8924, stopped after; 8000 untouched):** `node tools/check-deps.mjs`/`check-deps.test.mjs`, `physics.test.js`, `jump.test.js`, `eyeFeel.test.js`, `playerLook.test.js` all green. `?gpucompare=1` at 1024x768, 1600x900 and 1920x1080 (Browser-pane viewport resize + reload each time) now gives **byte-identical results at all three sizes: 7/7 PASS, holes=0 on every row**, same fgMax/bgMax per pose (e.g. stair pose fgMax 27/bgMax 15, outside 0.021%/2 cells; all others 0 outside) - the window-dependence itself is fixed. Confirmed gameplay (no `?gpucompare=` param) still derives `pxCellW/pxCellH` from the live window (10x15 @1920x1080 vs 5x8 @1024x768) - untouched. Note: this run's 7/7 does not reproduce the tester's committed-state stair/start holes (1 and 5) at all - either that was already fixed by a later commit on this branch, or it was in fact a window-size artifact that this fix resolves; either way, no residual FAIL was observed at any of the three required sizes on this commit.

### US-030b N-ray coverage anti-shimmer + flicker metric + owner walk-test  [Priority: P0] [Status: testing]

> **PO ruling (2026-09-24):** the owner walk-test is the acceptance gate for shimmer per this story's own text, and it PASSED. The automated `totalPct` flicker AC is waived as unreliable (step-1 warm-up outlier in the harness; GPU n=1 bit-matches JS yet scores higher, so the metric cannot be trusted either way). Moving to `testing` for a tester pass (regression/verify checks only, not re-litigating the flicker number) before `done`. Follow-up story US-044 opened to fix the harness.
> **Owner walk-test (2026-09-23, owner's Chrome, fresh port 8930, default grid 240x90): PASS.** Owner: "`?rays=1` is very bad but normal index.html [default 2x2 rays] is super good." The final acceptance gate is met. The automated `totalPct` AC still reads FAIL, which is attributed to the flicker harness (step-1 warm-up outlier; GPU n=1 bit-matches JS yet scores higher). Next session: the PO rules to accept US-030b on the owner walk-test, with a **follow-up to fix the flicker harness** so the metric matches what the owner sees; then the architect/tester close it.

> **Owner feedback (2026-09-23, after US-028a):** walking still looks "a bit strange" (residual swimming). Owner agreed to wait for the GPU path and a finer grid. Acceptance therefore includes an **owner walk-test in real Chrome at the default grid (320x120)**: the owner confirms that shimmer/swimming is no longer bothersome. If not, raise it with the PO before `done`.
As a player, I want the picture to stop "swimming" while I move, so that the world feels solid.
(D-009 stage 2b. After US-030a `done`.)

- [x] **N-ray coverage anti-shimmer**: each cell casts N sub-rays (default 2x2, configurable 1..4x4) and picks material/tone by coverage-weighted vote. Built (see programmer notes) - **the flicker AC below is not yet met**, so this checkbox marks "built and wired", not "achieves the target".
- [x] Parity re-run: with N = 1 the GPU cast still matches the JS caster under `?gpucompare=1` (US-029 thresholds), after the vote code lands. **7/7 PASS, holes 0, glyph/kind 100% on every pose** (re-ran the same `?gpucompare=1` suite US-030a left green).
- [x] **Flicker metric**: on `totalPct` (all changed cells, no neighbour exclusion), GPU (n=2) must be **>= 20% lower than JS 1-ray**, same grid/pose/motions as `bench-cast.mjs`'s US-028a metric. `interiorPct` reported for information only, required to be not worse than GPU n=1. The absolute "<=5% per 0.02 m step" bar is dropped (grid-dependent, unrealistic per architect analysis). **Re-measured after architect review 1 items 1-4 (fix pass below): still NOT MET** - `totalPct` is 13% *higher* than JS, not lower. See the fix-pass notes for the numbers and a likely confound (a first-frame warm-up artifact in the measurement harness itself, not yet isolated). **PO waiver (2026-09-24): accepted despite this reading. The harness's own step-1 warm-up outlier makes the number untrustworthy, and the owner walk-test below (the story's actual acceptance gate) passed. Follow-up: US-044 fixes the harness.**
- [x] Budgets at 320x120 with N = 2x2 on the owner's laptop: **GPU <= 4 ms**, **JS <= 2 ms** per frame (`?bench=1`).
