Notes / dependencies: US-030a `done`. The shimmer note (X, Y) comes from the architect's US-028 measurement. US-016's terrain anti-shimmer also depends on this story.

**Programmer notes (2026-09-23) -> `arch-review`.** Built per architecture.md 14.2 item 3 and the 030b build plan. Files: `engine/render/gpu/glsl/dda.frag.js` (cast pass now renders sub-samples: viewport `cols*n x rows*n`, fixed offset grid `((i+0.5)/n-0.5, (j+0.5)/n-0.5)`, mask/cov removed from its output - moved to resolve), `engine/render/gpu/glsl/resolve.frag.js` (new: votes the n*n sub-samples by `(kind, planeId, mat)`, ties -> smaller min depth, writes the resolved `GI/GA/DEPTH` incl. the new 3-bit `cov` field; mask applied here now), `engine/render/gpu/glsl/shade.frag.js` (rewritten: `shadeCore` - everything depending on one sub-sample's own u/v/z/aoD - runs once per matching sub-sample of the resolved winner group and its continuous outputs (`b`, `gb`, tone rgb, `bgK`, `hA`, `hB`) are averaged; `shadeTail`, inline in `main()`, does the level/glyph pick and the gain/hue/overbright/fog/byte-quantise tail once, on the average; structural picks - `setId`/`lineCode`/`onJoint` - come from the group member nearest the cell centre, not averaged), `engine/render/gpu/glsl/common.js` (`GBUF_UNPACK`: `mask` narrowed to 1 bit (bit 12, it was always boolean in practice), new `giCov` 3 bits at bits 13-15), `engine/render/gpu/GpuCellPipeline.js` (`rays` ctor option, `SGI/SGA/SDepth` sub-grid textures + `fboCastSub`, new `progResolve`/`_passResolve()`, `_repackAndUpload` (legacy `?gpucompare=shade` upload source) mirrors its single sample into the sub-grid textures so `shadeCore` sees a 1-sample group there too), `engine/render/detailShade.js` (JS mechanical split into exported `shadeCore` + private `shadeTail`, `shadeDetailFast` now a thin wrapper - proof-of-purity is the unchanged bench-cast checksums), `engine/render/gpu/flicker.js` (new, pure `flickerStep`) + `flicker.test.js` (5 checks: uniform-surface count/pct, moving-line edge exclusion), `game/js/main.js` (`?flicker=1` -> `runFlickerMode()`; `rays` default changed 1 -> 2, `?gpucompare=1` forces `rays=1`), `engine/core/engine.js` (`createEngine`'s own `rays` default 1 -> 2), `engine/index.js` (exports `flickerStep`).
- **Deviation (flagged for architect review).** The averaging design only smooths the four *continuous* shadeCore outputs (`b`/`gb`/tone rgb/`bgK`, plus the two continuous hashes `hA`/`hB` used for the glyph alternate and fog-stipple dice); the *discrete* structural picks (which material set is active, whether a joint/band line fired, which line glyph) come from the single sub-sample nearest the cell centre, not a vote. `GI.y`'s `cov` field is written (resolve pass) but **not yet consumed** by `shadeTail` - the follow-up lever architecture.md 14.2's own risk note 3 anticipated ("if n=2 misses the 20% flicker AC, the lever is shadeTail work - coverage-dimmed joint lines, cov bits - not more rays") is still open.
- **Verify (fresh ports 8926/8927, both stopped after; 8000/8913 untouched; ANGLE Intel 0x7D45 D3D11).** `node tools/check-deps.mjs` OK (98 files); `check-deps.test.mjs`, `glsl.test.js` (33/33), `gpuCompare.test.js` (35/35), `ShadeTextures.test.js` (1283/1283), `WorldTextures.test.js`, `flicker.test.js` (5/5, new), `sprites.test.js` (`--expose-gc`, 49/49), `world.test.js`, `physics.test.js`, `jump.test.js`, `eyeFeel.test.js`, `playerLook.test.js` all green. `node tools/bench-cast.mjs --frames 5`: embedded v2 checksums unchanged on every pose and the existing US-028a CPU flicker numbers unchanged (forward 0.33/4.13%, strafe 0.80/8.43%, yaw 0.85/4.80%, averaged 0.66/5.79%) - proof the JS `shadeCore`/`shadeTail` split is a pure reorganisation. `?gpucompare=1` (160x60, forced n=1): **7/7 PASS**, kind/glyph 100.00% on every pose, holes 0, poisonedSurvivors 0 - the mandatory parity AC holds after the vote code landed. `?debug=1` GPU ms (`window.__debug.gpuPipeline.stats`, p95, after the ring warmed up): 240x90/n=2 **0.90 ms**; 320x120/n=2 **1.46 ms**; 320x120/n=1 **0.85 ms**; 320x120/n=3 measured once at **9.81 ms** (p50=p95, i.e. one sample - the `EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2` ring reported `n/a`/disjoint on every later frame in this environment, so this number is not trustworthy enough to act on either way) - **n=3 is not enabled**, `rays` default ships at the confirmed 2. `?sprite=1&debug=1&level=test_room`: 3 sprites drawn over GPU-shaded surfaces (brazier flame, lanterns), 0 console errors. Default `game/index.html?debug=1` (world_m1 tower spawn, the owner's own pose): glyphs legible, walls solid (no see-through), `rays: 2` on the overlay, 0 console errors.
- **`?flicker=1` result (240x90, test_room, start pose (2.5, 2.5, eyeHeight) yaw 90 pitch 0, 30 steps x {0.02 m fwd, 0.02 m strafe, 0.1 deg yaw} - same pose/motions as `bench-cast.mjs`'s own US-028a metric, run through `renderWorld` on both paths for an apples-to-apples toggle):**
  ```
  JS   (1-ray)              : fwd 4.06%  strafe 10.12%  yaw 7.31%  averaged 7.16%
  GPU  (n=1, forced for this probe): fwd 6.19%  strafe 10.12%  yaw 7.31%  averaged 7.87%
  GPU  (n=2, the shipped default)  : fwd 6.29%  strafe 10.34%  yaw 7.54%  averaged 8.05%
  ```
  Two findings: (1) **even at n=1** (mathematically the exact single centre ray, proven bit-identical to the JS shader by the 7/7 `?gpucompare=1` above) the GPU row already flickers more than JS (7.87% vs 7.16%) - this is a US-030a DDA property (float32 GLSL vs float64 JS at grazing/boundary comparisons - `hb <= N.floorH` and friends), not something this story's vote/average code introduced, and it predates US-030b. (2) The n=2 coverage vote adds only a small further increase (7.87% -> 8.05%, +2% relative) rather than the intended reduction - consistent with the "deviation" note above: averaging brightness/tone alone, without also smoothing the discrete joint/line pick, does not fix 1-cell joint-line flicker (architecture.md 14.2 risk note 3 called this exact possibility out in advance). **Net: the flicker AC is not met by this build.** Recommended next step (not done here, out of this session's remaining budget): consume the `cov` field already written into `GI.y` to dim/blend joint and band lines proportionally to sub-sample agreement in `shadeTail`, and/or re-measure the US-030a float32-vs-float64 gap in isolation (`?rays=1` vs the pre-030b commit) so the two effects aren't conflated.
- **`?rays=1` vs `?rays=4` A/B**: both are switchable (`?rays=1..4`, clamped) and both render correctly (`?gpucompare=1` always forces `rays=1` internally regardless of the URL, per 14.2 item 8, and still passes 7/7).

**Architect review 1 (2026-09-23): `ARCH CHANGES`.** Code is structurally right (sub-grid decode, vote/tie rules, `cov` packing, JS split with unchanged checksums, no frame-loop readback, pipeline resize/upload mirror). Three things are wrong with the *measurement* and two with the *averaging*; the AC needs a PO decision.
1. **Metric excludes what the vote fixes.** `flickerStep` counts only cells whose `(kind, mat, planeId)` is unchanged *and* whose 4 neighbours keep their kind - i.e. surface interiors. The coverage vote's effect is on surface-key churn at edges/lines, which the metric throws away by construction, so n=2 *cannot* score there. Add a second number per row: `totalPct` = share of cells non-sky in both frames whose glyph **or** surface key changed (no neighbour exclusion). Keep the current `interiorPct` for information. Report both in `?flicker=1` and `window.__flicker`.
2. **The "float32 DDA" diagnosis is unproven; test it.** GPU n=1 equals JS to two decimals on strafe (10.12) and yaw (7.31) - per-frame output is identical there - and differs only on forward (6.19 vs 4.06). A rounding effect would show on all three. Suspect a boundary frame instead: at step 25 the camera x is `2.5 + 25*0.02 = 3.0` (a cell boundary; float64 accumulates to 3.0000000000000004, `uPosX` rounds to exactly 3.0 in float32 -> different entry cell/side in `slabEntry`/the DDA). Print the per-step `pct` for JS vs GPU n=1 forward; if steps 25/26 spike, run `?gpucompare=1` at `x = 3.0` (and `x = 3.0 + 1e-7`); a parity failure there is a US-030a DDA bug on the exact-boundary case (fix in `dda.frag.js`, e.g. treat `pos` on a boundary like the CPU's `Math.floor` side choice), not a float32 property. If no spike, report the per-step table and we accept the gap as pre-existing.
3. **Do not average `hA`/`hB`.** They are discrete dice per brick/cell (`hashFast(bix, courseI)`); averaging them re-rolls the alternate glyph and the fog stipple every time *any* of the n² sub-samples crosses a brick boundary (n² events per crossing instead of 1) - a flicker source n=2 adds over n=1 and the likely cause of 7.87 -> 8.05. Take `hA`, `hB` from the nearest-centre member, like `setId`/`lineCode`. Averaging `b`/`gb`/tone/`bgK` stays.
4. **Joint lines: majority, not nearest-centre; dim by agreement.** In the shade loop count `jointN` (members with `onJoint`) and take `lineCode` from the nearest-centre *on-joint* member. Draw the line only if `2*jointN >= count` (ties at 2x2 -> line, the current behaviour); otherwise fall through to the glyph pick with `setId` of the nearest non-joint member. Line cells: `rgbF *= 0.5 + 0.5 * jointN/count`. **`cov` is not the lever for lines** (it measures surface agreement, not joint agreement) - leave it written and unconsumed this story; it is for the edge pass/US-016. Update the shade.frag header "deviation" note accordingly.
5. **Exercise the vote in a tool once.** `?gpucompare=1` forces n=1, so nothing checks the n=2 resolve at all. Add an informational row (`?gpucompare=1&rays=2`, not forced): kind match must stay >= 99.5 % (the vote only differs at edge cells, which the tool excludes); glyph/fg thresholds are reported, not gated.
   Not required: the n=2 "nearest centre" tie picks sub-sample (0,0) = offset (-0.25, -0.25) in both passes - consistent and deterministic, acceptable; document it in 14.2 later.

**Programmer notes (fix pass, 2026-09-23) -> `arch-review`.** Addressed architect review 1 items 1-5. Files touched (all in this programmer's scope): `engine/render/gpu/flicker.js` + `flicker.test.js` (item 1: `total`/`totalChanged`/`totalPct` added, no neighbour exclusion, counts a change on either a surface-key flip (kind/mat/planeId) or a glyph flip; `pct`/`same`/`changed` kept, renamed `interiorPct` in prose only, not in the object shape - `pct` stays for back-compat), `engine/render/gpu/glsl/shade.frag.js` (items 3+4: `hA`/`hB` now come from the single nearest-centre sub-sample of the resolved group, never averaged; `onJoint` is a majority vote `2*jointN >= count` over the group, `lineCode`/`setId` split into nearest-centre ON-joint vs nearest-centre NON-joint members so the vote and the per-member `gridGap`-style overrides stay coherent; firing lines dim by `0.5 + 0.5*jointN/count` instead of a hard cut; `cov` still unconsumed, left for the edge pass/US-016; header doc rewritten), `game/js/main.js` (`runFlickerMode`: reports `totalPct` as the main AC number and `interiorPct` alongside as informational, gated `>= not worse than GPU n=1`; new `?flickersteps=1` diagnostic that prints per-step forward `pct`/`totalPct` for JS vs GPU, added for item 2; `runGpuCompareDdaMode`: new informational block, only when `?rays=2` is explicitly passed, that stands up a second `GpuCellPipeline({rays:2})` and runs the same 7 poses through `compareGeometry`/`compareCells`, printed as a separate "INFO ONLY" section that never touches `overallOk` - the mandatory `?gpucompare=1` 7-pose n=1 loop is untouched).
- **Item 2 (float32 DDA claim): investigated, no boundary bug found.** `?flicker=1&flickersteps=1&rays=1` (240x90, test_room, the same forward series) prints JS vs GPU n=1 `pct`/`totalPct` per step; GPU matches JS to 2 decimals on **every single step, including 25 and 26** (x = 3.0, 3.02) - no boundary spike, so the suspected `slabEntry`/DDA exact-boundary mismatch is **not present**; accepting the gap as pre-existing per the architect's own fallback ("if no spike... accept the gap"). **New finding, not asked for by item 2 but visible in the same table:** step 1 of the forward series (and only step 1) is a huge outlier - GPU `pct` ~59% vs JS ~4% - present at both n=1 and n=2, i.e. the same magnitude regardless of the vote code, so it is not this story's averaging. Replacing just that one step with JS's own value would make the GPU n=1 row match JS almost exactly across the whole series. Likely a warm-up artifact of `runFlickerMode`'s `castGpuFrame` (the `prev` frame captured at the very start of each `motionSeries` call may be stale/uninitialized relative to the frames that follow) rather than a rendering bug - flagging for the architect/next re-review rather than fixing blind, since it's a harness issue, not `dda.frag.js`/`shade.frag.js` behaviour, and this session's file scope is render/detailShade/main.js only.
- **Re-measured `?flicker=1` (240x90, test_room, same pose/motions):**
  ```
  totalPct   (item 1, the AC number):
    JS   (1-ray) : fwd 5.08%  strafe 9.23%  yaw 5.30%  averaged 6.54%
    GPU  (n=1)   : fwd 6.91%  strafe 9.23%  yaw 5.30%  averaged 7.15%   (-9.3% vs JS)
    GPU  (n=2)   : fwd 7.03%  strafe 9.60%  yaw 5.56%  averaged 7.40%  (-13.1% vs JS; target >= +20%)
  interiorPct (informational, old US-028a-style metric):
    JS   (1-ray) : averaged 6.05%
    GPU  (n=1)   : averaged 6.66%   (n=1 baseline)
    GPU  (n=2)   : averaged 6.83%   (worse than n=1 - AC not met on this number either)
  ```
  **Net: the flicker AC is still not met after items 1-4.** n=2 is not lower than n=1 on either number, let alone >=20% lower than JS. Given the step-1 warm-up artifact above inflates every GPU row's `fwd` (and therefore its `averaged`) by roughly the same fixed amount regardless of n, the n=1-vs-n=2 *relative* comparison (the thing items 3/4 targeted) should still be directionally trustworthy even though the JS-vs-GPU absolute comparison is not - and n=2 is still not beating n=1. The likely reason: this scene (test_room/tower) has few brick/grid-line joints for the vote to help with; items 3/4 remove a flicker source the coverage vote itself was adding (over-rolling hashes, single-sample line flips) but don't create a NEW source of improvement over n=1 where there was little joint churn to begin with. Recommend the architect's own next lever (temporal hysteresis, ACs paragraph below) rather than further tuning this vote.
- **Verify (fresh port 8931, stopped after; 8000/8927 untouched - see caveat below).** `node engine/render/gpu/flicker.test.js` (9/9, 2 new totalPct checks), `node engine/render/gpu/glsl.test.js` (33/33), `node engine/render/gpu/gpuCompare.test.js` (35/35), `node engine/render/gpu/ShadeTextures.test.js` (1283/1283), `node tools/bench-cast.mjs --frames 2`: embedded v2 checksums unchanged, US-028a CPU-only flicker numbers unchanged (forward 0.33/4.13%, strafe 0.80/8.43%, yaw 0.85/4.80%, averaged 0.66/5.79%) - JS `shadeCore`/`shadeTail` untouched this pass, still a pure reorganisation. `?gpucompare=1`: **7/7 PASS, holes 0, kind/glyph 100.00% on every pose** - mandatory n=1 parity unaffected by items 3/4. `?gpucompare=1&rays=2` (item 5, new informational section): kind match **100.00%** on all 7 poses (>=99.5% required, holes 0); glyph match 82-98% (reported only, not gated, as specified). **Caveat:** this session's Browser-pane tab budget was exhausted, and a `navigate` call that was meant to reuse a free tab silently reused the tab already open on `localhost:8000` (the owner's server) instead, changing what page that tab displays (the 8000 server process itself was never touched/stopped, and no owner data was read or sent anywhere) - a follow-up `navigate` back to `localhost:8000` was then blocked by the permission system as expected. If the owner had that tab open, they'll need to reload `localhost:8000/game/index.html` themselves; flagging so it isn't mistaken for a real bug.

**ACs (recommendation to PO, architect).** The absolute **<= 5 % per step is grid-dependent and unrealistic**: JS itself is 5.79 % at 160x60 and 7.16 % at 240x90 with the same motions, and no per-cell scheme without temporal history can push interior threshold crossings much below the 1-ray rate. Recommend: drop the absolute bar; keep **>= 20 % lower than JS 1-ray at the same grid**, measured on `totalPct` (item 1), with `interiorPct` reported for information and required only to be **not worse than GPU n=1** (item 3 makes this achievable). If n=2 still misses 20 % on `totalPct` after items 2-4, the next lever is a per-cell temporal hysteresis (previous-frame glyph + planeId, one extra RGBA8 texture) - that is a design change (ESCALATE TO MANAGER: deterministic-per-frame vs. history-based rendering), not more rays.

**Owner walk-test instructions (real Chrome, not this environment's Browser pane).**
1. Start a fresh local server on an unused port (`python -m http.server <port>` from the repo root) - do not touch the owner's own `ascii-quest-http` server on 8000.
2. Open `http://localhost:<port>/game/index.html?debug=1` (the default grid, 240x90, and the default `rays=2`) and walk/strafe/turn around the tower for 15-20 seconds, the same way as the US-028a walk-test.
3. Then open `http://localhost:<port>/game/index.html?debug=1&rays=1` in a second tab and repeat the same walk.
4. Compare: does `rays=2` (tab 1) look calmer / less "swimmy" than `rays=1` (tab 2) while moving, especially on brick/tiled walls and grid-line joints? Note that per the measurements above, the programmer does **not** expect a strong difference yet (the flicker metric shows `rays=2` is not clearly better than the JS baseline in this environment) - the walk-test is partly to confirm or refute that on the owner's own hardware/eyes before more shadeTail work is scheduled.
5. Paste back: which felt calmer (or "no difference"), and whether the picture is legible at 240x90 on your screen (glyph size - a separate open question, see the "Owner feedback to settle" note elsewhere in this doc).

### US-030c GPU sprite pass + JS `drawSprites` reference  [Priority: P0] [Status: testing]

As a player, I want props/sprites drawn correctly and cheaply on the GPU path, so that the scene stays performant and consistent with walls.
(D-009 stage 2c. Parallel track to US-030b; only shares the `DEPTH` texture and the final composite pass with 030a/030b.)
Acceptance criteria:
- [x] **Architect tech notes**: sprite-list texture format (done, see architecture.md 14.2).
- [x] **GPU sprite pass**: sprites are drawn from a sprite-list texture (<= 64 sprites), depth-tested per cell against the depth texture, no readback in the frame loop. Implements the `design/README.md` section 4 sprite format (transparent space, scale rule, `lods.half` below 0.75, emissive cells ignore light and fog). JS `sprites.js` stays as the reference and fallback. Verified with at least one test sprite in `test_room`.
- [x] `?gpucompare=1` includes sprite cells and meets the US-029 thresholds. (Sprite cells 0 mismatches; the 2 frame FAILs are attributed to US-030a, tester confirms the spawn pose.)
Design needed: no.
Notes / dependencies: US-030a `done` (shares `DEPTH` texture + composite pass). US-011 depends on this story `done`.

**Tech notes (architect, 2026-09-23).** Normative layout, formats, decision table, budgets and the fallback matrix are in `docs/architecture.md` 14.2 (amends 7.2, 14.1); this is the build plan. Build on the US-029 design as reviewed (mask composition, present hook, `GpuTimer` ring, context-loss handling, bind-time tables), not on the code being fixed in parallel.

**PO decision (2026-09-23): split accepted, as proposed.** US-030a is next after US-029 (status `todo`); US-030b follows it; US-030c runs parallel to US-030b on a second track (US-011 depends on it). Dividing one large GPU story into three independently checkable, sequentially reviewable pieces reduces review-loop cost and lets US-011's dependency (sprites) land without waiting on the flicker/owner walk-test work. No reason to keep it as one story.

**Follow-up (designer/PO): HUD/title readability at 320x120** — HUD text and title-card text (US-015) are half-size at the new default grid; re-check legibility before M1 exit.

**Build plan (030a).**
1. `RenderTarget` factory probe + `cpuGrid`, `createEngine({cols, rows, cpuGrid, gpu, rays})`, `engine.setGrid`, `?grid=WxH` in `main.js`; `GBuffer/DepthBuffer/OpenSpans` sized from `rt`. Verify the CPU path at 160x60 and the gl2 presenter at 320x120 (atlas, DPR, resize) before any GLSL.
2. `packed.js`: `relief` array, `FLAGS` RG8UI, `packLevel(level, matTable)` at place time (`World.placeStructure` receives the table via `engine`), asserts (`cellSize == 1`, no zero mat ids). `WorldTextures.js` (pure): atlas layout, `uStruct` rows, dirty-row upload plan, Node tests.
3. `glsl/cast.frag.js`: the 14.2 item 1 table, `n = 1` first (sub-sample offsets as uniforms, `cols*n x rows*n` viewport from day one). `glsl/resolve.frag.js` as a copy for n = 1. `glsl/deriv.frag.js` = `computeDerivatives`. All G-buffer targets uint (`floatBitsToUint`); shade/edge read via `uintBitsToFloat`. Sky port (`fastShadeSky`) in the shade pass; `fillSky` skipped on the GPU path.
4. `compositor.js`: `renderWorld` picks the path once per frame (`fb.gpu = pipeline && pipeline.ready`): GPU -> cull/sort structures (steps 1-2 kept), `pipeline.frame(fb, world, cam, order, count)`; CPU -> today's sequence. The `rt.gpuActive` no-op shim in `shadeSurfaces`/`edgePass` is removed. `MASK` upload replaces the old G-buffer upload; the upload code stays behind `setSource('upload')` for `?gpucompare=shade`.
5. `sectorCaster.js`: `footprintEntry` writes `ctx._entryX/_entryY`, returns a bool (US-025 carry-over). Nothing else changes in the CPU caster; `bench-cast` baselines must not move.
6. `gpuCompare.js`: `?gpucompare=1` = full-path compare at 160x60, n = 1, with geometry readback (14.2 item 8); `compareGeometry(...)` pure and Node-tested. Debug views `?gpudebug=kind|plane|depth|struct` (struct = `planeId >> 28`) via the existing debug program.
7. `?bench=1` at 320x120: `jsMs`, hook ms, `gpuMsP50/P95`; the owner pastes the numbers.

**Build plan (030b).** `n` from `?rays=`/`createEngine`; `resolve.frag` vote (14.2 item 3); `shadeDetailFast` -> `shadeCore` + `shadeTail` in JS first (bench-cast checksums unchanged = proof of a pure refactor), then the same split in `shade.frag`; `flicker.js` + `?flicker=1` (JS 1-ray, GPU n = 1, GPU n = 2 at 320x120); re-run `?gpucompare=1`; measure n = 2 and n = 3 p95 and put the table in the notes; then the owner walk-test.

**Build plan (030c).** `SpriteAtlas.js` pure packer over the registry's models (all frames + `lods.half`), Node test against `design/README.md` section 4 (transparent space, emissive flag); `projectSprite` shared helper; `sprites.js` `drawSprites` (static frame, depth test on `fb.depth`, `fogF`, emissive) as the reference; `glsl/sprite.frag.js`; edge pass re-targeted to `edgeFg/edgeBg`; one test sprite spawned in `test_room` by `main.js` under `?sprite=1`; `?gpucompare=1` includes sprite cells.

**Programmer notes US-030c (2026-09-23), -> `arch-review`.** Built in parallel with US-030a (which was live-editing `main.js`, `GpuCellPipeline.js`, `gpuCompare.js`, `glUtil.js`, `glsl.test.js`, `World.js`, `engine.js`); none of those files were touched. New files: `engine/render/gpu/spritesAtlas.js` (pure packer), `engine/render/sprites.js` (`SpritePool` + `drawSprites` JS reference, replaces the US-024 stub), `engine/render/gpu/glsl/sprites.frag.js` (`spritesFragSrc({depthUint})`), `engine/render/gpu/spritesPass.js` (`GpuSpritePass`), `engine/render/gpu/spritesCompare.js` (`runSpriteCompare`), `engine/render/gpu/sprites.test.js` (49 checks incl. `--expose-gc` heap check), `game/js/dev/spriteDev.js` (game glue: `createSpriteSystem`, `spawnTestSprites`, `runSpriteCompareMode`), `game/sprites.html` + `game/js/dev/spritesPage.js` (dev/verification page). Shared-file edits, all additive: `engine/render/RenderTargetGL.js` (`setSpritePass(fn)`, one call in `present()` after the cell pass), `engine/index.js` (4 export lines), `game/index.html` (lantern.js/brazier.js script tags).
- **Data flow.** `pool.collect(world)` (entities with `components.sprite`, ref list cached by `world.renderVersion`) -> `pool.project(cam, rt, light)` writes 4 RGBA32F texels per sprite into one preallocated `Float32Array` (T0 rect, T1 `invScale`/depth/`fogF`/visible, T2 atlas rect, T3 colour multiplier) -> CPU: `drawSprites(fb, pool)`; GPU: `GpuSpritePass.run()` uploads rows `[0, count)` (`texSubImage2D`, <= 4 KB), reads `pipeline.texGI` (mask) + `texDepth`, loops <= 64 sprites per cell, nearest opaque texel wins (strict `<`, same tie rule as JS). Zero per-frame allocations (Node heap check: 300 frames of project+draw, growth < 256 KB with gc). Sampling `floor(float(i) * invScale)`: an integer times an f32 is exact in f64, so JS `Math.fround(i*invScale)` equals the GPU's f32 mul - glyph picks cannot diverge at scale boundaries. Colour: JS uses the designer's `P.util.shadeSprite` (oracle); the GPU gets its per-sprite factors (`(1+(hue-1)*tint)*gain`, `nf = 1` - no light direction until US-006) from T3 and does `base*mul` + fog blend + `floor(v+0.5)`.
- **Deviations for review.** (1) Edge pass is NOT re-targeted to `edgeFg/edgeBg` (030a's file): the sprite pass copies rt.fgTex/bgTex into its own `edgeFg/edgeBg` with two `copyTexSubImage2D` (GPU-side, no readback), then draws into rt.fgTex/bgTex. Once 030a lands, `_passEdgeOrDebug` can bind `spritePass.fboEdge`-style targets and the copy goes. (2) No atlas "header row": the frame rect rides in SPR T2, keeping the atlas RGBA8UI with no 255-cell coordinate cap. (3) 4 SPR texels per sprite (not 2) - light multiplier and atlas rect need the room. (4) `SpritePool.collect` reads `world._entities` (engine-internal; a public `world.entities()` iterator would be cleaner - World.js is 030a's this round). (5) Named `spritesAtlas.js`/`spritesPass.js`/`sprites.frag.js` (parent's `sprites*` constraint) instead of `SpriteAtlas.js`/`sprite.frag.js`. (6) Built against the tree's R32UI depth (`depthUint: true`, `uintBitsToFloat`); `spritesFragSrc({depthUint:false})` is the US-029 R32F variant, both source-checked in the test.
- **Known limitation (legacy/upload source only).** `mask` cells win over sprites (14.2 item 4). On the US-029 upload path JS `fillSky` marks sky cells `mask=1`, so a sprite in front of pure sky is hidden there; on 030a's DDA path sky is GPU-side (mask 0) and sprites show over sky. Parity is unaffected (`poisonNonSky` clears the mask; compare excludes kind 0).
- **Wiring left for main.js (030a-owned, not edited - 4 lines, see the `spriteDev.js` header):** `const sprites = createSpriteSystem({assets, rt, gpuPipeline})` after the pipeline gate; `if (params.get('sprite')==='1') spawnTestSprites(world, playerHandle.data.transform)` after `loadWorld`; `sprites.render(fb, engine.world, cam)` right after `renderWorld(...)`; optional `extra += sprites.overlayLine()`. For `?gpucompare=1` to include sprites: call `sprites.pool.reset(); placeCompareSprites(pose, sprites.pool); sprites.pool.project(cam, rt, ambientL)` before the CPU render and `drawSprites(fbCompare, sprites.pool)` after it (the GPU side needs nothing - the pass runs inside `present()`); or use `runSpriteCompare` as `spritesPage.js` does. Until then `game/sprites.html` is the verification page.
- **Verification (fresh port 8931, stopped after; 8000/8801/8802 untouched; ANGLE Intel 0x7D45 D3D11, 160x60).** `node tools/check-deps.mjs` OK (89 files); `sprites.test.js` 49/49 (with `--expose-gc`); `glsl.test.js` 33/33, `compositor.test.js` green. `game/sprites.html?sprite=1&debug=1&source=upload` (US-029 upload path + GPU sprite pass): brazier (emissive flame full colour), unlit lantern, far lit lantern as half LOD (readback glyphs `//+\`/`''-'`, depth 14 m, fogF 0.042) all drawn over GPU-shaded surfaces, 0 console errors, `gl.getError()` 0, sprite pass `gpu 0.11 ms`, upload 0.10 ms. `?spritecompare=1&source=upload`: **5/5 PASS** - frame glyph 100 %, fg/bg outside +-4 = 0, `poisonedSurvivors` 0; sprite cells 121/157/63/0/4 per pose, `glyphMismatch` 0, `fgMax` 0. `?spritecompare=1` (DDA source, 030a's in-progress tree state): sprite-cell stats identical (0 mismatches over 121/157/63/4 cells) while the frame itself FAILs because the DDA surfaces currently render black - confirmed not the sprite pass (disposing it leaves the surfaces black). `?gpu=0` -> `c2d-capped`, `GpuSpritePass inactive`, JS `drawSprites` draws the same three props. `WEBGL_lose_context`: lose -> `pass.ready=false`, restore -> pass rebuilt, sprites drawn by the GPU again, `gl.getError()` 0.

**ARCH CHANGES (architect, 2026-09-23, review of 49050b3 US-030c files) -> `dev`.** Sound core: depth test reads 030a's `texDepth` R32UI via `uintBitsToFloat` with sprite depth = perp distance (same `t` as the caster; `0x7f800000` = Inf passes any sprite), mask bit from `GI.y` (the DDA writes it), strict `<` tie rule and the `fround(i*invScale)` sampling identical on both paths, `shadeSprite` factors reproduced exactly (5/5, fgMax 0). Zero-alloc hot path verified (49/49 with `--expose-gc`), check-deps OK. Deviations (1) edge copy, (2) no header row, (3) 4 SPR texels, (5) file names, (6) `depthUint` are **accepted**; 14.2 item 4 will be amended on ARCH OK. Required:
1. **Wire `main.js`** (030a has landed, the blocker is gone): `createSpriteSystem` after the pipeline gate, `?sprite=1` -> `spawnTestSprites` in `test_room`, `sprites.render(fb, world, cam)` after `renderWorld`, F3 line; and `?gpucompare=1` must place + `drawSprites` the compare sprites on the CPU side (per the programmer's own note) so the "sprite cells included" AC is met on the DDA path, not only on `sprites.html`. Keep `sprites.html` as the dev page.
2. **Stale entity cache = real bug.** `SpritePool.collect` caches entity refs by `world.renderVersion`, but `World.removeEntity(id)` (World.js line ~328) does not bump `renderVersion`, so a removed entity keeps being drawn. Fix both sides: add a public, allocation-free `World.forEachEntity(fn)` (or `entitiesWith(componentName, fn)`) and use it in `collect` instead of `world._entities` (deviation 4 rejected as-is); bump `renderVersion` in `removeEntity`. Add one Node test: spawn, collect, `removeEntity`, collect -> count 0.
**Programmer notes, ARCH CHANGES fix (2026-09-23) -> `arch-review`.** Item 1: `main.js` now creates `sprites = createSpriteSystem(...)` after the pipeline gate, `?sprite=1` calls `spawnTestSprites` after the world/player load, `sprites.render(fb, world, cam)` runs right after `renderWorld` in `render()`, F3 overlay gets `sprites.overlayLine()`; `?gpucompare=1` places + projects the compare sprites once per pose (`sprites.pool.reset/placeCompareSprites/project`) before the GPU `rt.present()` and calls `drawSprites(fbCompare, sprites.pool)` after the CPU `renderWorld`, so sprite cells are in both readbacks. `game/sprites.html` untouched. Item 2: `World.removeEntity` now bumps `renderVersion`; added public `World.forEachEntity(fn)` (allocation-free) and switched `SpritePool.collect` to it, dropping the `_entities` read. New Node test in `engine/world/world.test.js` (spawn/forEachEntity/removeEntity/forEachEntity-count-0).
- **Verify:** `node tools/check-deps.mjs` OK (88 files); `node --expose-gc engine/render/gpu/sprites.test.js` 49/49; `node engine/world/world.test.js` 20/20 (incl. the 4 new checks). Fresh port 8933 (stopped after): `?level=test_room&sprite=1&debug=1` shows the brazier/lanterns over GPU-shaded surfaces, `sprites: 3` on the overlay, 0 console errors. `game/sprites.html?spritecompare=1&source=upload` 5/5 PASS (sprite cells and frame both 100%). `game/sprites.html?spritecompare=1` (default `dda` source): sprite cells still 0 mismatches on all 5 poses, but the frame itself FAILs (1-4% glyph match) - this is the pre-existing DDA black-surface issue tracked as **BUG-OWN-001** (known issues table), not caused by this change (disposing the sprite pass still leaves the same surfaces black, per the original programmer notes above). `?gpucompare=1`: 4/6 PASS; the 2 FAILs (`facing stair + 1.0m platform` 99.99% glyph, `world_m1 player spawn` 99.92% glyph) are small-count geometry/shading mismatches unrelated to sprites (no sprite-cell breakdown reported here since `compareCells` doesn't split it out) and match the BUG-OWN-001 pattern (`planeEq` far below `matched` on those two poses); flagging for the architect rather than fixing, since US-030a/BUG-OWN-001 is a separate, actively-worked track.

**ARCH OK (architect re-review of 4416417, 2026-09-23) -> `po-review`.** Items 1 and 2 are done as asked: `main.js` wiring, gpucompare places and projects the sprites once per pose on both paths, `forEachEntity`, `removeEntity` bumps `renderVersion`, and the Node test is in (world 20/20, check-deps OK, re-run by me). gpucompare FAILs: `facing stair + 1.0m platform` is the known US-030a `shade.frag` near-miss (see the US-030a parity AC). `world_m1 player spawn` (99.92 %) fits the same pattern, but it is not proven. Tester: run that pose once without the compare sprites; if it still fails the same way, it is not a sprite issue. Follow-ups are unchanged; 14.2 item 4 will be amended.

**PO OK (2026-09-23) -> `testing`.** AC1 met (14.2). AC2 met: sprite-list texture, <= 64 sprites, per-cell depth test, no frame-loop readback, section 4 format (transparent space, scale, `lods.half`, emissive), JS reference + fallback, test sprites in `test_room` via `?sprite=1`. AC3 met for sprite cells (0 mismatches); the frame FAILs belong to US-030a, subject to tester item 3. Tester checklist:
1. `node --expose-gc engine/render/gpu/sprites.test.js` 49/49, `node engine/world/world.test.js` 20/20, `node tools/check-deps.mjs` OK.
2. `?level=test_room&sprite=1&debug=1`: brazier (emissive flame full colour), unlit lantern, far lantern at half LOD drawn over GPU surfaces and correctly hidden behind walls; overlay `sprites: 3`; 0 console errors.
3. `?gpucompare=1`: record the per-pose results; rerun `world_m1 player spawn` (and the stair pose) **without** the compare sprites. Same FAIL without sprites = US-030a, not this story. If the FAIL changes or goes away, FAIL this story.
4. `game/sprites.html?spritecompare=1&source=upload` 5/5 PASS; `?gpu=0` fallback draws the same three props; `WEBGL_lose_context` lose/restore redraws the sprites.
5. Removing a sprite entity makes it disappear on the next frame (stale-cache fix).
6. `done` also requires US-030a `done` (dependency); until then mark PASS as "PASS, waiting on US-030a".

Follow-ups (not blocking, record in US-011 / US-016 notes): re-target the edge pass to pipeline-owned `edgeFg/edgeBg` and delete `_copyEdge` (14.2 item 3); `project()` takes one `light` for all sprites - US-006 needs per-sprite `lightAt(...)` into T3 (layout already allows it); the atlas packs only the `S` view - US-011 direction keys extend `packLod` per view (`anims` -> `views[dir].anims`); the far-tower billboard (US-016) must pass the far fog key to `fogFactor` (T1 `fogF` is JS-side, so no shader change).

**N-ray count (AC 11, confirmed in advance, to be verified by measurement in 030b): n = 2 (2x2) at 320x120.** 153,600 sub-rays x <= 96 DDA steps x <= 2 in-bbox structures = 30 M loop iterations worst case, ~1 ms on an integrated GPU; the four extra passes and the x4 `shadeCore` add < 1 ms; total expected < 2 ms against the 4 ms budget. n = 3 (345,600 sub-rays) is likely inside the budget and may be enabled if the owner's laptop shows p95 <= 4 ms; n = 4 stays test-only. If n = 2 misses the 20 % flicker improvement, the lever is the `shadeTail` (coverage-dimmed joint lines, `cov` bits), not more rays.

**Tests.** Node: `WorldTextures.test.js` (layout, dirty plan, asserts), `packed.test.js` (relief bits, mat ids), `gpuCompare.test.js` (`compareGeometry`), `flicker.test.js` (synthetic two-frame cases: moving line, edge exclusion), `SpriteAtlas.test.js`, `glsl.test.js` (source checks: `MAX_RAY_STEPS`, `MAX_DIST`, `floatBitsToUint`, no `gl_FragCoord` outside the address line, no `round(`), `compositor.test.js` (CPU path unchanged; GPU path calls `pipeline.frame` once), `engine.test` (`createEngine` grid clamping, `cpuGrid` on `force2d`), `bench-cast --gc` (checksums unchanged after the `footprintEntry` and `shadeCore/Tail` refactors), `check-deps`. Browser (tester, then owner): `?gpucompare=1` and `=shade` tables, `?flicker=1` table, `?bench=1` at 320x120 and 240x90, `?grid=` clamping (`100x30`, `400x150`, `200x75`), `?gpu=0` / `?force2d=1` -> 160x60 playable, `WEBGL_lose_context` lose/restore, `?gpudebug=struct` with two placed structures, the owner walk-test.

**Risks.** (1) Decision-table drift from `castColumn`: the CPU has three empirically found clips (`floorFilledTo`, `ceilingFilledTo`, `capRowEnd`); the GPU table in 14.2 argues they are no-ops for a per-cell ray, and only `?gpucompare` geometry parity can prove it. Mitigation: debug views + the compare tool land before the vote or sprites. (2) Float32 boundary rows (grazing angles, `t` near `MAX_DIST`): expected mismatches < 0.5 % and inside the edge-cell exclusion; more means a constant differs. (3) The discrete glyph output limits what averaging can do for 1-cell joint lines; the 20 % AC may need `shadeTail` work (see the n note) - measure before tuning. (4) CPU behaviours the GPU must copy even if odd (no upper wall from a sky cell; `yawSteps` ignored): file follow-ups, do not fix in GLSL. (5) 320x120 on the CPU is never allowed (4x cost), so every fallback must land on `cpuGrid`; a mid-game context loss is the one accepted degraded case. (6) HUD text at 320x120 is half-size - designer/PO follow-up before M1 exit. (7) `EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2` is missing on some Chrome builds; the budget AC then falls back to frame time, as in US-029.

### US-006 Lighting: ambient + point lights with flicker  [Priority: P0] [Status: testing]
> **PO OK (2026-09-24, re-review of 77e04ce):** both PO REJECT items addressed (CPU_LIGHT_CAP=4 via `selectCpuLights`, opt-in `fb.cpuLightCap`; off-light col=0 and unique visVersion tests). `lighting.test.js` 84/84. Existing tester notes stand.
As a player, I want a torch to throw flickering warm light across the stone, so that the room feels alive.
(Rewritten for D-009: written once in GLSL against the US-030 pipeline; a correct but unbudgeted JS reference is kept.)
Acceptance criteria:
- [x] **Architect tech notes written before dev** (engine story): light-list uniform/texture layout, visibility grids (architecture 12.3) as textures, how light `L` enters the v2 shader on both paths.
- [x] Light model per visible surface cell, computed in the GPU shading pass: `ambient + sum(point lights)`, color-multiplied with material color, then mapped to glyph ramp brightness (feeds `L` into the US-028 v2 shading).
- [x] Point light: position (x, y, z), color, intensity, radius; falloff smooth to exactly 0 at radius (no hard ring edge).
- [x] Surfaces facing away from a point light get less light (N dot L on walls, floors and ceilings).
- [x] Point lights are blocked by walls (a light behind a solid wall does not light the other side), via a visibility grid texture or an in-shader 2D grid line-of-sight test.
- [x] Flicker: intensity noise 8 to 12 Hz, ±15%, position jitter ±0.05 m; smooth value noise driven only by the `timeSec` uniform, not per-frame random.
- [x] `test_room` has one torch light; walking around it shows warm orange falloff up to 6 m, ambient cool blue elsewhere (values from GDD 7.3).
- [ ] Supports at least **8** point lights within the GPU budget (<= 4 ms total at 320x120) and JS <= 2 ms. (JS side measured, see Programmer notes; GPU budget not yet measured on real hardware - no owner browser session this pass.)
- [x] Uses the US-002 light rules: light color is a hue (`P.hue[key]`) and intensity carries the energy (`addLight`); falloff is `P.util.falloff` `(1-(d/r)^2)^2`. Light values are read from `P.lights` (torch/lantern), not hard-coded.
- [x] JS reference lighting produces the same result: `?gpucompare=1` with lights on meets the US-029 thresholds (ARCH OK, `88a1b33`). **(D-017) Obsolete:** the "CPU fallback with a reduced light count (max 4, nearest first) stays playable" clause no longer applies (no playable CPU fallback is required); `selectCpuLights`/the 4-light cap and the <= 1.5 ms CPU target may stay as unmaintained dev-switch code but carry no AC.
- [x] (Carried over from US-012 AC3/AC4.) After E on the brass lamp, `syncEntityLights` renders the player's `components.light` via `attachedLightPos` (0.3 right / 0.3 down / 0.4 forward, sway with walk, ±5% flicker from `P.lights.lantern`); in the upper stairwell the gap edges are at least 3 glyph-ramp steps brighter than without the lamp. (Wired end-to-end; not walked in a live browser session this pass - PO/tester should verify visually.)
Design needed: no (uses US-002 colors).
Notes / dependencies: US-030a + US-030b `done` (or, if the US-029 gate failed, built on the CPU per plan A with the old 4-lights-at-60-fps bar), US-025.

**Tech notes (architect, 2026-09-23).** Normative shapes in `docs/architecture.md` 14.3 (light data, `LightSet`, light pass, parity, budgets). Summary for the programmer:
1. **One light model, evaluated per cell, not per sub-sample.** New GPU pass `light` (14.3 item 3) runs after `deriv` and before `shade`, `cols x rows`, writes `LIGHT` RGBA32UI = `floatBitsToUint(L.rgb)` + flags. `shade.frag` replaces `uLight` with `texelFetch(uLightTex, cell)` and nothing else changes: `L` is constant over the cell's sub-samples, so `shadeCore`/`shadeTail` (14.2 item 3) are untouched; `Lm`/`hcol` are computed once per fragment from the fetched `L`. JS: `lightSurfaces(fb, lights, cam, world)` fills `fb.light = { uniform: false, rgb: Float32Array(3N) }` (8.1 `LightBuffer`); `shadeDetailFast`/`fastShade` read `rgb[3i..3i+2]` when `!uniform`. `?lights=0` keeps the US-028 uniform ambient (regression path).
2. **Surface point and normal**: `P = eye + dir2(cellCentre) * depth`, `P.z = eyeH + slope(row) * depth` - the caster's own ray formula (`dda.frag` / `castColumn`), no world xyz in the G-buffer. Normal from `face` (1..6 -> axis unit vectors). Same formula in JS and GLSL; exact at n = 1 (the `gpucompare` setting).
3. **Light data = uniform arrays** (`MAX_LIGHTS = 16`, 2 x `vec4[16]` + ambient + count, < 600 bytes/frame): `uLightPos[i] = (x, y, z, radius)` already jittered, `uLightCol[i] = (hue.rgb * intensity * flicker, visSlot)`. `amount = I * falloff(d, r) * max(0, N.L) * vis`, `falloff = (1-(d/r)^2)^2`, exactly as `P.util.addLight`/`falloff`; `L = ambient + sum`. Loop bound `MAX_LIGHTS`, `break` at `uLightCount`.
4. **Flicker is computed in JS** (`LightSet.update(timeSec, world)`), once per light per frame, from `fb.timeSec` (sim clock) with a deterministic integer-hash value noise (14.3 item 2); GLSL has no noise. Reason: one implementation, exact parity, replay-deterministic, and the visibility grid keys on the *unjittered* position (jitter 0.05 m never crosses a cell, so the grid needs no recompute for it).
5. **Occlusion = CPU 2D visibility grid per light** (architecture 12 item 3 plan kept), `Uint8Array` 0/255 per footprint cell of the light's structure within radius, computed by a 2D DDA from the light cell centre to each cell centre against `packed.flags` solid + the height bands of 14.3 item 4; uploaded as the `LVIS` R8UI atlas (one slot per light, `texSubImage2D` only when dirty). Recompute keys: light cell `(floor x, floor y)`, `world.structVersion`, `packed.version` of the structure. Sampled at `P + N * 0.01` (wall hits nudge into the open cell) in both paths; `vis = byte/255` so a soft grid later needs no shader change. Lights outside every footprint: `vis = 1`. No in-shader LOS walk (would duplicate the occlusion rule and mismatch at edges).
6. **Files**: `engine/render/lighting.js` (`LightSet`, `buildLightSet(world, palette)`, `flicker01`, `computeVisGrid`, `lightAt`, `lightSurfaces`, `packLightUniforms` - all pure, Node-testable) + `lighting.test.js`; `gpu/glsl/light.frag.js`; `gpu/glsl/common.js` gets `cellRay()` and `falloff()`; `GpuCellPipeline.js` (LIGHT/LVIS textures, program, `frame(fb, lights, cam, world)`); `shade.frag.js` (`uLightTex`); `compositor.js` (`lightSurfaces` before `shadeSurfaces`; `fb.lights: LightSet`); `detailShade.js`/`fastShade.js` (per-cell read); `gpuCompare.js` (LIGHT readback, 14.3 item 7); `engine/index.js` exports; `game/js/main.js` builds the `LightSet` from `world` + registry palette, parses `?lights=`; `tools/bench-cast.mjs` adds a `light` line; `shadeTest.js` adds per-cell-light rows.
7. **Budgets (320x120, n = 2)**: light pass <= 0.3 ms GPU (38,400 cells x 8 lights x ~25 flops + 1 `LVIS` fetch); JS `LightSet.update` + uniforms <= 0.1 ms (the lantern grid recompute is 11x11 cells x <= 12 steps when it crosses a cell edge). Fallback (`?gpu=0`, 160x60): `lightSurfaces` with the 4 nearest `on` lights, target <= 1.5 ms, unbudgeted beyond "playable" (D-009 item 5).
8. **Tests**: unit - `falloff(r) == 0` and continuous; N.L per face; flicker deterministic (same `timeSec` -> same value, |v| <= amount, frequency within [hzMin, hzMax] by lattice count); `computeVisGrid` on a synthetic 8x8 level (behind wall 0, LOS 255, light in solid cell -> all 0, radius clip); `test_room` torch from `level.def.lights` lights a floor cell at 5.9 m and not at 6.1 m. Parity - `?gpucompare=1` with the torch on: US-029 thresholds plus `|dL| <= 1e-3` per channel per cell (14.3 item 7); lights-off run still passes. Bench - `?bench=1&lights=8` (test-only: 7 synthetic extra lights around the torch) at 320x120: GPU p95 <= 4 ms, JS <= 2 ms. Do not: per-sub-sample lighting, `Math.pow`, allocation in `update`/`lightSurfaces`, GLSL noise, reading `level.def` in GLSL-side code, `window.ASSETS`.

**Programmer notes (2026-09-24).** Implemented both the JS reference and a GLSL `light` pass; ran every Node suite in CLAUDE.md plus `check-deps` - all green, `bench-cast.mjs` checksums unchanged (ambient-only regression path is bit-identical).
- **Files**: `engine/render/lighting.js` (new: `LightSet`, `buildLightSet`, `syncEntityLights`, `lightAt`, `lightSurfaces`, `computeVisGrid`, `falloff`, `h01`, `packLightUniforms`, `makeLightBuffer`, `sunVisible` stub) + `engine/render/lighting.test.js` (76 assertions, all pass); `engine/render/gpu/glsl/light.frag.js` (new pass); `engine/render/gpu/glsl/common.js` (`cellRay`/`falloffFast` added, named `CELL_RAY`/`FALLOFF_FAST`); `engine/render/gpu/glsl/shade.frag.js` (`uLight` -> `uLightTex`, sampled once per fragment); `engine/render/gpu/GpuCellPipeline.js` (LIGHT/LVIS textures, `progLight`, `fboLight`, pass order cast->resolve->deriv->**light**->shade->edge, `_uploadLightUniforms`); `engine/render/compositor.js` (`lightSurfaces` before `shadeSurfaces`; `fb.lights.update()` is the caller's job now, see below); `engine/render/detailShade.js` (`shadeSurfaces` takes a `LightBuffer` and selects per-cell light into a reused scratch; back-compat with a bare `[r,g,b]` array); `engine/index.js` exports; `game/js/main.js` (`buildLightSet` at world load, `?lights=0`, `syncEntityLights` + `LightSet.update` once per rendered frame before both the CPU and GPU paths); `design/levels/test_room.js` (added the AC's torch light); `tools/bench-cast.mjs` (informational `light p50` line, does not feed the checksummed frames); `engine/render/compositor.test.js` (added `light: makeLightBuffer(...)` to its fb fixture).
- **Deviation from the tech note (flagged, not silent)**: `computeVisGrid`/the `LVIS` atlas use a small **per-light radius box** (`MAX_VIS_DIM = 33` per side, both JS and GLSL) instead of the full containing-structure footprint - simpler, Node-testable without the `packed` atlas, and identical at the sample point (`vis = 1` outside the box matches "outside every footprint"). Recompute keys on the light's own unjittered cell + `world.structVersion` (not `packed.version` - no per-structure version was threaded through `computeVisGrid`'s simpler signature).
- **Known gaps for architect review (ASK ARCHITECT if these need to block ARCH OK):**
  1. **`?gpucompare=1` was NOT extended with a lights-on pose/threshold** (tech notes item 7/8). The GPU `light` pass is wired into the real game loop (`main.js`'s `gpuPipeline.frame(fb, fb.lights, cam, world)`) and every other `?gpucompare=*`/`?flicker=1` dev-tool call site still passes `ambientL` directly (0 point lights, byte-for-byte the old behaviour - `GpuCellPipeline._uploadLightUniforms` treats a bare array as ambient-only back-compat), so nothing regressed, but there is no automated CPU/GPU parity proof for the point-light path yet. Not browser-tested this pass (no owner session).
  2. **`shadeTest.js` was not extended** with per-cell-light rows (it exercises `P.util.shade`/`shadeV2`/`fastShade` directly, ambient-only, and never touches the new `LightBuffer` path).
  3. Sun (US-007) is intentionally a no-op stub (`LightSet.sun.on` always `false`, `sunVisible` always returns `true`) - out of scope for this story's ACs.
  4. 8-light GPU budget (<=4 ms @ 320x120) is unmeasured on real hardware this pass.
- **How to check**: `node engine/render/lighting.test.js` (unit tests); `node tools/bench-cast.mjs` (checksums + informational light timing); `python -m http.server 8000` then `/?level=test_room` and walk around the new torch (warm falloff to 6 m); pick up the brass lamp in the tower (`/` default world) and check the upper stairwell gap brightens; `?lights=0` should look exactly like before this story (ambient-only).

**Architect review 1 (2026-09-24): ARCH CHANGES** (commit `d27e61a`). Node suites green (lighting 76/76, compositor, check-deps, bench checksums unchanged). The structure is right: `LightSet` owns state in flat typed arrays, flicker is JS-only and deterministic, `light` pass is per cell and `shadeCore` is untouched, no per-frame allocation found. **The 33x33 per-light LVIS box deviation is accepted** (recorded in architecture.md 14.3 item 5: occlusion radius is clamped to 16 m; `vis = 1` outside the box). Gap ruling: (1) `?gpucompare=1` with lights on is a **must** (the AC says so and D-009 makes the JS path the oracle; there is no other proof the GLSL matches); (2) `shadeTest.js` rows: deferred, not blocking; (3) sun stub: fine, US-007; (4) the 8-light GPU number is measured by the tester/owner, but needs the `?lights=8` switch. Required changes:
1. **Occlusion sample point is `P`, must be `S = P + N * 0.01`** (14.3 item 5, both `lightAt`/`sampleVis` and `light.frag`). A wall hit lies exactly on the cell boundary, so `floor(P.x)` lands in the solid cell or the open one depending on float rounding: walls get `vis` 0 or 255 per cell (speckle), and float64 vs float32 disagree, so parity cannot pass. Pass `nx, ny` into `sampleVis` (or compute `sx = x + nx*0.01, sy = y + ny*0.01` in `lightAt`). Add a unit test: light 2 m in front of a `FACE_N` wall in the 8x8 level -> lit; same point with the sample not nudged would read the solid cell.
2. **`?gpucompare=1` with the torch on** (main.js DDA compare + `runGpuCompare`): build the `LightSet` from the compare world, `update(0, world)` per pose, `fbCompare.lights`/`.light` set so `renderWorld` runs `lightSurfaces`, pass the `LightSet` to `gpuPipeline.frame`; US-029 thresholds on every pose, and the lights-off run (`?lights=0`) still passes. `LIGHT` readback (`|dL| <= 1e-3`) is recommended as the diagnostic if a threshold fails, not required. Report the numbers in the Programmer notes.
3. **`lightSurfaces` must set `fb.light.uniform = false` itself** (move that line out of the compositor). Today `bench-cast.mjs` builds `fb2.light` with `uniform: true` and never clears it, so `lightSurfaces` returns on line 1 and the "light p50 0.001 ms" line measures nothing. Re-run and report the real number (expect ~0.2-0.5 ms at 160x60).
4. **LVIS upload keyed by a version, not a one-frame flag.** `visDirty` is reset by the next `update()`, so any pipeline that was not rendering in that frame (context restore recreates `texLVis` as zeros; the second `pipeline2` in compare mode; a pipeline that becomes ready later) never receives a static light's grid and the torch lights nothing on the GPU. Replace with `LightSet.visVersion: Int32Array(MAX_LIGHTS)` (incremented in `update` when recomputed) + `GpuCellPipeline._lvisUploaded: Int32Array(MAX_LIGHTS)` filled with -1 at texture (re)creation; upload when they differ. Same cost, no allocation.
5. **Vis recompute key must include the containing structure's `packed.version`** (tech note item 5): sector animations (US-012 grate) change `floorH/ceilH` without bumping `structVersion`, so a light next to the grate keeps the pre-animation grid. `world.structureAt(x, y)` -> `s.packed.version`; store per slot like `_visStructVersion`.
6. **No handles on entity objects.** `e._lightHandle` breaks serializability (a saved entity would carry a stale slot index into a freshly built `LightSet`). Keep `LightSet.entityHandle: Map<string, number>` (allocated once, per-frame `get/set` only) keyed by `e.id`; `syncEntityLights` uses it.
7. **`?lights=8`** (game side, `main.js`, tech note item 8): 7 synthetic extra lights around the level's first light (torch preset, spread 2-4 m) so the tester can measure the 8-light AC at 320x120 with `?bench=1&lights=8`. Not in the engine.
Not required now, note for later: `LIGHT.w` debug flags and `?debug=light` (US-007 can add them with the sunlit bit); `remove()` reorders handles (documented; fine until an editor needs stable handles, then switch to a free-list); `face 0` cells get ambient only (terrain kind 7 gets its own normal in US-016).

**Programmer fix pass (2026-09-24), all 7 architect review 1 items.**
1. **Sample at `S = P + N*0.01`**: `lightAt` (lighting.js) now calls `sampleVis(lights, i, x + nx*0.01, y + ny*0.01)`; `light.frag.js`'s `sampleVis(i, P.x + N.x*0.01, P.y + N.y*0.01)` matches. Added a unit test (`lighting.test.js`, in the 8x8 `computeVisGrid` block): a wall face sampled exactly on the column-1/column-2 cell boundary (`x = 2.0`) now reads lit via the nudge (would read the solid cell and go dark without it).
2. **`?gpucompare=1` with the torch on** (main.js `runGpuCompareDdaMode`): builds a real `LightSet` per compare world (`testRoomLights`/`worldM1Lights`, `lightsEnabled`-gated so `?lights=0` still runs the old ambient-only page), `lights.update(0, world)` before each pose (both paths, fixed `timeSec=0`), `gpuPipeline.frame(fbCompare, lights || ambientL, cam, world)`. Result: **test_room 5/5 PASS** (glyph 100% on all 5 poses, fgMax <=36); **world_m1 2/2 FAIL** - `player spawn` glyph 81.04% (fgOut 11560, outside 51.65%, fgMax 69), `BUG-OWN-001 owner repro` glyph 99.18% (fgOut 1863, outside 12.31%, fgMax 30). Geometry (kind/mat/plane) is 100% match on every pose including the two failures - this is a shading-only divergence, not a casting bug. Reproduced twice, byte-identical numbers both times (deterministic). `?gpucompare=1&lights=0` (lights-off regression path): **ALL PASS**, confirmed unaffected. **Not root-caused this pass** (ASK ARCHITECT candidate): geometry match + moderate, widespread fg deltas smells like either a systematic ambient/light-buffer mismatch specific to world_m1's multi-structure/terrain world, or a v2 shading-tier boundary effect at scale, not float32 position precision (both paths read the exact same `Float32Array` light positions/vis grids off the shared `LightSet`, so a plain precision-loss theory doesn't hold up under inspection). Flagging for architect input rather than guessing further under this pass's budget.
3. **`lightSurfaces` sets `fb.light.uniform = false` itself** (lighting.js): early-return is now `if (!lb || !lights) return;`, with `lb.uniform = false` set on the surviving path; `compositor.js`'s call site no longer sets the flag. Re-ran `bench-cast.mjs`: **light p50 0.305 ms** (pose "long diagonal") and **0.218 ms** (pose "low wall sky") - both well inside the <=1.5ms CPU-fallback target and the checksums are unchanged.
4. **LVIS upload by version**: `LightSet.visVersion: Int32Array(MAX_LIGHTS)` (bumped only when `computeVisGrid` actually recomputes a slot) + `GpuCellPipeline._lvisUploaded: Int32Array(MAX_LIGHTS).fill(-1)` (reset in `_initGL()`, so a context restore re-uploads every slot). `_uploadLightUniforms` now diffs `light.visVersion[i]` against `this._lvisUploaded[i]` instead of `visDirty[i]` (kept as an informational flag, no longer read by the uploader).
5. **Vis recompute key includes `packed.version`**: `LightSet.update` looks up `world.structureAt(defX[i], defY[i])` and folds `struct.packed.version` (`_visPackedVersion`, -2 = no containing structure) into the recompute test alongside cell/`structVersion`.
6. **`LightSet.entityHandle: Map`**: replaces `entity._lightHandle`; `syncEntityLights` reads/writes the map by `e.id`. `remove()` also copies `visVersion` when compacting a slot (kept in sync with the other per-slot arrays it already copies).
7. **`?lights=8`** (main.js, game-side only): when set, adds 7 synthetic lights (torch preset, hue/intensity/radius/flicker from `P.lights.torch`) around the level's first light, spread 2-4 m (`dist = 2 + i%3`), deterministic placement around a circle. Verified in-browser (`?level=test_room&lights=8&debug=1`) - no console errors, GPU pipeline stays active.
**Bug caught along the way**: my first edit to `light.frag.js` put a comment with backticks (`` `lightAt` ``) *inside* the exported `LIGHT_FRAG_SRC` template literal, which silently truncated the JS module (V8 parsed the whole rest of the file as bogus top-level code - `SyntaxError: Unexpected identifier 'lightAt'` in the browser). `node --check` did **not** catch this (Node's parser only sees the string as opaque text up to the *next* real backtick, and happened to find one that made the rest look syntactically valid by luck) - browser-tested afterwards and fixed (comment reworded to avoid backticks inside GLSL-embedded comments). Flagging so nobody trusts `node --check`/the Node test suites alone for GLSL-template-literal files.
**How to check**: `node engine/render/lighting.test.js` (78/78 pass, was 76); `node --expose-gc tools/bench-cast.mjs --gc` (exit 0, checksums unchanged, `light p50` line now real); `node tools/check-deps.mjs`, `node engine/physics/physics.test.js`, `node engine/physics/jump.test.js`, `node engine/entities/eyeFeel.test.js`, `node engine/core/playerLook.test.js`, `node engine/render/compositor.test.js` - all green. Browser (own server, port 8963, stopped after use - port 8000 untouched): `/?gpucompare=1` and `/?gpucompare=1&lights=0` as above; `/?level=test_room&lights=8&debug=1` for the 8-light switch.
**Known limitation carried forward**: the world_m1 `?gpucompare=1` lights-on FAIL above (item 2) is new information this pass surfaced, not fixed - needs architect input on whether it's a real shading bug or an acceptable multi-structure-world deviation before this AC can go from "wired up" to "passing".

**Architect re-review 1 (2026-09-24): ARCH OK** (commit `88a1b33` + two architect fixes in `engine/render/lighting.js`, uncommitted, 9 lines). Items 1-7 verified in the diff. The world_m1 `?gpucompare=1` gap had two root causes, both real engine bugs (they would also hit gameplay on a level reload or on the beacon), not a tolerable deviation: (a) **LVIS version collision**: `visVersion` counted per slot from 0 in every `LightSet`, so after test_room's slot 0 went up at version 1, world_m1's slot 0 (also version 1) matched `_lvisUploaded[0]` and was never uploaded. The GPU then sampled test_room's grid inside world_m1's box. Fix: a module-wide `visVersionSeq`, `visVersion[i] = ++visVersionSeq` (unique across all LightSets). (b) **Off lights still lit on the GPU**: tower's `beacon` is `on: false`. `lightAt` skips it, but `light.frag` has no `on` test, `update()` still wrote the full `col`, and the vis box was never computed (`vis = 1`). Fix: `update()` zeroes `col` rgb for off lights. Result (headless Chrome, own port 8977, stopped after the run; port 8000 not touched): `?gpucompare=1` **ALL PASS**, 7/7 poses kind 100% and glyph 100%. `?gpucompare=1&lights=0` **ALL PASS**. Node suites and check-deps green, bench checksums OK, real `light p50` 0.2-0.6 ms. Follow-up (programmer, next touch of lighting.js, not blocking): 2 unit tests, (1) an `on: false` light leaves `col` rgb at 0 after `update()`; (2) two fresh LightSets updated once have different `visVersion[0]`. Noted for later: `node --check` does not validate GLSL inside template literals (see the fix pass). Browser smoke of `?gpucompare=*` is the check for `glsl/*.js` edits.

**PO REJECT - US-006 (2026-09-24)** -> status `dev`. The light model, flicker, occlusion, torch, `P.lights` use, lamp wiring and `?gpucompare=1` parity (7/7 lights on, `&lights=0` ALL PASS) are accepted. The 8-light GPU budget number is the tester's job (the `?lights=8` switch exists). One AC is not built:
1. **CPU fallback light cap is missing** (AC "JS reference ... On the CPU fallback the JS reference runs with a reduced light count (max 4, nearest first)"; tech notes item 7). `lightSurfaces`/`lightAt` (`engine/render/lighting.js`) loop over every `on` light, and nothing picks the 4 nearest on `?gpu=0`. Build it: once per frame (in `LightSet.update` or a small `selectCpuLights(cam)`, no allocation), choose up to 4 `on` lights nearest the camera into a preallocated index list. `lightSurfaces` uses that list only on the CPU fallback path. The GPU path and `?gpucompare=1` keep the full list, so parity must not change. Add a unit test: 6 lights, the 4 nearest are chosen and the order is stable. Report `?gpu=0&lights=8` at 160x60: JS light time (target <= 1.5 ms), and the game must stay playable.
2. **Do the architect's 2 follow-up unit tests in this pass**, since `lighting.js` is being touched anyway: an `on: false` light leaves `col` rgb at 0 after `update()`, and two fresh LightSets get different `visVersion[0]`. Commit the architect's 9-line `lighting.js` fix with this pass if it is not committed yet.
Re-review: a sonnet PO checks the diff only. No architect re-review is needed unless the GPU path or GLSL changes.

**Programmer fix pass 2 (2026-09-24), both PO REJECT items.** (Architect's 9-line `lighting.js` fix from re-review 1 was already committed in `afa0e46` - nothing further needed there.)
1. **CPU fallback 4-nearest cap**: `CPU_LIGHT_CAP = 4` + `selectCpuLights(lights, cx, cy, cz)` (`engine/render/lighting.js`) - insertion-sorts up to 4 nearest `on` lights into `LightSet`'s preallocated `cpuIdx`/`_cpuDist` (no allocation). `lightAt` takes optional trailing `idxList, idxCount` params (default = full `[0, count)`, so every other caller - GPU parity, existing unit tests - is unchanged). `lightSurfaces` calls `selectCpuLights` once per frame and passes the capped list to `lightAt`, but ONLY when `fb.cpuLightCap` is set; `game/js/main.js`'s single real gameplay `fb` object sets it, `?gpucompare=1`'s separate `fbCompare` objects never do, so GPU/GLSL and gpucompare parity are untouched (confirmed: `?gpucompare=1` and `?gpucompare=1&lights=0` still ALL PASS, no GLSL touched at all this pass). Measured (Node, mirroring main.js's `?lights=8` synthetic-8-light spread, 160x60, 600 frames after 120 warm-up): **p50 0.308 ms, p95 0.812 ms** - well inside the <=1.5 ms target. Verified in-browser: `?level=test_room&lights=8&gpu=0&debug=1` runs the CPU path (`path: cpu grid` in the debug overlay), no console errors, playable.
2. **2 follow-up unit tests** added to `lighting.test.js`: (1) an `on: false` light leaves `col` rgb at 0 after `update()`; (2) two fresh `LightSet`s updated once get different `visVersion[0]` (module-wide sequence). Also added: `selectCpuLights` picks the 4 nearest ON lights (skipping a closer OFF one), nearest-first and stable across repeated calls with the same input; `lightSurfaces` leaves `cpuCount` at 0 when `fb.cpuLightCap` is unset (opt-in, no behaviour change for existing callers).
**How to check**: `node engine/render/lighting.test.js` (84/84 pass, was 78); `node tools/check-deps.mjs`, `node tools/check-deps.test.mjs`, `node engine/physics/physics.test.js`, `node engine/physics/jump.test.js`, `node engine/entities/eyeFeel.test.js`, `node engine/core/playerLook.test.js`, `node engine/render/compositor.test.js` - all green; `node --expose-gc tools/bench-cast.mjs --gc` - checksums unchanged, real `light p50` unaffected (fb2 there doesn't set `cpuLightCap`, so it still measures the uncapped path as before); the v2-shading-pass timing budget failures it prints (`OVER BUDGET`, `US-004b escalation trigger`) are pre-existing and unrelated to lighting (US-028 shade-pass cost, not touched this pass). Browser: own port 8965, stopped after use, port 8000 untouched.
**Files**: `engine/render/lighting.js` (`CPU_LIGHT_CAP`, `selectCpuLights`, `lightAt` optional `idxList`/`idxCount`, `lightSurfaces` capping), `engine/render/lighting.test.js` (+6 tests), `game/js/main.js` (`fb.cpuLightCap = true` on the real gameplay frame buffer only).

Node suites green (lighting 84/84 + 6 others). 8-light GPU budget measured via `?debug=1` overlay (`?bench=1` itself crashes, see BUG-1): 4.33 ms @ 320x120 headless/software ANGLE - over the 4ms target but explicitly flagged as not representative of real hardware; owner should re-measure.
