### US-013 Rolling boulder  [Priority: P0] [Status: done]

**PO OK (2026-09-23) -> `testing`.** ACs verified at physics level: sphere radius 0.6, push threshold/slowdown, no-step-up, 20/20 pushes settle in NW hollow <4s and stay, rotation frames, no player/boulder overlap, restart resets to stair base. Visibility AC is legitimately deferred to US-011 (prop spawn) per architect ruling. Tester checklist (Node-level): `node engine/physics/roller.test.js` (31/31), `node game/js/quest/boulder.test.js` (29/29), `node engine/physics/physics.test.js` (187/187), `node engine/physics/jump.test.js` (111/111), `node game/js/quest/tower.test.js` (63/63), `node tools/check-deps.mjs`.

As a player, I want to push the heavy boulder off the stairs and watch it roll away, so that the world feels physical.

**D-011 (PO, 2026-09-23):** no change in behaviour. The only art change is moss on its top rows (US-011 reskin). Per D-015 the architect evaluates Rapier against the in-house sphere code when this story is picked up.
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] Boulder = sphere, radius 0.6 m, uses the sector physics (gravity, floor heights, wall collision).
- [ ] Walking into it at >= 0.5 m/s pushes it (player slows to 50% while pushing); it follows floor slope, has rolling friction and settles; restitution 0.3 against walls.
- [ ] Cannot climb any step (step threshold 0 for the boulder); cannot be pushed up the stair.
- [ ] From the start position, a push sends it down the slope into the NW hollow, where it comes to rest within 4 s and stays; the stair base is then clear.
- [ ] Rotation frames (US-011) advance with distance rolled and direction.
- [ ] Player and boulder never overlap; the boulder cannot trap the player (if the player is squeezed, the player is pushed out).
- [ ] The NW hollow is the boulder's only stable resting place: from any push direction and speed, it ends in the hollow and can never come to rest re-blocking the stair (base or any step) or the wake area. Once in the hollow (floor -0.3 m, boulder step threshold 0) it cannot be pushed back out. Test: 20 pushes from varied angles/speeds, all end in the hollow.
- [ ] Restart (US-017) puts the boulder back at its start position on the stair base.
Design needed: no (uses US-011 boulder).
Notes / dependencies: US-009, US-010, US-011.

**Tech notes (architect, 2026-09-23).** Normative APIs: `docs/architecture.md` 7.4 (Rollers).
1. **D-015 evaluation: in-house `moveSphere` + roller, not Rapier.** Rapier (`@dimforge/rapier3d-compat`, about 1.5-2 MB with its WASM embedded, async init) fails two of D-015's adoption conditions. (a) Collision would have to come from Rapier colliders built from the sector grid and the terrain, not from `World` queries, which D-015 and D-007 require. The grate's live ceiling would also have to be mirrored into those colliders. (b) Its snapshots are binary, not our JSON `WorldState`. We would store only pose and velocity and lose contact warm-start, so a save/load mid-roll would diverge. It also cannot express our rules natively (step threshold 0, the tilt field, the push slow-down, the player being the in-house capsule), so we would write custom glue anyway, for one sphere. The in-house version reuses `moveCapsule` (`moveSphere` = rise-blocked capsule), is deterministic by construction and costs <= 0.02 ms per step. **Re-evaluate Rapier** only for a future story with many dynamic bodies, stacking, or real rotational contact (M3+ physics puzzles). That would be its own engine story.
2. **Engine:** `moveSphere` (out param; the section 5 signature is updated), `stepRollers`, `resolveBodyContacts`, `rollFrame`, `Level.tiltAt` (the generic `layers.tilt` format; document it in `engine/world/MAP_FORMAT.md`), and `body.speedScale` in `integrate` step 3 (default 1, so existing physics tests stay bit-identical). New `PhysicsConfig` keys: `pushMinSpeed 0.5`, `pushSpeedScale 0.5`, `rollerDefaults`.
3. **Data:** the tower prop `boulder` (`dynamic: true, radius: 0.6`) spawns as entity `tower.boulder` with `body` + `roller` components. The mapping `dynamic -> body + roller` goes in the US-011 prop spawn. The tilt layer and the heights already exist. No quest behaviour is needed. Restart resets the boulder through serialize (US-017).
4. **Events:** none required. Optional: `roller:rest` via the entity event ring, for US-020 sound.
5. **GPU:** nothing. The boulder is a sprite; the frame comes from `rollFrame`.
6. **Tests:** `engine/physics/roller.test.js` (inline fixture levels): restitution 0.3 on a face and on a corner; cannot climb a 0.01 m rise; drops are allowed; friction settles without reversing; sleep and wake; the push threshold at 0.49 vs 0.51 m/s; the actor's `speedScale` is 0.5 only while pushing; the no-overlap invariant over 600 steps of squeezing the actor against a wall; determinism (two runs bit-identical); a serialize mid-roll -> deserialize continues identically. `game/js/quest/boulder.test.js` (real tower data): 20 pushes at varied angles and speeds, all end in `o` cells within 4 s; the boulder is never at rest on `s`/`_`/stair/wake cells; it cannot be pushed out of the hollow; the stair base is clear afterwards. Also run `node tools/check-deps.mjs`.
7. **Files:** `engine/physics/sphere.js`, `engine/physics/roller.js` (+test), `engine/physics/integrate.js` (one line), `engine/physics/config.js`, `engine/world/Level.js` (`tiltAt`), `engine/world/MAP_FORMAT.md`, `engine/index.js`, `game/js/main.js` (step line), `game/js/quest/boulder.test.js`. Fully disjoint from US-012/014/017, apart from one line in `main.js`.

**Programmer (2026-09-23), implemented per the tech notes above -> `arch-review`.**
1. `engine/physics/sphere.js`: `moveSphere(world, x, y, dx, dy, radius, z, opts, out)` = `moveCapsule(..., grounded=true, opts, out)` with `opts.height`/`opts.stepUpMax` set to `2r`/`0` in place (caller-owned `opts`, no allocation).
2. `engine/physics/roller.js` (new): `stepRollers` (tilt accel, friction as a magnitude clamp that never reverses, `moveSphere`, face/corner restitution, rollDist/yaw/sprite-frame, sleep, vertical fall/land) and `resolveBodyContacts` (push threshold + wake, separation roller-then-actor, restore-and-zero-velocity fallback), plus `rollFrame`. All scratch objects are module-level and reused (architecture.md section 9).
3. `engine/world/Level.js`: `tiltAt(x, y, out)` - the 8 numpad directions are a uniform ramp (full `grade` everywhere, by design never sleep-eligible); **`'5'` (sink) tapers linearly to 0 at `hollowCenter`** (`grade * min(dist, 1)`), which is a numeric choice I had to make (the tech note only says "sink toward centre", no formula) - a uniform pull it is not, since `gravity*grade (1.0) > rollFriction (0.8)` by design for every direction, so an untapered sink would never lose the tug-of-war against friction and the boulder would orbit the centre forever instead of the AC's "comes to rest ... and stays". Confirmed against the real tower data (see item 5). Documented in `engine/world/MAP_FORMAT.md`.
4. `engine/physics/config.js`: `pushMinSpeed 0.5`, `pushSpeedScale 0.5`, `rollerDefaults {restitution 0.3, rollFriction 0.8, sleepSpeed 0.05, sleepTime 0.25}`. `engine/physics/integrate.js`: step 3 now multiplies by `body.speedScale` (default 1, defaulted in `ensureScratch`) - existing US-008/009 tests still 187/187 and 111/111 (speedScale defaults to 1, bit-identical).
5. **Sleep gate correction:** the tech note's "tilt 0" is a tolerance, not literal float zero - a sink's pull is only exactly 0 at its exact centre, which a discrete sim settles near, never on. I gate sleep on `gravity*|tiltAt| < rollFriction`, i.e. wherever friction can fully hold the boulder against the local slope every step (a real physical rest, and self-consistent with the "ramp is never sleep-eligible" rule) - not a hand-tuned epsilon. Without this the 20-push test settled velocity to exactly 0 (friction wins each step) but never flagged `sleeping`, so a contact could never re-wake it and later stories (US-017 restart) had no clean "at rest" signal.
6. **Spawn note (flagging, not deciding):** the tech notes assign "the mapping `dynamic -> body + roller` goes in the US-011 prop spawn," but no generic prop-to-entity spawn exists yet in `World.load`/`main.js` - US-011 is still `status: design` (art only, no programmer pass). I did not add a generic spawn to `World.js`/`world_m1.js` (out of my file list, and it's arguably US-011's scope). `game/js/quest/boulder.test.js` spawns the boulder itself from the real `tower.js` prop data (`world.spawn('prop', ..., {body, roller}, 'tower.boulder')`), matching how `spawnTestSprites` already does this for other props (`game/js/dev/spriteDev.js`) - so the physics is fully exercised and tested against real data, but **the boulder does not yet appear/exist when you actually play the game in a browser** (nothing spawns it there). Flagging for the architect/PO: either US-011's prop-spawn work needs to land before US-013 is playable end-to-end, or someone should add the same small `world.spawn(...)` call for `dynamic: true` props (a few lines, additive, in `World.load` or `main.js`) as part of closing this story out.
7. **Tests:** `node engine/physics/roller.test.js` 28/28 PASS (synthetic fixture levels, all items from the tech notes' list 6). `node game/js/quest/boulder.test.js` 29/29 PASS (real tower data: 20 pushes at varied angles/speeds all settle in the `hollow` tag within 4 s, never on `stairBase`/a stair cell, cannot be pushed back out by 5 hard shoves, restart returns it to its authored start). `node tools/check-deps.mjs`: unrelated pre-existing FAILs only (`tools/bench-voxel.mjs`, untracked US-039 work in progress on another track - nothing under my files). Also reran and confirmed green: `physics.test.js` 187/187, `jump.test.js` 111/111, `eyeFeel.test.js` 18/18, `playerLook.test.js` 10/10, `tower.test.js` 63/63, `world.test.js` 20/20, `serialize.test.js` 9/9, `packed.test.js` 5/5, `terrain.test.js` 19/19.
8. Not committed (per instructions).

**Architect review #1 (2026-09-23): ARCH CHANGES** - tests run: roller 28/28, boulder 29/29, physics 187/187, jump 111/111; check-deps findings are all US-039's `tools/bench-voxel.mjs`, none in this story. Rulings: (a) **Sink taper + sleep gate accepted** and made normative in 7.4: a linearly tapering sink is a bowl potential; with Coulomb-style rolling friction the boulder rests wherever `gravity*|tilt| < rollFriction` (tower: 20*0.05*min(d,1) < 0.8, i.e. inside 0.8 m of the centre), so the gate is the static-friction condition, not an epsilon. Deterministic: pure functions of state, fixed dt, no wall clock. (b) **Spawn stays in US-011** (the generic `dynamic -> body + roller` prop spawn belongs with the prop->entity spawn; a boulder without US-011's sprite is invisible anyway; a second path here would be duplicated then deleted). PO: US-013 can only be walk-tested end to end after US-011's programmer pass; `boulder.test.js` on real tower data is the gate until then. Required changes:
1. **`resolveBodyContacts` fallback restores to the wrong position.** `startX/startY` are captured at entry, i.e. the post-`integrate` position where the overlap was found, so "restore to step-start" is a no-op and only the velocity is zeroed; the 7.4 induction argument ("non-overlapping by induction") needs the pre-`integrate` position. Fix: `integrate` records `body.prevX = t.x; body.prevY = t.y` on entry (defaults in `ensureScratch`; US-008/009 tests stay bit-identical), and the fallback restores `at.x = actorBody.prevX; at.y = actorBody.prevY`. Add a roller.test case where both restore branches are forced (roller wedged in a corner, actor driven in from the open side) and assert the final overlap <= 1e-3.
2. **Sleep only while grounded**: add `body.grounded &&` to the sleep gate, so a roller cannot be flagged `sleeping` mid-fall (a sleeping roller skips the tilt/friction block after landing and would sit on a slope until pushed).
Noted, no change needed: face (`blockedX/Y`) and corner (`nx/ny`) restitution cannot double-apply (capsule sets `nx/ny` only for corner contacts, and after a face flip `vn >= 0`). Tilt is applied while airborne (unphysical, harmless for a 0.3 m drop). Zero per-step allocation confirmed (module-level scratch). Re-review: opus, diff only.

**Programmer (2026-09-23), ARCH CHANGES #1 fixed -> `arch-review`.**
1. `engine/physics/integrate.js`: `ensureScratch` defaults `body.prevX/prevY` (0,0); `integrate` now records `body.prevX = t.x; body.prevY = t.y` as its first action, before any of the step's movement - the true pre-`integrate` position. `engine/physics/roller.js`'s `resolveBodyContacts` fallback now reads `startX/startY` from `actorBody.prevX/prevY` (was the post-`integrate` `at.x/y` captured at entry - a no-op restore). New `roller.test.js` case ("forced fallback: both restore branches") uses a hand-rolled `WorldQuery` (not a grid `Level` - D-008 allows any `sectorAt`/`outsideSector` shape) with a 1-m-wide open column boxed by two solid columns, too narrow for the roller (1.2 m diameter) to fit without touching both faces - wedging it so its own separation move can't clear it, and the same far face also stops the actor's own escape a few cm later, forcing the final restore. `actorBody.prevX/prevY` is set to a distant, legal, non-overlapping point standing in for "last step's recorded position" (decoupled from the alcove's geometry, since real single-step continuity can't reach it) - isolates the restore path itself. Asserts the restored position and a final overlap <= 1e-3.
2. `engine/physics/roller.js`: sleep gate now also requires `body.grounded` (`if (body.grounded && speedNow < roller.sleepSpeed && ...)`), so a mid-fall roller is never flagged sleeping.
3. **Tests:** `node engine/physics/roller.test.js` 31/31 PASS (28 prior + 1 new forced-fallback case, 2 assertions folded into it... actually 3 new assertions). `node game/js/quest/boulder.test.js` 29/29 PASS. `node engine/physics/physics.test.js` 187/187, `node engine/physics/jump.test.js` 111/111, `node game/js/quest/tower.test.js` 63/63 - all unchanged/green (the `tower.test.js` "5 behaviours not registered" line is a pre-existing informational log, not a failure).
4. Files touched (all within the allowed list): `engine/physics/integrate.js`, `engine/physics/roller.js`, `engine/physics/roller.test.js`. Not committed.

**Architect re-review (2026-09-23): ARCH OK -> `po-review`.** Diff of HEAD (integrate.js, roller.js, roller.test.js) only. #1: `integrate` records `prevX/prevY` first, before any movement; the fallback restores to it; the forced test covers both branches (the synthetic wedge is acceptable). #2: sleep gate now requires `body.grounded`. No new allocations. Spawn is still blocked on US-011 (ruling b).

### US-014 Lever opens the grate  [Priority: P0] [Status: done]

**Tester (2026-09-23): PASS (Node-level).** All checklist items green; report `docs/test-reports/US-014.md`.

**PO OK (2026-09-23) -> `testing`.** Engine mechanism and behaviour body are correctly scoped and verified (Node tests, no allocation, serialize round-trip, GPU dirty-row update). Visible grate/prompt behavior is legitimately blocked on US-011 (lever prop, `EntityHandle.play`) and US-012 (`E` interaction, prompt hiding); re-check the two visual/prompt ACs then. Tester checklist (Node-level, no browser needed yet): `node engine/world/sectorAnim.test.js` (85/85), `node game/js/quest/tower.test.js` (63/63, incl. lever.pull via `world.fireInteraction`), `node tools/check-deps.mjs`, confirm `stepSectorAnims` is called once per world-mode frame in `game/js/main.js` before `integrate`, confirm save-mid-open/load/finish determinism test passes, confirm `world_m1.js` no longer has the redundant `'tower.grate.open'` state key.

As a player, I want to pull the lever and see the grate rise, so that I understand cause and effect and can reach the summit.

**D-011 reskin (PO, 2026-09-23):** the lever sits in a brass gear housing (US-011 art); its hub gear advances with the 5 pull frames, so the machine visibly drives the chains. The grate stays iron. Prompt, timings, collision and data rules are unchanged. Optional (not an AC): a gear-ratchet click with the US-020 sound.
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] Prompt `[E] Pull lever` on the mid ledge; pulling plays the 5-frame lever animation over 0.4 s.
- [ ] The grate sector's ceiling rises from floor height to open height (>= 2.2 m above the floor) over 1.5 s with ease-in-out; collision updates live (blocked while below 1.70 m clearance).
- [ ] Before pulling: the upper stair is impassable. After: passable.
- [ ] One-way: after use, the lever shows no prompt.
- [ ] Grate is visible from the lever position, so the player sees it move.
- [ ] (D-006 / D-008) The lever is a `def.interactables` entry (`interact: 'lever.pull'`). The grate animation is driven by the grate sector's `dynamic` data (`ceilOpen`, `openTime`, `ease`) through a generic engine "animate sector ceiling" call. `lever.pull` is registered from `game/js/quest/` and names its target by tag (`grate`), not by cell coordinates. Grate open/closed state is part of the serialized world state (US-025).
Design needed: no (uses US-011 lever + grate).
Notes / dependencies: US-010, US-011, US-012.

**Tech notes (architect, 2026-09-23).** Normative APIs: `docs/architecture.md` 7.4 (Sector animation).
1. **Engine:** `world.animateSectorTo(tag, 1, {delay})` + `stepSectorAnims(world, dt)` (step 1 of the order, so collision is live: the 7.1 head-clearance rule reads the legend `ceilH`, which blocks while clearance < 1.70 m). The ease and `openTime` come from the sector's `dynamic`. `dynamics[tag] = {t, target, delay}` is serialized, so a save mid-opening resumes. Two required clean-ups: a tag Map in `placeStructure`, and relief written in place in `updateAnimatedSector` (no per-step allocation).
2. **GPU update (nothing new to write):** each step, `animateSector` -> `updateAnimatedSector` bumps `packed.version` and widens `dirtyY0/Y1` to the grate rows +-1. On the next frame `planFrameUpdate` uploads only those rows (`GEOM`, `FLAGS` incl. relief `g`) with `texSubImage2D`, then resets them to -1. `structVersion` is unchanged, so there is no atlas rebuild. Do not touch `engine/render/gpu/*`.
3. **Game:** `game/js/quest/lever.js` holds `lever.pull`: `ctx.entity.play('pull')` (the 5-frame / 0.4 s US-011 clip, which also turns the hub gear), `world.animateSectorTo(ctx.def.target.tag, 1, {delay: 0.4})`, `world.state['tower.lever.pulled'] = true`, return `true`. The generic `once` then hides the prompt. There are no cell coordinates.
4. **Data:** already present (`G` legend `dynamic`, interactable `target: {tag: 'grate'}`). Remove the redundant `'tower.grate.open'` from `world_m1.js` `state`: `dynamics` is the only truth.
5. **Events:** `world:sectorAnimated` (existing) and `world:sectorAnimDone` (new; US-020 can hook the ratchet sound to it).
6. **Tests (`engine/world/sectorAnim.test.js`):** with delay 0.4 s and open 1.5 s, `t` reaches exactly 1 at step 24 + 90 and follows the ease-in-out curve; `isSectorPassable` on the grate cell flips exactly when `ceilH - floorH >= 1.70`; after an in-place update, `packed.geom/flags/relief` equal a fresh `packLevel` of the mutated level; `planFrameUpdate` after one step lists exactly rows `[y0-1, y1+1]`, and after the frame `dirtyY0 == -1`; a save mid-open -> load -> finish is bit-identical to an uninterrupted run; `stepSectorAnims` does not allocate. `tower.test.js`: `lever.pull` on the real data opens the grate and gives no second target (the last part needs US-012).
7. **Files:** `engine/world/World.js` (`animateSectorTo`, tag Map), `engine/world/packed.js` (in-place relief), `engine/world/serialize.js` (`target`/`delay`), `engine/world/sectorAnim.test.js`, `engine/index.js`, `game/js/quest/lever.js`, `game/js/quest/index.js`, `game/js/main.js` (step line), `design/levels/world_m1.js` (one state key). The engine part can start now; the prompt path needs US-012 merged.

**Programmer notes (2026-09-23), status -> `arch-review`.**
- `World.js`: `placeStructure` now builds `structure.tagMap` (tag -> legend char) once. `animateSector(tag, t01)` (direct jump, used by `World.load`/`deserialize`/existing direct-jump callers e.g. `tower.test.js`) and the new `animateSectorTo(tag, target01, {delay})` (records intent only, no packed touch) both use it via a shared `_findDynamic(tag)`; both return `false` for an unknown tag. New exported function `stepSectorAnims(world, dtSec)`: consumes `delay` first, but when the remaining delay is SMALLER than the step it spends the leftover inside the same call and uses the rest of `dtSec` to move `t` (not "wait one more whole step") - needed because `0.4 - 24*(1/60)` isn't exactly `0` in float, so a naive "consume-then-continue" loses a full step. Clamp-to-target has a `1e-9` epsilon (mirrors `capsule.js`'s `SKIN`) so ~90 unequal float increments still land exactly on `target` at the designed step count, not one call later. New `_restoreDynamics(placed, tag, d)` (used by both `World.load` and `deserialize`) jumps to `d.t` AND restores `d.target`/`d.delay` (defaulting to `t`/`0` for older saves without those fields), so a save mid-open resumes and finishes identically to an uninterrupted run (tested).
- `packed.js`: `updateAnimatedSector` no longer allocates a fresh relief/`Uint8Array` every animation step - `packed._reliefFloorRise`/`_reliefCeilDrop` scratch arrays (added to `packLevel`'s own return, lazily created on an older packed layout) are reused and the packed `relief` array is written into in place.
- `game/js/quest/lever.js` (new): `lever.pull` - `ctx.entity.play('pull')`, `world.animateSectorTo(def.target.tag, 1, {delay: 0.4})`, `world.state['tower.lever.pulled'] = true`, returns `true`. No literal coordinates. `game/js/quest/index.js` now registers `lever.pull` to this real function (the other 4 names stay stubs).
- `game/js/main.js`: one added line, `stepSectorAnims(engine.world, dt)`, first in the `mode === 'world'` block, before `integrate` (7.4 fixed-step order item 1). `design/levels/world_m1.js`: removed the redundant `'tower.grate.open'` state key (a comment explains `structure.dynamics` is the only truth).
- **Tests:** `engine/world/sectorAnim.test.js` (new, 85 assertions) covers every item in tech note 6: unknown-tag `false`, the exact ease-in-out curve step by step (24 delay + 90 open, epsilon-safe), the `isSectorPassable` flip at 1.70 m clearance, in-place `packed.geom/flags/relief` matching a fresh `packLevel`, `planFrameUpdate`'s exact `[y0-1, y1+1]` dirty range and its `dirtyY0/Y1` reset (also confirms `structVersion` is untouched - no atlas rebuild), an idle-loop allocation smoke check (byte-exact only under `--expose-gc`), and the save-mid-open -> load -> finish determinism check (byte-identical `ceilH` and a fresh-`packLevel` match on the resumed run). `game/js/quest/tower.test.js` gained a `lever.pull` behaviour section (calling `world.fireInteraction` directly, since `updateInteraction`/US-012 doesn't exist yet - the "no second target" AC is `once`-flag handling that lives there, not tested here) and a serialize/deserialize determinism check.
- **Verified:** `node tools/check-deps.mjs` (100 files OK), `node tools/check-deps.test.mjs` (8/8), `node engine/world/sectorAnim.test.js` (85/85), `node game/js/quest/tower.test.js` (63/63), `engine/world/{world,serialize,packed,terrain}.test.js`, `engine/physics/{physics,jump}.test.js`, `engine/entities/eyeFeel.test.js`, `engine/core/playerLook.test.js`, `engine/render/gpu/WorldTextures.test.js` - all green. Not run in a browser (no visible art yet: US-011 lever/grate props are still `design` status, so there is nothing new to see on screen - `EntityHandle.play` still throws per its US-011 stub, meaning `lever.pull` cannot be fired end-to-end from the real interaction system until US-011 AND US-012 land; this story's own scope is the engine mechanism + behaviour body, per the tech notes' "the prompt path needs US-012 merged").
- **Known limitations / follow-ups for architect review:** (1) `animateSector`/`animateSectorTo` now return `boolean` (previously `animateSector` returned `undefined`) - a compatible, additive change, no caller checked the old return value. (2) The `once`/used-flag prompt-hiding half of AC "one-way: after use, the lever shows no prompt" is out of scope here (needs US-012's `updateInteraction`); flagging so it isn't mistaken for missed scope. (3) `world_m1.js`'s tower structure still has no `dynamics` entry of its own (the grate starts at its authored closed `ceilH`, `t=0`, no `structure.dynamics.grate` key at all until first pulled) - confirmed this matches "closed/authored ceilH" being the implicit rest state, not requiring a seeded `dynamics` entry.

**Architect review #1 (2026-09-23): ARCH OK -> `po-review`.** Tests run: sectorAnim 85/85, tower 63/63, physics/jump green; check-deps clean for these files (the 4 findings are US-039's `tools/bench-voxel.mjs`). Verified: `stepSectorAnims` allocates nothing per step (in-place relief via `packed._reliefFloorRise/_reliefCeilDrop`, `for..in`, event payloads only on moving steps, as before); `_findDynamic`/`animateSector` allocate but are interaction/load-time only; delay residual and the 1e-9 clamp are deterministic and land `t` exactly on `target` at step 24+90; `dynamics[tag] = {t, target, delay}` round-trips through `structuredClone`/JSON with old saves defaulting to rest; GPU visibility needs nothing new (`updateAnimatedSector` bumps `packed.version` + dirty rows, `planFrameUpdate` uploads those rows, `structVersion` untouched, tested). Boolean return on `animateSector` accepted. Known limits (fine for M1, recorded in 7.4): a tag resolves to the first structure that has it, so two placed copies of the same level share one grate; `lever.pull` end to end waits on US-011 (`EntityHandle.play`) and US-012 (`once` prompt hiding).

Acceptance criteria – Programmer (D-011 additions; apply together with the ACs below):
- [ ] **Map card, first show:** 0.5 s after the title card fades out, the map card fades in (0.3 s, ramp-step fade rule) centred over the 3D view, with the scene behind it dimmed to bg x 0.35. While it is shown, movement and look input are ignored; the world keeps animating (the burner flickers). After a minimum of 1.0 s on screen, any key or mouse click dismisses it (0.3 s fade out). The dismissing key is consumed, so it does not also move, jump or interact. The `WASD move - Mouse look` hint starts after the dismissal, not after the title.
- [ ] **`M` key:** from the first dismissal until the end trigger, `M` opens the same card (same fade and dim), and `M`, Esc or any other key closes it. It cannot open during the wake/title, while the end sequence runs, or on the end screen. Opening it does not release pointer lock and does not pause physics. It is a toggle with no timeout.

Take what light you can.` shows once on entering `hintBurner` while the lamp is not taken. `Climb. You cannot see the signal from down here.` shows once on entering `hintClimb`. Standard hint rules apply (bottom-left, fade 0.3 s, 8 s timeout, at most one hint on screen, queued in order).

**Tech notes (architect, 2026-09-24).** Architect tech notes: docs/architecture.md §7.6 (normative).
- Engine (generic, no strings): `engine/ui/richText.js` (per-glyph colours, typewriter count, fade), `engine/ui/panel.js` (model -> PanelArt, open/close/step fade, UI-grid layout, dim rects), `engine/ui/sceneDim.js` (`fb.sceneDim`, CPU twin + pass-F uniforms; map dim 0.35, plate 0.18, hint plate 0.35), `Input`: `Mouse0`, `anyPressed()`, `consumePressed()`, `GAME_KEYS` += `KeyM`/`KeyN`.
- Game: `quest/wake.js`, `quest/mapCard.js` (first show, `minShowSec`, `M` gating), `quest/hints.js` (FIFO controller; `hint.show` via `registerQuestBehaviours({hints})`, returns `true`), `ui/titleCard.js`, `ui/endCard.js` rewritten on `uiStyle.endText` (per-line colours via RichLine). `M` = card, `N` reserved for mute (US-020).
- State in `world_m1.js` `state` (restart resets): `quest.wakeT`, `ui.mapCard.shown/dismissed/opened`, `hints.shown`, `hints.done`, `hints.walkT`, `hints.chartT`. Runtime queue/panel state reset only in `'world:loaded'`.
- Copy `levelPatch.towerHints` into `design/levels/tower.js` `triggers[]` by hand after US-011 lands. UI stays 1 glyph = 1 scene cell; positions scale by centre from the 160x60 UI grid (the `uiScale 'layer'` mode is out of scope).

**Programmer notes (2026-09-24) -> `arch-review`.** Implemented per architecture.md §7.6.
- Engine (`engine/ui/`, exported via `engine/index.js`): `richText.js` (`hexToRgb`, `compileRichLine`, `drawRichLine` - fade + typewriter `count`), `panel.js` (`buildPanelArt`, `Panel` class with `open/close/step/layout/pushDim`, `drawPanel`), `sceneDim.js` (`createSceneDim/resetSceneDim/pushDimRect/applySceneDim`, CPU only - see limitation below). `engine/core/input.js`: `Mouse0` (mousedown/up), `anyPressed()`, `consumePressed()` (removes from `pressed` and ignores via `isDown` until the code's own keyup, immune to OS auto-repeat), `GAME_KEYS` += `KeyM`/`KeyN`.
- Game: `quest/wake.js` (`wakeFrame` pure timeline function + `drawEyelid`), `quest/mapCard.js` (`initMapCard/stepMapCard/isMapOpen/getMapPanel` - first-show delay off `quest.wakeT`, `minShowSec` gate, `M` toggle, consumed-key dismiss), `quest/hints.js` (FIFO controller, `hint.show` registered in `quest/index.js` forwarding to `hints.request` via `ctx.engine.assets.uiStyle`), `ui/titleCard.js`, `ui/endCard.js` (rewritten: `computeEndCardState`/`drawEndCard` now read every line/colour/cursor/timing from `uiStyle.endText`, per-line `RichLine`s via `richText.js`). `main.js` wires the wake clock, map card, hints, dim, eyelid, title card and rewritten end-card draw into `update()`/`render()`, gated by `questUiActive` (true only when the loaded world declares `quest.wakeT` in its initial state - `world_m1`; `?level=<name>` adhoc worlds are unaffected).
- `design/levels/tower.js` `triggers[]`: `hintBurner`/`hintClimb` copied by hand from `title.js` `levelPatch.towerHints`, after `hintJump`, as the designer specified. `design/levels/world_m1.js` `state`: added `quest.wakeT`, `ui.mapCard.dismissed`, `hints.done`, `hints.walkT`, `hints.chartT` (all restart-reset for free via `deserialize(initialState)`; runtime panel/hint-queue state is rebuilt in the `'world:loaded'` handler, which fires on both first load and `R`).
- Tests (all passing): `engine/ui/richText.test.js` (13), `panel.test.js` (23), `sceneDim.test.js` (7), `game/js/quest/wake.test.js` (17), `hints.test.js` (21, incl. FIFO order, `skipIfState` permanence, done-before-shown, the 20 s chart timer + M-cancels-it, 8 s timeout, restart-equivalent reset), `mapCard.test.js` (13, incl. gating during wake/title, `minShowSec`, consumed-key dismiss, `M` reopen with no `minShowSec`, blocked while ending), `game/js/ui/endCard.test.js` (14, per-line colours/alt text/cursor from `uiStyle.endText`), plus a `tower.test.js` addition (4 checks: both zones present, spawn outside `hintBurner`, lamp inside, circles disjoint - confirms the designer's own geometry claims against the real placed data). All existing Node suites (physics, jump, eyeFeel, playerLook, fade, behaviours, boulder, restart, tower) and `node tools/check-deps.mjs`/`check-deps.test.mjs` still pass.
- **Follow-up pass (2026-09-24, same day) - GPU dim, shine, browser verification:**
  1. **GPU scene-dim wired.** `sprites.frag.js` gains `uDimAll`/`uDimCount`/`uDimRect[4]`/`uDimMul[4]` uniforms, applied right after the fade block on the same non-mask cells (`k = min(uDimAll, mul of every rect containing the cell)`, glyph unchanged - mirrors `applySceneDim` exactly). `spritesPass.js` gains `setSceneDim(dim)` (copies a `SceneDim` into plain per-uniform fields, no texture needed) and uploads them every `run()`. `main.js` computes `sceneDim` once per frame (as before) and now also calls `sprites.pass.setSceneDim(sceneDim)` before `rt.present()`, resetting it to identity for both the ending screen and the informational n=2 gpucompare row. Added `sprites.test.js` checks (uniform declarations, dim-after-fade ordering, identity guard, `setSceneDim` data copy) and a new `?gpucompare=1` pose "card open (sceneDim 0.35 + plate 0.18)" - **PASS** (see below).
  2. **`title.shine` implemented.** `titleCard.js` draws the title logo through its own loop (`drawTitleGlyphs`, a copy of `drawPanel`'s frame/fade logic) instead of the generic `drawPanel`, so it can add the diagonal sweep during `titleState === 'hold'` only: a cell whose UNFADED glyph is `#` inside `0 <= x - slope*y - pos < width` (pos sweeping `-16..w+16` once per `shine.period`) is lerped toward `shine.color` by `shine.amount`. Subtitle still draws via the generic `drawPanel` (no shine). New `game/js/ui/titleCard.test.js` (6 checks: gating, plain colour outside the hold, shine tint inside the band, no tint outside the band or on non-`#` glyphs).
  3. **Verified live in the browser** (own server, port 9001, stopped after - `netstat`/`taskkill` confirmed only that PID). The Browser pane throttles `requestAnimationFrame` to a full stop while hidden between tool calls (confirmed via `loop._lastTime` freezing and `document.hidden`/pane-hidden state) - real time-based animation could not be watched continuously, so verification drove the fixed-step loop directly (`loop._tick(...)` in a script loop) and read back either `world.state` or the actual GPU-composited frame (`rt.readbackPresent()` - `rt.cells` itself is GPU-path scratch, only meaningful together with the per-frame mask, so it was not used for "what's on screen" checks). Confirmed: wake black->blink->rise->KESTREL title+"SOMEONE IS CALLING" subtitle (screenshot, brass/copper gradient visible) -> map card auto-opens with dim (screenshot: full chart text incl. torn edges, colours, "- any key -"); dismiss key is consumed and arms `hints.chartT`; `hints.shown` gets `move`/`capture` correctly (FIFO, one at a time, readback confirmed the exact hint string+colour in the composited frame); `M` reopens/closes (`ui.mapCard.opened` true, card content visible again); `hintBurner`/`hintClimb` zone triggers fire exactly once via real `updateTriggers` + `hint.show` (teleported the real player entity across the circle boundary rather than walking, to keep the session short); end card (`quest.endT` fast-forwarded to the restart-ready screen) readback confirmed all 4 lines' exact text AND colour (`signal`/`someone` = uiText `[232,226,208]`, `[R]` = gold `[255,210,74]`) plus the blinking cursor; `R` (via the real `computeEndCardState`/`engine.setWorld(deserialize(initialState))` path, endT fast-forwarded first to reach the restart-gated screen without a full walk) reset `quest.wakeT`, `ui.mapCard.shown/dismissed/opened`, `hints.done`, `hints.walkT`, `hints.chartT` and `quest.endT` all back to their initial-state defaults (`hints.shown` correctly got a fresh `capture` entry post-restart, since pointer lock - real browser state, not `world.state` - was still unlocked in the new session). `?gpucompare=1`: **13/14 PASS**, the only FAIL is the pre-existing, known `world_m1: boulder mid-roll` (BUG-LIGHT-001, unrelated to this story); the new "card open" dim pose is among the 13 PASSes. No console errors on either page (`read_console_messages`, `onlyErrors: true`, both pages: none). 320x120/240x90 side-by-side legibility comparison and a real mouse-driven playthrough were not done (time budget) - reasonable next step for the tester.

**ARCH OK (architect, 2026-09-24, review of 69e4a99 + a06f85c) -> `po-review`.** Engine `richText`/`panel`/`sceneDim` are generic (no strings/keys/`ASSETS`), `Input.anyPressed/consumePressed/Mouse0` + `KeyM/KeyN` as 7.6 item 5, GPU `uDim*` mirrors `applySceneDim` (same non-mask cells, after fade, identity skipped), no per-frame allocation in the engine hot paths, check-deps clean, all new suites pass. Small fixes applied by the architect (uncommitted, tests green):
1. `mapCard.js`: removed the module flag `firstShowActive`; first show is now derived from `world.state` (`shown && !dismissed`, card reopens if restored closed), so no card flag lives outside `world.state` (7.6 "Do not"). `mapCard.test.js` M-before-dismissal case updated to this rule.
2. `main.js`: `stepHints` signals are a reused `hintSignals` object (was one literal per step); `hints.js request()` checks queue/current before `defFor` (no closure per step while a hint waits).
3. `titleCard.js`: the subtitle panel view is a reused module object (was one per frame).
4. `?gpucompare=1` "card open" plate rect is now asymmetric `[10,4,70,26]` (the old centred rect could not catch a GPU x/y flip). Tester: re-run `?gpucompare=1`, the card-open pose must PASS.
Non-blocking: `computeEndCardState`/`drawEndCard` still allocate per frame (out object, `hexToRgb`, cache key string) - only on the end screen, pre-existing shape; clean up when US-021 reuses it.

- **Tester notes:**
  1. Do a full live, mouse-driven playthrough (real time, no `_tick` driving): black 1.0 s, blink with a half-close, lying pose rising over 1.2 s (input ignored until the rise ends), `KESTREL` + `SOMEONE IS CALLING` title (1 s / 3 s with shine / 1 s), then the map card after 0.5 s with the scene dimmed.
  2. Map card: it can't be dismissed before 1.0 s. Then any key or click closes it, and that key must not move, jump or interact. The `WASD move - Mouse look` hint only starts after the dismissal. `M` reopens and closes the card (`M`, Esc or another key), and physics keeps running. `M` must not open the card during the wake, the title, the end sequence or the end screen.
  3. Hints: bottom-left, one at a time, queued in order, 0.3 s fade, 8 s timeout. Check `Shift run` (10 s of walking), `[Space] Jump` (`hintJump`, never if you already jumped), `Click to capture mouse`, the chart hint at +20 s (never if `M` was already pressed), `hintBurner` (only while the lamp is not taken) and `hintClimb`.
  4. End card: check the text and the per-line colours (incl. the woken variant), the gold `[R]` and the blinking cursor. Then press `R`: the wake, the map card and all hints must replay from the start.
  5. Re-run `?gpucompare=1` including the new off-centre "card open" pose, which must PASS. `world_m1: boulder mid-roll` failing is the known BUG-LIGHT-001 and is not a US-015 fail.
  6. Legibility of the title, map card, hints and end card at 160x60 and 240x90 (and a look at 320x120). Check that no console errors appear, and re-run all the Node suites.

**Tester FAIL (2026-09-24, docs/test-reports/US-015.md) -> back to `dev`.** All Node suites pass (check-deps + 19 targeted UI/quest/gpu suites). Live verification (own server, port 9003, stopped after - only that PID) confirmed the full state machine live: wake timeline, title (shine/gradient visible), map card open/1.0s-gate/consumed-dismiss/`M` toggle with physics still running, all 6 hints incl. `hintBurner`/`hintClimb`/`hintJump` firing via real trigger zones, restart resetting every new flag, end-card per-line colours matching exactly via GPU pixel readback (`[232,226,208]` uiText, `[255,210,74]` gold `[R]`). `?gpucompare=1` 13/14 PASS, card-open pose passes, boulder mid-roll matches the accepted BUG-LIGHT-001 pattern. **BUG-1 [major]:** at `160x60`, `drawPauseOverlay` (`game/js/ui/pauseOverlay.js`, unconditional call in `game/js/main.js:670` whenever pointer isn't locked) has no exclusion/dim-rect logic against the map-card panel, so its fixed-row `Click to resume` plate lands on the same row as the card's `Crown print:` line and both are unreadable - reachable in real play (wake runs ~8s before the card opens; a player may not have clicked yet). 240x90/320x120 don't collide (more vertical room), same root cause. No console errors. Environment limitations noted in the report: pointer lock never engages and real-time rAF stalls between tool calls in this browser tooling (matches the programmer's own prior note), so verification used manual fixed-step ticking + GPU readback + player-entity teleport into hint zones rather than literal mouse/WASD play; `Shift run` relied on existing `hints.test.js` coverage instead of a live check. Next: programmer fixes BUG-1 (skip/relocate the pause overlay while a panel is open, or give it a `pushDim`-style exclusion), re-check 160x60 legibility, then back to `po-review` -> tester.

### US-016 Far overworld view through the breach  [Priority: P0] [Status: done]
As a player, I want to see a vast, colorful landscape with the signal shining on a distant tower, and my city's lights behind me, so that I feel the world is huge and I want to go out there.

**D-011 reskin (PO, 2026-09-23):** terrain, recipe, position (800 m, azimuth 255) and the engine ACs are unchanged. What changes:
- The far tower becomes the **signal tower**. Its body stays dark and unlit (fog cap 0.40), and it gets a small **static aether-teal emissive light** at the top (1-2 cells at 240x90, never smaller than 1 cell). It is emissive, so it ignores lighting, but it takes far fog capped so it stays clearly visible. The SOS pulse (D-013 timing) is a later P2 story, not M1.
- **Ferrum's lights** are added on the opposite horizon (bearing about 75 degrees, behind the player at the breach and visible from the summit walkway over the interior wall top; the designer sets the exact bearing). They are a horizon billboard beyond the terrain far limit: a low band of warm amber emissive pinpoints (`. ' *`) with a wall-and-tiers silhouette hint, about 24-40 cells wide and 2-4 rows at 240x90. They draw only over sky/far cells, never over structure or near terrain. They are not point lights.
- The torn *Kestrel* envelope is snagged on the rocks below the breach (a US-011 wreckage prop placed in `world_m1.js`), visible when looking down from the breach.
- "Unchanged by US-022" now reads: waking the relay does not change the signal tower or Ferrum in M1.

**Tech notes addendum (architect, 2026-09-24): D-011 art + D-017.** Architect tech notes: docs/architecture.md §14.4 (items 1-10) + items 11-14 (addendum; they override the older text below where it differs).

**Programmer notes (2026-09-24): steps 4-6 (build order continuation of 34f299a/54ee5e4). Status stays `dev` - step 4 is not fully green, see below.**
- **Step 5 done, browser-verified:** per-sprite fog colour (SPR now 5 RGBA32F texels, `T4 = fogR,fogG,fogB,0`; `engine/render/sprites.js` `project()`/`drawSprites`, `engine/render/gpu/glsl/sprites.frag.js`, `spritesPass.js` - dropped the old single `uFogColor` uniform). A `fogModel:'far'` billboard (the signal tower) now fogs toward `mix(fog.far.color, fog.far.colorFar, fogFactor(depth,'far'))` - the same gradient `shadeTerrainFar` paints the terrain with - instead of the flat `fog.interior` approximation item 7 flagged. `world.horizon[]` now actually draws: `projectHorizon` (`engine/render/sprites.js`) places Ferrum by angle (bearing/elev/angular, tier by projected rows same rule as an ordinary sprite, fixed per-entry fog/fogColor) as an extra sprite-pool slot at `HORIZON_DEPTH = 1e6`, so the existing depth test alone keeps it off every terrain/structure cell (only sky). One pool/shader, both paths. New Node tests: `engine/render/gpu/sprites.test.js` (projectHorizon column/row at yaw==bearing, off-screen >=90 deg skip, tier switch, never-over-finite-depth, far-fogModel gradient vs the old flat colour), `engine/world/world.test.js` (horizon load + every validation error), `engine/world/serialize.test.js` (round-trip). All green, plus every pre-existing suite in CLAUDE.md + this story's own list.
- **Step 6 done, real-GPU numbers (owner's machine, ANGLE D3D11):** `EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2`'s `TIME_ELAPSED_EXT` can't be nested (only one active query per context), so a second whole-pass `GpuTimer` around `_passTerrain()` always read n/a; `TIMESTAMP_EXT` query-counters (which don't need to be "active") were tried next but this GPU always returned the identical clock value for both ends (delta 0) - a driver gap, not a logic bug. Fell back to a CPU `performance.now()` bracket around the terrain draw call only (`GpuCellPipeline._terrainTsBegin/End/_pollTerrainTs`, `stats.terrainGpuMs/P50/P95`, HUD `terrain Xms`), the same honest-labelling as the file's existing `uploadMs`/`drawMs` (submit time, not a true GPU-side query). At 320x120 from the breach (1486.5, 1025.0, eye 7.6) looking out (yaw 270): **terrain P50 ~0.1 ms, P95 ~0.2-1.6 ms (budget: <= 1.0 ms p95)** - comfortably inside budget most frames, with one early startup-transient frame at 1.6 ms. **Total GPU (`gpuMs`, cast+terrain+resolve+deriv+light+shade+edge+sprite) read ~8.5 ms**, over the US-018 "<= 4 ms" budget line - but this reading is unreliable in the headless automation harness used here (the whole-frame `gpuMsP50` visibly froze across several seconds of real rendering, most likely the tab losing focus/pausing under automated input rather than a real per-frame cost; the owner's own **real-mouse** check is needed for a trustworthy total-GPU number, same as the existing "owner to-do: real-GPU 8-light check" note above).
- **Step 4 NOT fully green - ASK ARCHITECT.** Added the two new `?gpucompare=1` poses (`game/js/main.js` `runs[]`, plus `worldM1.terrain.bakeFarSync()` right after `loadCompareWorld` so terrain is actually marched on this page - it was never baked here before, so no world_m1 pose had exercised kind-7 parity until now): `world_m1: summit east (relay plinth, yaw 87.6)` (1489.0, 1025.0, eye 8.2) and `world_m1: breach looking back east (yaw 87.6)` (1486.5, 1025.0, eye 7.6). All 14 pre-existing poses still PASS (both `?gpucompare=1` and `?gpucompare=shade`, ALL PASS unaffected). The two new poses **FAIL**: `summit east` kind 99.88%/glyph 99.80%, depthViol 40/uvViol 40 of 3946 matched kind-7 cells, fgMax 74; `breach looking back` glyph 99.85%, holes 4, depthViol 26/uvViol 26 of 3895, fgMax 170/bgMax 210. Diagnosed the worst offenders directly (temporary instrumentation, removed): close-range terrain hits (t ~35-57 m, not far/band-edge cells) where GPU and JS land on DIFFERENT march steps entirely (several metres apart, sometimes a different terrain feature - not float32 "noise"). Both new poses share `yawDeg 87.6`, which is nearly parallel to the tower's long (x) footprint edge - the ray's y-component (`dirY = -cos(87.6deg) approx -0.042`) is small, so the structure-bbox `slabTerrain`/`slab2D` 2D slab test's y-axis division `(y1-ey)/dy` is by a near-zero `dy`: a tiny GPU-float32-vs-JS-float64 difference in the computed ray direction is amplified into a metres-scale difference in the skip interval's exit `t`, which can flip whether a given march step counts as "inside the tower's bbox, no sample" on one path but not the other, shifting the eventual hit onto a different terrain feature. This is a genuine numerical-stability property of the grazing-ray slab test at these specific bearings (not a spec deviation, and not something a tolerance bump alone fixes safely without possibly masking a real bug elsewhere) - **ASK ARCHITECT**: is a grazing-ray slab-test robustness fix (e.g. widen/round the skip interval, or a different structure-occlusion test) in scope, or should the two new poses use different (less grazing) bearings, or does 14.4 item 9's "kind-7 cell count within 1%" wording already intend a softer, count-based comparison here instead of the strict per-cell `compareGeometry` this harness reuses for every other pose? Left `dev`, not `arch-review`, pending that answer.

**Architect answer + ARCH review (2026-09-24) -> `ARCH CHANGES`, stays `dev`.** The slab test is not the cause (a float32 emulation of the GLSL march matches the float64 oracle on every cell of both poses, and the bbox exit is at t <= ~20 m, far before the 35-57 m hits). Found by per-cell readback: two bugs in the **JS oracle** `castTerrain`, both fixed by the architect in `engine/render/terrainCaster.js` (JS only; the GLSL was already right): (a) the row sample was `row + 0.5`, but the DDA/GLSL ray is `cy + oy` with `oy = 0` at n = 1, i.e. `row` (a half-row error = 1-5 % depth on close terrain); (b) it only marched rows inside the open span, but the sector pass can paint sky rows below the horizon (a low far ceiling) and close them off the span, while pass A2 marches every `kind == 0` sub-ray; the oracle now marches every cell with `depth == +Inf` (item 6 updated in architecture.md 14.4). Result: `?gpucompare=1` **16/16 ALL PASS** (both new poses kind/glyph 100 %, depthViol/uvViol 0), `?gpucompare=shade` ALL PASS, terrainCaster/TerrainTextures/sprites/terrain/world/gpuCompare Node suites and check-deps green. No pose change and no tolerance change needed.
Required changes (programmer):
1. **Item 9 poses missing.** Add `breach` (1486.5, 1025.0, eye 7.6, yaw 270, pitch 0), `breachDown` (pitch -30), `parapetSky` (yaw 255, pitch +20) and `signal tower` (yaw 255, pitch +2) to `?gpucompare=1` - this is the story's main view and had no parity pose. Architect probe with these four: the first three PASS at 100 %; `signal tower` FAILs on **uvViol 1** (1 far cell, a bisection knife edge, depth within 1 %).
2. **Terrain uv tolerance (item 9).** In `compareGeometry`, kind-7 cells use the item 9 rule for u/v too: `|du|,|dv| <= 0.01 * max(1, depth)` (the 5-step bisection resolves t to ~0.1 % of t, so the sector `1e-3` tolerance is at the resolution limit for terrain). Sector kinds keep `1e-3`. Add a `gpuCompare.test.js` case. Then target 20/20 ALL PASS.
3. **`?terrain=0` (item 8) is not implemented.** Parse it in `game/js/main.js` and skip terrain on both paths (GPU: `_terrainActiveThisFrame = false`; JS: don't call `castTerrain`). Needed for item 4 below.
4. **Terrain timing is not a GPU number.** `terrainGpuMs*` is a CPU `performance.now()` bracket around a draw call (submit time, ~0 regardless of shader cost). Rename to `terrainSubmitMs*` (HUD `terrain cpu`) and measure the pass A2 GPU cost as the A/B delta of the whole-frame `gpuMsP50/P95` at the breach pose, `?terrain=1` vs `?terrain=0`, at 320x120. Record both totals and the delta in this story. The "total ~8.5 ms" reading: **owner to verify on real hardware with the real mouse**; if `?terrain=0` alone is already > 4 ms it is US-018's, not US-016's; if the delta is > 1.0 ms p95, report it here (ASK ARCHITECT) before tuning.
5. Minor: `sunFromWorld` allocates a new object per frame on the GPU path - give it an optional `out` param (module scratch in the pipeline). `_uploadTerrainUniforms` hard-codes `uFarMap` x0/y0 = 0 - read `terrain._farGridDraw.x0/y0` (same value today, one source of truth).
6. Add a `terrainCaster.test.js` regression for the architect fix: (a) a cell outside the open span with `depth == +Inf` is marched, a cell with finite depth is not; (b) the written depth equals `marchTerrainRay` with `slope = (horizonRow - row) / planeDistY`.
Reviewed and OK: pass A2 placement/ping-pong and bind-time set choice, march GLSL = oracle (literal twin), deriv skip for kind 7, shade kind-7 branch (b via GA.w), texture upload keyed on farVersion + world, per-sprite fog colour T4 on both paths, `projectHorizon` + `HORIZON_DEPTH`, no engine->game imports.

**Tech notes (architect, 2026-09-23)** - normative detail in `docs/architecture.md` 14.4 (march, textures, shading, oracle, far tower, parity). Blocked on US-030 `done` (needs pass A/B, `SDEPTH`, `uStruct*`, the sprite pass and the shared `ray()` GLSL).

Summary of the design: terrain is pass **A2 `terrain`** at sub-sample resolution between the DDA (A) and resolve (B), ping-ponging the sub-sample G-buffer (set 1 -> set 2); each sub-ray marches the baked 8 m heightfield (`FARH` R32F, manual bilinear) up to `min(sectorDepth, 1500)`, skipping structure bboxes, and writes `kind 7 / mat = type / planeId = PLANEID_TERRAIN / aoD = b`; `shadeTerrainFar` (GLSL + JS twin) does bands, colour tiers, glint and far fog; sky is untouched (`skyHorizon == fogFar`). The far tower is a `billboard` entity in `world_m1.js` through the sprite pass with `minCells 3x4`, `unlit`, `fogModel 'far'`, `fogMax 0.40`. Deviation from the AC wording, for the PO: lighting `b` is **not** baked per cell - normals come from the height grid at the hit and the sun stays a uniform (D-007 wording, US-007 compatible); same picture, no re-bake when the sun changes.

Files (engine): `engine/render/terrainCaster.js` (real `castTerrain` + pure `marchTerrainRay`), `engine/render/terrainShade.js` (`shadeTerrainFar` JS twin), `engine/render/GBuffer.js` (`KIND_TERRAIN = 7`, `PLANEID_TERRAIN`), `engine/render/detailShade.js` (kind-7 branch -> `shadeTerrainFar`; `computeDerivatives` skips kind 7), `engine/render/edgePass.js` (kind 7 passthrough), `engine/render/sectorCaster.js` (`fillSky` paints kind 0 only - verify), `engine/render/sprites.js` (`projectSprite`: `minCells`, `detailRows`, `unlit`, `fogModel`/`fogMax` -> `fogF`, `fogRGB`), `engine/world/Terrain.js` (`farHDraw`, `farMaxH` built at bake end, `farVersion` bump), `engine/world/World.js` (`billboard` entity type -> sprite pool), `engine/render/compositor.js` (`castTerrain(fb, terrain, cam, {sun, timeSec, step...})`, sun derived from the level's `sun` until US-007), `engine/render/gpu/TerrainTextures.js` (+ `.test.js`), `engine/render/gpu/glsl/terrain.frag.js`, `glsl/common.js` (shared `ray()`), `glsl/shade.frag.js` / `deriv.frag.js` / `edge.frag.js` (kind-7 branches), `engine/render/gpu/GpuCellPipeline.js` (pass A2, set-2 textures, terrain uniforms, one-shot `FARH`/`FARTYPE`/`TLOOK` upload keyed on `farVersion`), `engine/render/gpu/gpuCompare.js` (terrain counters, band/tier exclusions), `engine/index.js` (export `castTerrain`, `marchTerrainRay`, `shadeTerrainFar`, `KIND_TERRAIN`). Content: `design/models/far_tower.js` (designer: `min` 3x4 + `detail` 5x8 frames, moved from the recipe), `design/levels/world_m1.js` (`farTower` billboard entity), `design/levels/overworld_far.js` (`farTower.model` reference), `game/js/main.js` (`?terrain=0`). Tools: `tools/bench-poses.js` (breach, breachDown, parapetSky, ringLook).

Build order: (1) `Terrain.farHDraw` + `TerrainTextures` packer + tests; (2) JS `marchTerrainRay` + `castTerrain` + `shadeTerrainFar` on the CPU path with the breach poses (visual sanity in `?gpu=0`); (3) GLSL pass A2 + ping-pong + kind-7 branches; (4) `?gpucompare=1` terrain poses to threshold; (5) far-tower billboard (designer model first); (6) bench at 320x120.

Test plan:
- Node `engine/render/terrainCaster.test.js` (stub recipe with analytic `heightAt`; no `design/` import): flat terrain at `H` -> `tHit = (eyeH - H) / -slope` within 0.5 %; a ray with `slope >= 0` and `eyeH >= farMaxH` takes 0 steps; a ray whose hit lies inside a structure bbox skips it and hits beyond (or misses); nothing is written where `depth` is finite; out-of-map -> no write; `castTerrain` on a frame with all spans closed does nothing; horizon seam: a level whose floor equals the terrain height around it produces contiguous rows (last sector-floor row + 1 == first terrain row) at pitch -35, 0, +35; zero allocations over 100 frames (the `compositor.test.js` pattern); CPU-fallback step options still hit within 2 % of the fine schedule on flat ground.
- Node `terrainShade.test.js`: tier/band/fog/glint rules on fixed inputs (b = 0.2/0.6/0.9, t = 100/400/1200/1450 -> space), hash determinism (same inputs -> same glyph), all outputs bytes 0..255.
- Node `TerrainTextures.test.js`: sizes, `farHDraw = farH + canopy` only on forest texels, `TLOOK` code packing round-trip, asserts on > 3 glyphs / unknown palette key.
- Node `glsl.test.js`: terrain source contains the JS-injected constants (`MAX_TERRAIN_STEPS`, `STEP_MIN`, `STEP_K`, `KIND_TERRAIN`) and no literal band/fog numbers.
- Browser: `?gpucompare=1` over the 4 breach poses meets 14.4 item 9; `?flicker=1` on `breach` shows the GPU n = 2 terrain share <= the JS 1-ray share; `?bench=1` at 320x120 looking out of the breach: GPU total <= 4 ms p95 with terrain <= 1.0 ms, JS <= 2 ms; `?gpu=0` at 160x60: JS <= 8 ms total; `?terrain=0` A/B; pitch sweep -35..+35 shows no horizon step at the breach; the far tower is >= 3x4 cells at 320x120 and dark at fog 0.40. Designer re-checks `overworld.html` against the game at 320x120.

**Programmer notes (partial pass, 2026-09-24) -> stays `dev`.** This story is large (the architect's own 14.4 build order has 6 steps); this pass completes the DATA/JS layer (build order items 1-2) plus the sprite-side engine needs (D-011 addendum items 1/3), all Node-tested and wired into the live pipeline. It does **not** complete the GPU GLSL march (build order item 3+) or the horizon billboard draw - see "Not done" below. **ASK ARCHITECT:** is it useful to review/merge this data+JS slice now (it is independently correct and tested, and unblocks the `?gpu=0`/oracle path), with the GLSL pass + horizon draw as a follow-up pass on the same story - or hold the whole story in `dev` until one pass covers both? Recommend the former given the size, but deferring to the architect's call per CLAUDE.md's "engine story -> architect review" workflow.

Done, tested (all Node suites below are new/updated and green; `check-deps` and the CLAUDE.md suite list all pass):
1. **`engine/world/Terrain.js`:** `farHDraw` (= `farH` + `recipe.recipe.forest.canopy` on forest texels only) and `farMaxH`, built at the end of both `bakeFarSync`/`bakeFarStep` (`_finishBake`); a `_farGridDraw` grid view over `farHDraw` for `util.gridHeight` sampling (physics-facing `farH`/`_farGrid` untouched).
2. **`engine/render/GBuffer.js`:** `KIND_TERRAIN = 7`, `PLANEID_TERRAIN = -1` (the "one constant plane" the item 4 spec calls for - never collides with a real `packPlaneId` structSeq).
3. **`engine/render/gpu/TerrainTextures.js` (new, Node-only, no `gl`):** `packTerrainTextures(terrain, palette)` builds the `FARH`/`FARTYPE`/`TLOOK` upload-ready arrays per item 3 (colours linear 0..1, albedo/glint flag, near/mid/far glyph codes packed 8 bits each). Not yet consumed by `GpuCellPipeline.js` (no GPU upload wired - see "Not done").
4. **`engine/render/terrainShade.js` (new):** `shadeTerrainFar` - the pure JS function per item 5 (colour tier, band-by-distance glyph pick, water glint, fog blend, heavy-fog space-out), with a bit-exact `hashFastU`/`hashFast01` twin of `glsl/common.js`'s `HASH_FAST`. One simplification flagged: the water-glint tint colour reuses the type's own TLOOK "light" colour (`colors[2]`, already palette-driven and literally named e.g. `riverLight`) instead of a separate `uRiverLight` uniform/field - same data, one fewer moving part.
5. **`engine/render/terrainCaster.js` (rewritten; was an 11-line no-op stub):** real `castTerrain(fb, terrain, cam, world, opts)` + pure `marchTerrainRay` per item 4's march (D-017: always `stepMin 3, stepK 0.03, maxSteps 128`, no CPU-easier schedule, no perf AC of its own), plus `shadeTerrainCells(fb, terrain, world, timeSec)` (a new deferred-shading pass for kind-7 cells only, since terrain looks up colour through `TLOOK`, not a `MaterialTable`). Interim sun: `world.structures[0].level.def.sun` (azimuth/elevation) + the active `timeOfDay`'s `ambientI`/`sunI`, documented as a placeholder until US-007 owns the uniform (matches this story's own tech-notes wording "sun derived from the level's sun ... until then"). **Deviation flagged for architect review:** span narrowing after a terrain pass assumes per-column hit/miss is monotonic by row (every row is still marched independently, matching the GPU algorithm exactly - no early break) to know how far to `narrowBottom`; a genuine non-monotonic gap is not expected on `overworld_far`'s rolling-hill recipe but would leave that one cell unresolved rather than mis-shaded. `compositor.js` now calls `castTerrain(fb, world.terrain, cam, world)` then, after `shadeSurfaces`, `shadeTerrainCells(fb, world.terrain, world, fb.timeSec || 0)`, before `edgePass` (kind-7 cells pass through the edge rule untouched today, since kind 7 fails `isVert`/`isUp` - matches the spec's "deriv and edge pass kind 7 through untouched" without needing edgePass.js changes; `computeDerivatives` was **not** special-cased to skip kind 7 as the file list suggested - harmless (nothing reads `dudx`/`dvdx` for kind 7) but flagged as an unfinished polish item, not a correctness bug.
6. **`engine/index.js`:** exports `castTerrain`, `shadeTerrainCells`, `marchTerrainRay`, `shadeTerrainFar`, `packTerrainTextures`, `KIND_TERRAIN`, `PLANEID_TERRAIN`, the terrain step constants.
7. **`engine/world/World.js` (item 13, `world.horizon[]`):** `def.horizon` is validated (unique id, known model, numeric bearing/elev/angular, fog 0..1) and copied to `world.horizon`; `engine/world/serialize.js` round-trips it (content, not state). Verified end-to-end against the real `world_m1.js` (`ferrumLights` horizon entry): loads, validates, and `serialize(deserialize(...))` is byte-identical. **Not done:** nothing draws it yet (no `projectHorizon`, no sprite-pool horizon slot, no GLSL) - see "Not done".
8. **`engine/world/World.js` (item 7, billboard entities):** a `type: 'billboard'` entity's top-level fields (`model`, `unlit`, `fogModel`, `fogMax`, `minCells`, `detailRows`) are folded into `components.sprite`/`components.billboard` at load (so `SpritePool` and `serialize`/`deserialize` need no special case - confirmed the real `farTower` entity round-trips). `engine/render/sprites.js`'s `SpritePool`: `push`/`collect` carry the optional `components.billboard` through; `project()` gained (additively, without touching the BUG-OWN-002 scale/upscale-cap lines the architect may be working on in parallel): a `min`-LOD pick by projected rows (`rowsOnScreen < detailRows`, independent of the existing `half`-by-scale pick), a `minCells` clamp on the final screen rect (grows outward from centre / upward from the feet row, after the LOD pick), `unlit` (b = 1, skips `lightAt` even on the per-sprite `LightSet` path) and `fogModel`/`fogMax` (`P.util.fogFactor(depth, 'far')` capped at `fogMax`, `'interior'` default unchanged). **Simplification flagged:** the far-fog blend still targets the single existing `fog.interior` colour (no new SPR texel for a per-sprite fog colour) - the fog *fraction* is correct and capped, only its exact hue toward `fogFarNear`/`fogFar` is approximated. Because the GPU sprite pass (`sprites.frag.js`) only ever consumes the already-computed per-sprite `T1`/`T3` values `project()` writes, **this item needed zero GPU shader changes** - both paths are correct and parity-by-construction.
9. **D-011 addendum item 12 (per-key emissive fog cap), both paths:** `spritesAtlas.js`'s `packFrame` now encodes a key's `fogMax` into the atlas texel alpha (`a = 1 + round(fogMax*254)`, `a = 1` = today's unfogged default) instead of always `1`. `sprites.js`'s `drawSprites` and `glsl/sprites.frag.js` both decode it (`fe = min(spriteFogF, (a-1)/254)`) and blend emissive cells toward the fog colour by `fe` - identical formula, ported line for line (fixed the "no backticks inside a GLSL template literal" mistake once, per the lessons list, before it shipped). `spritesAtlas.js` also now packs `m.lods.min` (previously only `half` was packed) and guards models with no `world.w/h` (a `horizon: true` model like `ferrumLights` has none - it is placed by angle, not metres).
10. **Tests (all new/updated, all green):** `engine/render/terrainCaster.test.js` (135 checks: flat-floor hit distance within 0.5%, slope>=0/eyeH>=farMaxH takes 0 steps, outside-the-far-map miss, a hill recipe's climbing-ray escape, end-to-end `castTerrain` writes `KIND_TERRAIN`/the constant `PLANEID_TERRAIN`, no-op before `farReady`, structure-bbox skip (with a documented step-quantization margin at the very edge, matching the GPU algorithm's own limitation - see deviation above), 100 frames with no throw); `engine/render/gpu/TerrainTextures.test.js` (15 checks); `engine/render/terrainShade.test.js` (43 checks: band selection, determinism, byte-range sweep, heavy-fog space-out, glint). Existing suites updated only to import the two new design models (`far_tower.js`, `ferrum_lights.js`) `world_m1.js` now needs: `engine/world/world.test.js`, `engine/world/serialize.test.js`, `engine/render/gpu/sprites.test.js`. Full run: every suite in CLAUDE.md's list + `terrain.test.js` + `compositor.test.js` + `lighting.test.js` + `sprites.test.js` (81) + `gpuCompare.test.js` (35) + `glsl.test.js` (33) + `WorldTextures.test.js`/`ShadeTextures.test.js` + `check-deps` (129 files) + `check-deps.test.mjs` + `bench-cast.mjs --gc` - **all green**. `bench-cast`'s own v2-vs-3.5ms escalation-trigger line still fails, unrelated to this story (pre-existing, tracked separately under US-028/US-004b; nothing in this pass touches `sectorCaster.js`'s hot cast path).

Only `?gpu=0` (the CPU/JS oracle path) shows real terrain today.

- No horizon billboard DRAWING (`projectHorizon`, the sprite-pool slot, `HORIZON_DEPTH`) - `world.horizon[]` loads/validates/serializes correctly but nothing renders `ferrumLights` yet, on either path.
- No far-tower/Ferrum `?gpucompare=1` poses (`summitEast`, breach-looking-back-E), no `bench-poses.js` additions, no GPU ms measurement at 320x120 (nothing to measure - the GPU terrain pass doesn't exist).

**Browser verification + a real bug found and fixed (own server, port 9023, stopped after - port 8000 untouched).** `game/index.html` was missing `<script>` tags for `design/models/far_tower.js`/`ferrum_lights.js` (`world_m1.js` already referenced both, via the `farTower` entity and the new `horizon[]` entry) - fixed, both load cleanly now, no console errors on page load. Live-game verification via `window.__debug` (teleporting the player to the breach eye, forcing `terrain.bakeFarSync()`): found and fixed a real bug - `castTerrain` was bounding the march's `tMax` by `spans.depth[x]` (OpenSpans' per-COLUMN "how far the sector DDA stepped before giving up/leaving the structure's grid" bookkeeping value, e.g. ~6.5 m at the breach) instead of `FOG_FULL`. Per item 1, `tMax` should be the per-CELL resolved sector depth (+Infinity for every cell still in the open span, by the span's own invariant) - using the column-level give-up distance instead capped every ray at the structure's own exit point, so terrain past a breach/opening never marched. One-line fix (`terrainCaster.js`); `spans.depth[x]` is unrelated bookkeeping the terrain caster doesn't need. Re-verified directly against the live `window.__debug.world`/`gbuf`/`depthBuffer` (reconstructing the same `fb`/`cam` the real render loop uses, since the automated pane's own `render()`/rAF loop would not produce a visible frame here - a pre-existing tool limitation this project's own history already documents, e.g. the US-017 notes on rAF throttling in this exact pane, not something new): `castTerrain` now resolves ~47% of the 9,600 cells to `KIND_TERRAIN` at the breach pose (was 0 before the fix), and `shadeSurfaces`/`shadeTerrainCells` both produce correct, non-black, plausible glyphs and colours for both ordinary structure cells and the new terrain cells when invoked against that reconstructed live frame buffer. Recommend the owner do one real-Chrome look at `?gpu=0` from the breach as a sanity check, since this automated pane could not render a live frame to screenshot for a final visual check.

A note on scope, not an excuse: the architect's own build order lists 6 steps for this story and separately estimated the GPU pipeline work (of which this story's march is one slice) at multiple stories' worth of effort (`docs/architecture.md` section 14, "Effort: 6-7 stories"). Attempting the remaining GLSL/pipeline work without a further browser-iteration budget in this pass risked shipping unverified shader code, which seemed worse than stopping here with a clean, fully-tested foundation, one real bug found and fixed through direct verification against live game data, and a clear list of what remains.

**Architect answer to the ASK (2026-09-24).** Keep US-016 in `dev`, with **one** ARCH review when the GPU side lands. Reason: the JS slice is the oracle, and an oracle only gets reviewed properly next to the GLSL it must match (item 9 parity). Don't split the story. The slice is not being thrown away; it stays as it is. Rulings on the flagged deviations: `tMax = FOG_FULL` for open-span cells is right (item 1). Monotonic `narrowBottom` is accepted (sky fill only). Glint tint = `TLOOK[type][2]` is accepted: drop `uRiverLight` from 14.4 item 3. `computeDerivatives` kind-7 skip goes in with step 3. **Not accepted:** the far-fog hue approximation on billboards. Item 7 requires a per-sprite `fogRGB` = `mix(fogFarNear, fogFar, f)` for `fogModel: 'far'`, computed in `project()` and carried in the SPR texels (step 5). Spot-check found **BUG-OWN-004**: `shadeTerrainFar` read TLOOK 0..1 as bytes, so terrain was black. The architect fixed it (row 25i). Add a brightness test.

GPU build order (14.4; each step leaves the game working):
1. **Upload + uniforms.** Wire `packTerrainTextures` into `GpuCellPipeline.js`: `FARH` R32F, `FARTYPE` R8UI, `TLOOK` RGBA32F. Upload on `farReady` and on each `farVersion` change, never per frame. Uniforms per item 3. Move `sunFromWorld` into one exported helper so both paths use the same sun.
2. **Pass A2 `glsl/terrain.frag.js`.** The item 4 march, literally, with manual 4-`texelFetch` bilinear. Sub-sample res, reads set 1, writes set 2. Resolve binds set 2 when `terrain && farReady`. A compile/link failure goes to the US-045 startup error (item 11). `?terrain=0` skips the pass. Test it first with a debug colour: kind 7 = magenta.
3. **`shade.frag` `kind == 7` branch.** Port `shadeTerrainFar` line for line: TLOOK stays 0..1, `*255` only at the byte step, same GAIN LUT, same `HASH_FAST`. `deriv`/`edge` pass kind 7 through untouched, plus the CPU `computeDerivatives` skip.
4. **Parity.** In compare mode (`timeSec = 0`), add poses `breach`, `breachDown`, `parapetSky` and `ringLook` to `?gpucompare=1` and `bench-poses.js`, using the item 9 terrain thresholds. Get ALL PASS before going on.
5. **Horizon + billboard fog.** `projectHorizon` + one sprite-pool slot per `world.horizon[]` entry, `HORIZON_DEPTH = 1e6`, per-sprite `fogRGB` (above). Item 14 Node tests. Add compare poses `summitEast` and breach-looking-back-E.
6. **Measure.** GPU ms at 320x120 on the breach pose: the terrain pass must be <= 1.0 ms p95. Record it in US-018, then set `arch-review`.

**Programmer notes (2026-09-24, step 1 only) -> stays `dev`.** Small pass this time, being honest about scope: the 6-step build order is a multi-session amount of GLSL/pipeline work (per the architect's own effort estimate and the prior pass's notes), and shipping partial, unverified shader code (steps 2-3) risked breaking the whole GPU path for a marginal gain given the remaining budget - not worth it. Done, tested:
- **BUG-OWN-004 regression test:** added to `engine/render/terrainShade.test.js` (near terrain at b=0.9, no fog, must not be near-black on any channel) - passes against the architect's existing fix in `terrainShade.js` (TLOOK colours * 255 at the byte step).
- **Step 1 (upload + uniforms), the texture half only:** `engine/render/gpu/GpuCellPipeline.js` now imports `packTerrainTextures` and adds `texFarH` (R32F), `texFarType` (R8UI), `texTlook` (RGBA32F) - 1x1 placeholders until a world's `terrain.farReady` flips, same pattern as the existing world-atlas textures. New `_ensureTerrainTextures(world)` runs from `_hook()` (gated on the `useDda` path, right after `_ensureWorldTextures`) and re-uploads only when `terrain.farVersion` or the `world` object changes - never per frame. Added to the `dispose()` cleanup list and to context-restore reset (`_terrainVersion = -1` etc., mirroring `_worldAtlas = null`). **Not done:** the uniforms half of item 1 (`uTerrainMaxH`, `uFarMap`, `uSunDir`, `uTerrainLight`, `uFogFar*`, `uTimeSec`) - there is no terrain GLSL program yet to bind them to (that's step 2), so setting uniform values now would be dead code with nothing to verify against. `sunFromWorld` (`engine/render/terrainCaster.js`) is now exported (and re-exported via `engine/index.js`) per the item 1 instruction, ready for step 2 to call from both the JS oracle and the GPU uniform upload.
- **Steps 2-6: not started.** No `glsl/terrain.frag.js`, no pass A2/ping-pong wiring, no `kind==7` branches in shade/deriv/edge, no new `?gpucompare=1` poses, no horizon billboard draw, no GPU ms measurement. The default `gl2` path still shows sky where terrain should be.
- **Verification:** all Node suites in CLAUDE.md + terrain/terrainShade/TerrainTextures/sprites/gpuCompare/glsl/world/serialize + `check-deps` + `check-deps.test.mjs` (own fixture-failure suite) all green - no regressions. Browser smoke test (own server, port 9031, stopped after - port 8000 untouched): loaded the default `tower` level with `?debug=1`, waited for the terrain bake, then forced `terrain.bakeFarSync()` via `window.__debug` to flip `farReady` true (`farVersion` 1) - `_ensureTerrainTextures` then ran every frame with no new console errors/warnings and the GPU pipeline stayed active (`[GpuCellPipeline] active`). This confirms the new upload code path is inert-but-safe, not that it's pixel-correct (nothing samples these textures yet).

**Programmer notes (2026-09-24, steps 2-3) -> stays `dev`.** Continues from step 1 (34f299a). Done, tested:
- **Step 2 (`glsl/terrain.frag.js`, pass A2):** new `TERRAIN_FRAG_SRC` program, literal GLSL twin of `marchTerrainRay`/`castTerrain` (item 4) - same step schedule (`MAX_TERRAIN_STEPS`/`STEP_MIN`/`STEP_K`/`T_START`/`FOG_FULL` all JS-injected, not hand-copied), same 5-step bisection, same structure-bbox skip (`slabTerrain`, a literal port of `slab2D`), manual 4-`texelFetch` bilinear on `FARH` (`farHBilinear`, matches `overworld_far.js`'s `util.gridHeight` exactly - no hardware filtering). D-017/item 11 simplification: since this is a per-sub-ray fragment shader (not per-column like the JS oracle's span logic), "march only where the sector pass left the cell open" collapses to "march only where the pass-A sub-sample's own kind is 0" - a sub-ray that already has a structure hit is copied through untouched, no march at all (documented in the file's own header comment; this is the sub-ray-granular equivalent of the JS oracle's per-column open-span walk, not an independent design). `GpuCellPipeline.js`: new `texSGI2`/`texSGA2`/`texSDepth2` ("set 2") + `fboTerrainSub`, `progTerrain`, `_passTerrain()` (runs between `_passCast()` and `_passResolve()` only when `world.terrain && terrain.farReady`); `_passResolve()` now binds one of two prebuilt tables (`_resolveBindsSet1`/`_resolveBindsSet2`, no uniform, no per-frame allocation) depending on that flag, per item 2's "bind-time choice". `uStructA/B/Count` uploaded to `progTerrain` too (`_uploadUStruct`), `uFarMap`/`uTerrainMaxH` uploaded whenever the far bake (re)runs (`_uploadTerrainUniforms`, called from `_ensureTerrainTextures`).
- **Step 3 (`shade.frag` kind==7 branch):** ported `shadeTerrainFar` line for line into `TERRAIN_SHADE_GLSL` (also in `terrain.frag.js` - same file the march program lives in, included by `shade.frag.js`): same TLOOK 0..1-then-*255-at-the-byte-step order (BUG-OWN-004), same `GAIN` LUT (reuses `uFgMin`/`uFgMaxGain`/`samplePowLUT`, already in `shade.frag` for material shading - `gainOf`'s formula is the same `pow(bc,fgGamma)` curve, just with the terrain palette's own `fgMin`/`fgMaxGain`, which are the SAME global `palette.shading` values), same `HASH_FAST`. `deriv.frag.js` skips kind 7 (writes 0, per the architect's ruling on the ASK). **One deviation from architecture.md 14.4 item 4's literal wording, not from the JS oracle:** item 4 says the march pass writes `aoD = b` (computed there); the JS oracle instead computes `b` in the SEPARATE `shadeTerrainCells` pass (`terrainNormal` + `sunFromWorld`), because a `shade.frag` kind==7 branch needing its own `uFarH` texture unit would have pushed `shade.frag`'s sampler count to 17 (`MAX_TEXTURE_IMAGE_UNITS` is 16 on the dev GPU - confirmed by a real link failure during this pass, see below). Kept `farHNormal`/`sunFromWorld`'s dot product in the MARCH pass instead (which already has `uFarH` bound for the height march) and passed `b` through `GA.w` (`aoD`) to `shade.frag` - same maths, same inputs (`sunFromWorld` is the one exported helper both the JS oracle and this GPU path call), just computed one pass earlier than item 4's prose describes; the architect's own build-order step-3 text ("port `shadeTerrainFar` line for line", `shadeTerrainFar` itself already takes `b` as an input) is consistent with this split. Flagging for the architect to confirm or override.
- **Two real bugs found and fixed via the browser during this pass (own server, port 9033, stopped after - port 8000 untouched):** (1) a GLSL redefinition (`uBandNear` etc. declared twice - once by hand in `shade.frag.js`, once by the included `TERRAIN_SHADE_GLSL` snippet) caught by a real shader compile error; (2) the texture-unit overflow above, caught by a real program-link error (`FRAGMENT shader texture image units count exceeds MAX_TEXTURE_IMAGE_UNITS(16)`) - fixed by moving `b`'s computation into the march pass as described. After both fixes: `?gpucompare=1`'s existing 14 poses stay **ALL PASS** (`kind=100.00%`, `glyph=100.00%` on every pose, `[gpucompare] ALL PASS`), `[GpuCellPipeline] active`, no console errors. Further live verification via `window.__debug` (teleporting the player to the breach eye per `design/preview/overworld.html`'s pose, `world.entity('player').transform`, forcing `terrain.bakeFarSync()`, then reading back `GI`/`GA`/`Depth` and the shade-pass-only textures within one JS call so the game's own rAF loop can't advance the frame mid-check): the GPU march resolves up to ~10,094 of 21,600 sub-sampled cells to `KIND_TERRAIN` (was 0 before this pass) looking out through the breach, with plausible `mat`/`dist`/`aoD` (b) values varying smoothly across columns (e.g. `dist` 745-756 m along a ridge, `aoD` ~1.03-1.04). The shaded RGBA came back all-zero for these cells in that same check - traced to the automated pane's pointer-lock-less session drawing a full-screen pause overlay (`giMask(gi.y) == 1` on every cell, confirmed by reading the mask bit directly), which correctly routes every cell (not just terrain) through `shade.frag`'s existing `maskU != 0u` JS-passthrough branch - not a terrain bug. Recommend the owner do one real-Chrome look (with working pointer lock) at the breach to confirm the visual colours/glyphs, since this automated pane cannot unlock the pointer to get past that overlay.
