### US-016b Terrain recipe follow-up for the world model  [Priority: P0] [Status: done]
As a player, I want the land outside the tower to be one continuous world, so that it meets the tower seamlessly and can later be walked on.
Acceptance criteria – Designer (small, D-008):
- [x] `heightAt(x, y)` and `typeAt(x, y)` are documented and implemented as continuous analytic functions usable at any sample spacing (2 m near, 8 m far), with the baked 8 m grid equal to sampling them.
- [x] Near-LOD look spec for 2 m cells within 300 m, with glyph bands and colors extending the current near band, and a preview swatch.
- [x] Flat 2.4 m crown radius covering the tower footprint and outcrop, plus the handover rule: within 6 m of a structure's outer ring, terrain height blends linearly to the ring height. The mismatch where the player can stand is exactly 0; the preview check shows max |delta| = 0.00 m along the ring.
- [x] One-paragraph sketch of per-chunk overrides (height stamp, type paint) as JSON for the future editor.
Design needed: yes (recipe + doc + preview update).
Notes / dependencies: feeds US-025 (World terrain sampler) and US-026 (near LOD). Cosmetic for the M1 far view.

**PO APPROVED (2026-09-22) – US-016b done** (design/data-only story; exercised by the US-016 / US-025 / US-026 tests). The coordinator verified both previews in the browser: `overworld.html` 17/17 checks, including an 8,100-point finiteness check; `tower.html` 18/18; no console errors.
- (a) `heightAt(x, y)` / `typeAt(x, y)` are continuous and analytic at any spacing. The river-bed carve is smoothed, and slope for `typeAt` is measured over a fixed 2 m, so the type does not depend on sample spacing. There is `util.bakeChunk` for 2 m chunks and `util.gridHeight(G, x, y)` for baked grids.
- (b) Near-LOD bands: close < 40, near < 150, mid < 300 m, with a stable per-cell dither handover at 280-320 m to the far grid. Variation is world-keyed, so it does not shimmer as the camera moves. The detail (slope faces, trunks, flowers, reeds, foam) is good material for US-026.
- (c) A flat 2.4 m crown covers the whole footprint (bastion and outcrop included), with a 6 m linear blend to the ring height. The mismatch where the player crosses is exactly 0.
  - **Change to approved US-010 data, accepted:** legend `,` is raised from 1.0 to 2.4 m so the outer ring is uniform. Those cells are unreachable in M1 and are seen only through the sun crack and from the summit, so it changes nothing about play.
  - New rule for future structures, adopted: the outer ring is flat or has at most 0.3 m variation between neighbours.
- (d) Per-128 m-chunk JSON overrides (height stamps, type paints), with the tower crown as the first entry and a JSON round-trip check. This is the seed for US-027 and the editor.
Designer note (2026-09-22): **Preview ready for PO review.**

The near part samples the real `tower.js` sectors. The page also shows the top-down map with the view cone and towers, terrain swatches by distance, the far-tower silhouette and automated checks.
- **Data:** `design/levels/overworld_far.js` (seeded recipe plus reference `generate()`, 256x256 x 8 m). Doc: `design/levels/overworld_far.md`.
- **Content:**
  - A river valley about 450 m W, rolling hills and forests, and a path to a ford.
  - Fog uses palette `fog.far` (50 to 1500 m) into `skyHorizon`.
  - The far tower is 800 m WSW on a hill crown. It is dark, unlit and not emissive, with fog capped at 0.40, and it breaks the skyline. It is drawn no smaller than a 3x4 silhouette.
- Status stays `design`.

Designer note, US-016b (2026-09-22): **Preview ready for PO review.** `design/preview/overworld.html` (v2) has viewpoints for the breach, the path, the meadow and "looking back at the tower", a near-LOD toggle, a handover profile and 17 checks. Checks cover continuity, grid = function, 2 m vs 8 m agreement, and a NaN sweep. The earlier NaN came from the old page calling v1's `heightAt(G, x, y)`; that call shape now throws a clear TypeError.

The tower's outer ring is now flat 2.4 m: legend `,` went from 1.0 to 2.4, an M1-unreachable cosmetic change. The ring mismatch is exactly 0, and a check prints it.

### US-017 End trigger, fade and restart  [Priority: P0] [Status: done]
As a player, I want a satisfying ending when I step out onto the hill, so that the slice feels complete.
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] Entering the outcrop trigger cells locks input; the camera walks forward 1 m over 1.5 s and pitches slightly down toward the valley.
- [ ] Screen fades to black over 2 s (glyphs dim down the ramp, not just an overlay alpha). Use the `ASSETS.uiStyle.fade` rule (US-015 art) for both the 3D view and the text. End-text layout, colors and blinking cursor come from `uiStyle.endText`.
- [ ] Text, centred, typed on at 30 chars/s. First line depends on the relay state (D-003, D-011): not woken (default, and always if US-022 is not built) = `The signal is still calling.`; woken = `One relay wakes. The signal is still calling.`. Then `Someone is out there.`, then after 1.5 s `- to be continued -`, then `[R] Wake again`. (PO placeholder text per D-011; the writer's final lines in `docs/story.md` replace it, and the strings live in `uiStyle.endText`, never in code.)
- [ ] R restarts the slice from the wake sequence with all state reset (lamp on the gondola bracket, boulder on stair, lever up, grate down, relay dead, map card "shown once" and hints reset).
- [ ] (D-006 / D-008) The end trigger is the `def.triggers` entry `end` (cells tagged `trigger:end`, `walkTo`, `pitchTo`) with behaviour `quest.end`, registered from `game/js/quest/`. Restart = reload the world from the level data + `deserialize` of the initial state (US-025), not a hand-written reset list, so nothing can be forgotten.
Design needed: no.
Notes / dependencies: US-010, US-015, US-016.
**D-011 reskin (PO, 2026-09-23):** only the end-card text changes (above). The `uiStyle.endText` strings move to the new lines, and the designer updates the `title.html` end-screen buttons. The mood shifts from "melancholy" to "I broke out, now what's out here?". Writer: final lines, each <= 40 chars, ASCII 32-126.

**Tech notes (architect, 2026-09-23).** Normative APIs: `docs/architecture.md` 7.4 (Triggers, Fade, Restart).
1. **Engine:** `engine/world/triggers.js` (`world.triggers` built at load; cells from the `trigger:<id>` tag, with `def.cells` as the fallback; circle + `zMin` for the hint triggers; enter-edge firing; generic `once`; event `trigger:fired`). `engine/ui/fade.js` (`createFadeLut`, `fadeGlyph`, `applySceneFade`) with `fb.sceneFade` on non-mask cells. `engine.setWorld(world)`.
2. **Game:** `game/js/quest/end.js` holds `quest.end`: it sets `world.state['quest.endT'] = 0` and returns `true`. A per-step game function advances `quest.endT`. While `endT >= 0`, `main.js` passes zero controls and `usePressed = false`. Walk: the player moves toward `origin + def.walkTo`, capped at 1 m over 1.5 s, through `moveCapsule` (so it cannot clip). Pitch eases to `pitchTo`. Then `sceneFade` runs 1 -> 0 over 2 s (timings from `uiStyle.endText`/`uiStyle.fade` if present; otherwise 1.5 s + 2 s as in the ACs). `game/js/ui/endCard.js` types the text at 30 chars/s, picking the first line by `world.state['tower.beacon.lit']`; all strings come from `uiStyle.endText`. R (only once `[R]` is shown) calls `engine.setWorld(deserialize(initialState, assets))`. Hints and the map card read and write `world.state`, so the restart resets them for free.
3. **Data:** already present (`X`/`Y` tagged `trigger:end`; `def.triggers.end` with `walkTo`, `pitchTo`, `trigger: 'quest.end'`). Add `quest.endT: -1` and `ui.mapCard.shown: false` to the `world_m1.js` `state`.
4. **GPU:** no world-texture work. `setWorld` swaps the world object, and `GpuCellPipeline` already does a full atlas rebuild on world identity change. **The GPU fade** (`uSceneFade` + `FADELUT` in sprite pass F: `engine/render/gpu/glsl/sprite.frag.js`, `GpuCellPipeline.js`) is sub-task **US-017-gpu**, to be done **after US-030b is ARCH OK**. Until then the GPU path may show the fade by forcing CPU fading of the UI only; the story is not done without it.
5. **Tests:** `engine/world/triggers.test.js`: the tag/cells union and the mismatch warning; enter fires exactly once; `once`; circle `zMin`; standing inside after a load. `engine/ui/fade.test.js`: `a = 1` is the identity, `a = 0` gives space and black, letters use index 9, the fg gain curve, `applySceneFade` leaves mask cells alone, no allocation. `game/js/quest/restart.test.js` (real `world_m1`): take the lamp, pull the lever, stop mid-open, push the boulder, fire `quest.end`; then `serialize(deserialize(initialState))` deep-equals `initialState`; `packed.geom` equals a fresh load (proves the level def was not mutated); `validateBehaviours` is empty. GPU fade: `?gpucompare=1` gains a pose with `sceneFade = 0.5`, compared against the CPU path.
6. **Files:** `engine/world/triggers.js` (+test), `engine/world/World.js` (load table), `engine/ui/fade.js` (+test), `engine/render/compositor.js` (CPU `applySceneFade`), `engine/core/engine.js` (`setWorld`), `engine/index.js`, `game/js/quest/end.js`, `game/js/quest/index.js`, `game/js/ui/endCard.js`, `game/js/main.js`, `game/js/quest/restart.test.js`, `design/levels/world_m1.js` (state keys); later (US-017-gpu) `engine/render/gpu/glsl/sprite.frag.js` + `GpuCellPipeline.js`.

**Parallel plan (architect):** start **US-013** and **US-014** now, on disjoint files (physics vs world). Then **US-012**, then **US-017**; the two overlap in `World.load` and `main.js`, so either run them one after the other or merge carefully. US-017-gpu comes after US-030b ARCH OK. None of the four touches `engine/render/gpu/*` except US-017-gpu.

**Programmer notes (2026-09-24) -> `arch-review`.** Built per the tech notes; one small addition beyond the documented API shape, called out below.
1. **Triggers** (`engine/world/triggers.js`): `buildTriggers(world)` (called from `World.load`, right after `world.interactables`) + `updateTriggers(world, engine, actor)`. Cell triggers union the `trigger:<id>` tag with `def.cells`, warn once (per `structId.id`) on a mismatch, and use a `Uint8Array` mask; circle triggers use `x,y,r,zMin` (world coords). One deviation from the literal ctx shape in tech note item 2 (`{engine, def, entity}`): `fireTrigger`'s ctx also carries `structId` (the placed structure the trigger belongs to), since `quest.end` needs it to resolve `origin + def.walkTo` into world coords without a literal coordinate (US-010 tech note 1 rule) and nothing else in the documented shape supplies it. `world.triggers` is runtime-only, rebuilt every load/deserialize, never serialized.
2. **Fade** (`engine/ui/fade.js`): `createFadeLut`, `fadeGlyph`, `applySceneFade`, wired into `compositor.js`'s CPU-only tail (`fb.fadeLut` + `fb.sceneFade`, both optional so every other `renderWorld` caller is unaffected). Confirmed live in Chrome (`?debug=1`, forcing `world.state['quest.endT']`) that the end-card text renders correctly and reads back byte-exact from `rt.cells`; the GPU-DDA path's own scene fade is out of scope here (see point 5).
3. **Restart** (`engine.setWorld`, `engine/core/engine.js`): sets `engine.world`, rebinds `world.events`, emits `'world:loaded'`. `game/js/main.js` now builds `playerHandle`/`look`/`lightSet`/the light-count-8 test rig inside a `'world:loaded'` listener registered before the first `engine.loadWorld` call, so a restart (`engine.setWorld(deserialize(initialState, assets))`) rebuilds all of it exactly like the first load did - no separate reset list. `initialState = serialize(engine.world)` is captured immediately after `engine.loadWorld` returns (which, since `Events.emit` is synchronous, is already after that listener ran once).
4. **Quest** (`game/js/quest/end.js`, `game/js/ui/endCard.js`): `questEnd` (the `quest.end` behaviour) stores a transient `_endWalk` (start pos, unit direction, pitch0/pitchTo) on `entity.components.body` - `_`-prefixed, so `serialize.js`'s `stripScratch` drops it, same convention as `integrate.js`'s `_move`/`_collideOpts`. `stepEnd` (called every fixed step from `main.js`, after `updateTriggers`) advances `quest.endT` and, only through `walkSec`, moves the actor via `moveCapsule` (reusing `body._move`/`_collideOpts`, already populated by `integrate()` earlier in the same step) and eases pitch. `endFadeAmount`/`computeEndCardState` are pure functions of `world.state['quest.endT']`, so a restart's fresh `-1` value cleanly hides everything. `ASSETS.uiStyle.endText`/`uiStyle.fade` don't exist yet (US-015 is still `design`) - `readEndTimings`/`drawEndCard` already read them when present (same fallback precedent as `crosshairStyle`, US-012); until then the timings are the AC fallback (1.5 s walk, 2 s fade, 1.5 s gap, 30 cps) and the end-text color falls back to `palette.ui.endText` ('uiText').
5. **Deferred (per tech note item 4, unchanged):** GPU scene fade (`uSceneFade`/`FADELUT` in the sprite pass) is **US-017-gpu**, blocked on US-030b ARCH OK - not attempted here. On the gl2/GPU-DDA path today, `renderWorld`'s early-out means the 3D view does not visibly fade (confirmed live); the end-card TEXT still renders correctly (JS-drawn, independent of the DDA cast) since it only appears after the fade would have completed. The `?gpucompare=1` pose with `sceneFade = 0.5` from tech note item 5 is left for US-017-gpu, for the same reason.
6. **Tests:** all Node suites in CLAUDE.md + `check-deps` pass, plus the three new suites (`engine/world/triggers.test.js` 17 checks, `engine/ui/fade.test.js` 15 checks, `game/js/quest/restart.test.js` 16 checks, the last against real `world_m1`: lamp, lever + mid-open grate, a pushed boulder, `quest.end`, proving `initialState` is unaffected by any of it and `serialize(deserialize(initialState))` round-trips exactly).
7. **Browser-verified** (own server, port 8987, stopped after - port 8000 untouched): loaded `game/index.html?debug=1`, forced `world.state['quest.endT']` through the walk/fade/text/continue/restart phases via `window.__debug`, confirmed the end-card lines (including the beacon-lit first-line branch not exercised, and the blinking `[R] Wake again_` cursor) render byte-exact in `rt.cells` with no console errors. Restart itself (pressing `R` to actually flip the world) could not be confirmed live in this automated headless-Chrome pane - `requestAnimationFrame`/the fixed-step accumulator appear heavily throttled there (`quest.endT` barely advances over real wall-clock time, and even a synchronous instrumentation patch of `loop.update` saw 0 calls over 1 s of real time) - almost certainly a headless/background-tab rAF throttling artifact of this tool, not the game. The restart mechanism itself (`engine.setWorld` + the `'world:loaded'` rebuild, `deserialize(initialState)`) is covered end-to-end by `restart.test.js`. Recommend the owner do one real-Chrome click-through of `R` as a sanity check.
8. **Known limitation:** `ASSETS.uiStyle.endText`/`uiStyle.fade` don't exist yet (US-015 still `design`); the writer's final end-card lines (`docs/story.md`) also aren't wired in yet (D-011 PO placeholder text is used, per the story's own note). Neither blocks this story per the tech notes' documented fallback.

**ARCH CHANGES #1 (architect, 2026-09-24) -> `dev`.** Engine parts (triggers, fade LUT, `setWorld`), tests and the `world:loaded` rebuild are sound; all suites + check-deps pass. Fixed by the architect in the working tree (uncommitted, <20 lines each, please keep): (a) `main.js`: while ending, `controls.yaw/pitch` come from the transform (and `look` is synced to it) - `integrate` was copying the stale `look.pitchDeg` back every step, so the camera snapped back up the moment the walk phase ended; (b) `main.js` render: `fb.lights = lightSet` per frame - `fb.lights` was bound once at `fb` creation, so after a restart it still pointed at the old world's LightSet; (c) `main.js` + `engine/core/playerLook.js`: `look.dispose()` before rebuilding it on restart, and `PlayerLook` reads `document.pointerLockElement` at construction (an already-locked canvas gets no `pointerlockchange`); (d) `end.js` `stepEnd`: exit on the previous `endT`, so the step that crosses `walkSec` still runs with `frac = 1` (90/60 is not exactly 1.5; the walk/pitch ended ~1 % short).
Required changes (programmer):
1. **GPU scene fade lands in this story** (was US-017-gpu; US-030b is `done` and per D-017 the GPU path is the only shipping path - the "fades to black" AC is currently not met where the game ships). Sprite/composite pass F (`engine/render/gpu/glsl/sprites.frag.js`, `spritesPass.js`): `uniform float uSceneFade` + a 128x1 `R8UI` `uFadeLut` texture (`lut.idx`) + the ramp codes (`lut.ramp`, second row or a `uFadeRamp` 1D texture); for every NON-mask cell (mask bit is already in `uGI` bits 12-15) apply exactly `fadeGlyph` (`j = round(a * idx[code])`, glyph = `ramp[j]`) and `fg *= minGain + (1-minGain)*a`, `bg *= a`, with `a >= 1` a no-op. Upload the LUT once (when `fb.fadeLut` identity changes), the uniform per frame from `fb.sceneFade`. No new per-frame allocation.
2. **CPU oracle order:** move the `applySceneFade` call out of `compositor.js` (`renderWorld`) to after `sprites.render(...)` in `main.js` (a `sceneFade`-aware spot after sprites; the sprites write plain non-mask cells and must fade too, matching pass F). Tech-note text updated in `docs/architecture.md` 7.4.
3. **Parity:** `?gpucompare=1` gains one pose with `sceneFade = 0.5` (US-029 thresholds) and `engine/render/gpu/glsl.test.js`/`gpuCompare.test.js` get the LUT/uniform wiring covered if they already cover the other pass-F uniforms.
4. Re-run the suites + `check-deps`; note in the Programmer notes whether the ASSETS `uiStyle.endText`/`fade` fallback is still in use (accepted for arch; PO decides whether the hard-coded placeholder strings may ship before US-015).
Not blocking: the headless R-restart not being observable (rAF throttled) - `restart.test.js` covers the mechanism; tester does the click-through.

**Programmer fix pass (2026-09-24) -> `arch-review`.** All 4 required changes done; did not touch the architect's 5 already-committed fixes (4b8d73c).
1. **GPU scene fade** (`engine/render/gpu/glsl/sprites.frag.js`, `spritesPass.js`): added `uSceneFade`, `uFadeMinGain`, `uFadeRampLen` uniforms + `uFadeLut` (R8UI 128x1, `lut.idx`) / `uFadeRamp` (R8UI rampLen x1, `lut.ramp`) textures. Applied once per fragment, after the sprite hit-test (so both the plain edge-pass copy AND a sprite-covered cell get it), to every non-mask cell (the existing `giMask` early-return already excludes mask cells): `j = floor(uSceneFade * idx[code] + 0.5)` (never GLSL `round(` - `sprites.test.js`'s lexical rule, now also asserted for the new code), glyph -> `ramp[j]`, `fg *= minGain + (1-minGain)*a`, `bg *= a`, skipped whole when `uSceneFade >= 1.0`. `GpuSpritePass.setFadeLut(lut)` uploads once per identity change (`UNPACK_ALIGNMENT=1` for both R8UI uploads, restored after); `spriteDev.js`'s `render()` mirrors `fb.sceneFade`/`fb.fadeLut` onto the pass every frame (cheap no-op once uploaded).
2. **CPU oracle order:** `applySceneFade` call moved from `compositor.js` (`renderWorld`'s CPU tail) to `game/js/main.js`, right after `sprites.render(...)`, guarded by `!fb.gpuDda` (unchanged gating - the GPU-DDA path never touched `fb.rt.cells` there anyway). `docs/architecture.md` 7.4 already read correctly for this (the architect had pre-written the target text in 4b8d73c), so no doc edit was needed.
3. **Parity:** added a `world_m1` spawn-pose row with `sceneFade = 0.5` to `?gpucompare=1` (`runGpuCompareDdaMode`, main.js) - GPU side sets `sprites.pass.sceneFade`/`setFadeLut` before `rt.present()`, CPU oracle calls `applySceneFade` after its own `drawSprites`. Also cleared `rt.cells.mask` before that CPU call: `shadeSurfaces`/`drawSprites` write through `setCellRGB`, which sets `mask=1` as a side effect (the real "JS overlay wins" signal) on every cell they touch, which would make `applySceneFade` skip almost the whole oracle image - harmless in real gameplay (the GPU-DDA path never runs this CPU cast), but wrong for an oracle with no genuine UI drawn. Reset the shared fade state to 1 before the informational `?rays=2` loop so it doesn't inherit the mandatory loop's last `sceneFade`. `?gpucompare=1`: **ALL PASS** (8 rows, browser-verified, port 8989, stopped after - port 8000 untouched). Added lexical coverage to `sprites.test.js` (uniform/texture presence, fade-skip-when-`>=1` branch, already-covered "no `round(`" rule now also exercises the new code).
4. **Real bug found + fixed along the way:** the sceneFade=0.5 parity pose first failed at glyph=0.07% (colors matched, glyphs didn't). Root cause: `applySceneFade` (`engine/ui/fade.js`) wrote the faded glyph into `cb.glyphIdx[i]` only, never into the duplicate copy at `cb.fg[fi+3]` that `CellBuffer.js` documents as the authoritative RGBA8 upload byte (every other writer, `setCell`/`setCellRGB`, keeps both in sync) - so anything reading `rt.cells.fg` directly (GPU texture upload on the CPU fallback path, `readbackPresent`, the compare tools) saw the pre-fade glyph even though colors and `cb.glyphIdx` had faded correctly. One-line fix (`fg[fi + 3] = newIdx`) + a new assertion in `fade.test.js` (5c). This was a pre-existing bug (present since the story's first cut), not something this pass introduced; flagging since it likely affected the CPU-only (`?gpu=0`) fallback's on-screen fade too, not just this compare tool.
5. **Suites:** all Node suites in CLAUDE.md + `check-deps` + `check-deps.test.mjs` + `triggers.test.js`/`fade.test.js`/`restart.test.js` + `sprites.test.js`/`gpuCompare.test.js`/`glsl.test.js` - ALL PASS. `ASSETS.uiStyle.endText`/`fade` fallback status unchanged from the last review (still not wired in; US-015 still `design`), not touched by this pass.

**Architect re-review (2026-09-24, cdb481a): ARCH OK -> `po-review`.** All 4 items done correctly. GPU fade in pass F has the same math as `fadeGlyph`/`applySceneFade` (no `round(`, `a >= 1` is identity, mask cells return early, and a plain copy and a sprite cell take the same path). The LUT uploads once per identity change, with `UNPACK_ALIGNMENT` restored after. There are no per-frame allocations. `_initGL` resets the ref, so a context restore uploads the LUT again. The `fg[fi+3]` desync fix is correct and has test 5c. Clearing the mask in the compare oracle is justified. Re-ran: fade, sprites (55), glsl (33), gpuCompare (35), restart, check-deps (114): all pass. Advisory, not blocking: if the pipeline is ready but the sprite pass failed to init (`gpuDda` true, `pass.active` false), neither path fades. This is a rare degraded state and matches how sprites already behave there.

**PO OK – US-017 ready for testing (PO, 2026-09-24).** Checked against every AC. Trigger + input lock + 1 m / 1.5 s walk + pitch (`def.triggers.end`, `quest.end` from `game/js/quest/`): met. 2 s ramp-step fade on CPU and GPU (pass F, sprites included; `?gpucompare=1` sceneFade=0.5 ALL PASS): met. End-card lines match the D-011 placeholders exactly (spot-read `game/js/ui/endCard.js`), 30 cps, 1.5 s gap, `[R] Wake again` with cursor: met. Restart = `setWorld(deserialize(initialState))`, no reset list, covered by `restart.test.js`: met. **Accepted deviation:** `uiStyle.endText`/`uiStyle.fade` do not exist yet (US-015 is `design`), so the timings use the AC fallback values and the placeholder strings sit in `game/` code (not engine code). The strings-from-`uiStyle` part of AC 2/3 moves to US-015 as a re-check AC (same precedent as `crosshairStyle`, US-012).
Tester notes:
1. Real Chrome, `game/index.html`: walk to the outcrop end trigger live (no forced `quest.endT`). Input locks; the camera walks ~1 m over 1.5 s, pitches down and stays pitched (no snap back up).
2. The fade takes 2 s and steps down the glyph ramp (not an alpha overlay). It includes sprites (lamp, boulder, any visible sprite), both on the default GPU path and on `?gpu=0`.
3. End card: centred, typed at ~30 chars/s; first line `The signal is still calling.` (default); `Someone is out there.`; after ~1.5 s `- to be continued -`; then `[R] Wake again` with the blinking cursor. R does nothing before `[R]` is shown.
4. Before the end, take the lamp, pull the lever (grate up), push the boulder. Then press R: the wake sequence restarts, the lamp is back on the gondola bracket, the lever is up, the grate is down, the boulder is on the stair base, the lights are correct (no stale lamp light) and mouse look works. Do a second full run to the end and restart again.

The 8-light GPU budget (US-006/US-007) is met with a wide margin; the headless 5.4 ms reading was software GL. Still to measure in US-018: the overworld view (US-016) and the worst-case poses.
As a player, I want the game to stay perfectly smooth, so that movement always feels responsive.
Acceptance criteria:

- [ ] (D-017) **Obsolete:** "CPU fallback stays playable (US-028 deferred perf gate)" is dropped (no playable CPU fallback is required).
- [ ] **Grid setting**: `?grid=WxH` and `createEngine({cols, rows})` accept 160x60 to 320x120 (clamped), **default 240x90 on `gl2`** (D-009 amendment 2) and forced 160x60 on the fallback; both 240x90 and 320x120 must meet the bar above. The in-game option is US-038 (M2), not required in M1.
- [ ] No per-frame allocations in the hot render loop that cause visible GC stutter (no frame > 25 ms during a 60 s walk-through), on both paths.
Design needed: no.
Notes / dependencies: final check before M1 exit; overlay part can be built with US-004. D-009 budgets.

### BUG-CAST-001 Thin wall-top (TOP) band: CPU caster overdraws the cap with a farther floor/step  [Priority: P1] [Status: testing]
Row 25c. Repro: `?gpucompare=1` "world_m1: boulder mid-roll" (+ `&sun=0`): GPU kind 5 TOP at d ~2.7/2.2 (browser 2.95/2.40), CPU kind 4 FLOOR / kind 2 STEP at d ~5.4.

**Architect tech notes (2026-09-24).**
1. **Root cause (confirmed by a Node probe, float64 and float32 alike, so NOT a float32/`<=` grazing issue):** the **GPU is right, the CPU is wrong.** Traced ray (col 111, row 13 at 9x16 px cells): the ray crosses the 1.4 m solid cell (tower local x 16) from h 1.420 down to 1.378, so it hits the wall top at t 2.699 (row 13 lies in the cap's projected range (12.57, 13.49]). `castColumn` draws that cap row (`castPlane(..., GK_TOP)` after the solid-cell wall face), but then sets `openBottom = min(openBottom, wallRowStart - 1)`, which leaves the **cap rows open**. Only `floorFilledTo` records them, and neither the floor plane in `castFloorCeiling` nor the step-face branch clips to `floorFilledTo`. The far stair floors/risers behind (fH 0.3..1.2, grazing ray) project into the same row and **overwrite** the nearer cap sample (the G-buffer and DepthBuffer are last-write-wins). This only shows up at grazing angles over a thin top with a nearly-as-high surface behind it, which is why only kind-edge cells differ.
2. **Fix (CPU only, `engine/render/sectorCaster.js`, solid branch of `castColumn`, the line after the TOP-cap `castPlane`):** close the rows the cap drew. `openBottom = Math.min(openBottom, ctx._planeR1 >= ctx._planeR0 ? ctx._planeR0 - 1 : wallRowStart - 1);`. That is safe because the cap rows `[r0, wallRowStart-1]` plus the wall face below them are contiguous and opaque. **Rule (add it to the caster header comment):** every opaque band a segment emits must narrow `openBottom`/`openTop` to its own edge. `floorFilledTo`/`ceilingFilledTo` only clip draws and never stand in for closing the span. Do not touch `dda.frag` (it is the first-hit reference here). Keep the `hb <= N.floorH` line as it is.
3. **Probe result:** the same one-line patch in a scratch copy takes the boulder-mid-roll pose from **12 kind/depth mismatches to 0** against a JS port of `dda.frag` (the whole 160x60 grid, float64 and `Math.fround`).
4. **Tests to add:**
   - (a) `engine/render/sectorCaster.cap.test.js` (Node, inline level fixture, no `design/` import): camera at eye 1.6 m over a -0.3 m floor, one 1.4 m solid cell about 2.4 m ahead, then rising open cells at 0.3/0.6/0.9/1.2 m, pitch -20. Assert that the grazing-row cell is `GK_TOP` at depth = `(1.4-1.6)/slope` (1e-6), and that no cell in that column gets a depth greater than a nearer opaque sample's depth on the same ray.
   - (b) `compareGeometry` also **reports** `edgeKindMismatch` (a count over kind-edge cells, not gated) and prints it on the `geom:` line, so this class of bug is no longer invisible.
5. **Verify:**
   - `?gpucompare=1` and `&sun=0`: boulder mid-roll PASS (fgMax <= 64, `edgeKindMismatch` ~0), and the other 13 rows are unchanged.
   - `node tools/bench-cast.mjs`: if a test_room checksum changes, the programmer diffs the changed cells, confirms each one is a cap-overdraw cell, and re-records the checksum with a note.
   - Also run `compositor.test.js`, `lighting.test.js`, `gpuCompare.test.js` and `check-deps`.
   - Cost: none (one compare per solid cell).
6. **BUG-LIGHT-001 item 6 (OWN-001 `dLViol 3`, dLMax 0.036):** the "4 nearest" hypothesis is **ruled out**. `fbCompare` has no `cpuLightCap`, so both paths use the full list, and only the brazier is `on`. The likely cause is a vis-grid texel flip: `S = P + toLight*0.02` lands on different sides of a cell boundary (float32 P on the GPU), and at that distance the light is weak, so the flip costs only about 0.036. Check it with a readback of the 3 cells: CPU vs GPU `S`, vis cell index, vis value and falloff. If it is a boundary flip, count it like the sunlit-flag flips (<= 0.5 % of `kind != 0` cells) and then make `compareLight` gate `ok` (14.3 item 7). If it is not a flip, report back to the architect before changing any tolerance.
Acceptance: as in row 25c, plus 4(a) green and `compareLight` gating (or a documented reason why not).

**Programmer notes (2026-09-24) -> `arch-review`.**
- **Fix (item 2):** `engine/render/sectorCaster.js`, solid branch of `castColumn`: after the TOP-cap `castPlane` call, `openBottom = Math.min(openBottom, capDrawn ? ctx._planeR0 - 1 : wallRowStart - 1)` (`capDrawn = ctx._planeR1 >= ctx._planeR0`), replacing the old unconditional `wallRowStart - 1`. Added the "every opaque band must narrow its own edge" rule to the module header comment. `dda.frag` untouched.
- **Tests added:**
  - `engine/render/sectorCaster.cap.test.js` (new, inline fixture, no `design/` import): eye 1.6 m over a -0.3 m floor, a 1.4 m solid cell close ahead (entryDist 0.2 m) so the cap's own row band is wide (not sub-pixel) at a 160x60/9x16px grid, then rising open cells behind at 0.3/0.6/0.9/1.35 m (the nearest, 1.35 m, is "nearly as high" as the 1.4 m cap - the exact shape of the bug). Asserts every row in the cap's analytically-known band (rows 85-133) is `KIND_TOP` at a depth inside the cap's own `[entryDist,exitDist]`, and that no farther `KIND_FLOOR` sample lands inside that depth range. Verified this test actually catches the bug: temporarily reverted the fix locally, reran - rows 85-87 flipped to `KIND_FLOOR` at the farther cell's depth (exactly the overdraw), 4 assertions failed; restored the fix, 100/100 pass again.
  - `compareGeometry` (`engine/render/gpu/gpuCompare.js`): now also counts `edgeCells`/`edgeKindMismatch` (a kind-mismatch count restricted to the kind-edge cells the existing exclusion skips), reported only, not gated (`r.pass` unchanged). Wired into `game/js/main.js`'s `?gpucompare=1` per-pose `geometry:` line and console log as `edgeKindMismatch N/M`.
- **Item 6 (OWN-001 `dLViol 3`) - not re-investigated this pass.** Still reproduces (`?gpucompare=1`: "BUG-OWN-001 owner repro" `light: MISMATCH  dLMax 0.0363  dLViol 3`), unchanged from BUG-LIGHT-001's own report - this fix only touched CPU geometry (`castColumn`'s `openBottom`), nothing in `lighting.js`/`compareLight`, so no change was expected or observed here. The vis-grid-boundary-flip readback the architect's hypothesis calls for (CPU vs GPU `S`, vis cell index/value, falloff on the 3 cells) needs its own pass; leaving `compareLight` ungated for now rather than guessing at a tolerance. Recommend a small follow-up story/ASK ARCHITECT rather than folding it into this fix.
- **Verification:** all Node suites pass (`sectorCaster.cap.test.js` 100/100, `compositor.test.js` 10/10, `lighting.test.js` 116/116, `gpuCompare.test.js` 35/35, `glsl.test.js` 33/33, `check-deps.mjs`/`check-deps.test.mjs` clean, `physics.test.js` 187/187, `jump.test.js` 111/111, `eyeFeel.test.js` 18/18, `playerLook.test.js` 10/10). `node --expose-gc tools/bench-cast.mjs --gc`: `ALL CHECKS PASS`, every `test_room` checksum (v1 fast/reference and v2) matched the embedded baseline unchanged - this fixture's grazing geometry doesn't occur in `test_room`, so no checksum re-record was needed. Browser (own server, port 9011, stopped after): `?gpucompare=1` and `&sun=0` both **14/14 PASS**; "boulder mid-roll" fgMax/bgMax 102/77 -> **1/0**, `edgeKindMismatch 0/1144`; every other row's `edgeKindMismatch` is 0 except the pre-existing, unrelated `test_room` "facing stair + 1.0m platform" (1/1057, reported only). No console errors.
- **Known limitation:** item 6 (OWN-001 `dLViol 3`) left open as above.

**Architect review (2026-09-24, 7848887): ARCH OK -> `testing`.** Fix is exactly tech notes item 2 (cap rows closed via `_planeR0 - 1` only when the cap drew; fallback unchanged), header rule added, `dda.frag` untouched. `edgeKindMismatch` is reported only, `pass` unchanged. Re-ran: `sectorCaster.cap.test.js` 100/100, `gpuCompare.test.js` 35/35, check-deps OK, bench-cast: every checksum/baseline check OK; only the v2 timing trigger (3.5 ms) flickered FAIL from machine load, same as in the BUG-LIGHT-001 tester report, not caused by this change (one compare per solid cell). Item 6 moved to **BUG-LIGHT-002** (row 25d). Tester: `?gpucompare=1` and `&sun=0` 14/14, boulder mid-roll fgMax <= 64, `edgeKindMismatch` 0 on all rows except `test_room` facing stair (1/1057, known, reported only).

### BUG-LIGHT-002 OWN-001 `dLViol 3`: point-light vis-grid boundary flip  [Priority: P2] [Status: done]
> **Test PASS (2026-09-24, main session, 185e7a6):** `?gpucompare=1` and `&sun=0` 14/14 ALL PASS with light compare gating; see docs/test-reports/BUG-LIGHT-002.md.
Row 25d. Carried from BUG-CAST-001 tech notes item 6 / BUG-LIGHT-001 review item 6. Not an M1 exit criterion.

**Programmer notes (2026-09-24) -> `arch-review`.**
- **Check step (before touching anything):** instrumented a temporary `?lightdebug=1` readback in `game/js/main.js` (removed after use) that, for the OWN-001 pose's `dLViol` cells, printed each light's GPU-vs-CPU `litCount`, N.L, radius test, and vis-grid floor index. Result on cell (118,31): `gpuLitCount=0 cpuLitCount=1` for light 0 (the brazier) - a full inclusion/exclusion flip, not a gradual falloff difference (the magnitude, `dL=0.0363`, matches this light's own `fo*ndotl*vis` contribution almost exactly). Radius and N.L tests were nowhere near their own zero-crossing (`ndotl64=0.1248`, `dist=3.98` vs `r=6`) in either precision. The vis-grid sample point, `P.x - visOx`, printed as exactly `6.000000` on both paths at display precision - i.e. the "toward the light" 0.02 nudge (BUG-LIGHT-001's fix) landed the sample almost exactly back on a vis-grid cell boundary. That is consistent with the architect's hypothesis (BUG-CAST-001 notes item 6 / architecture.md 14.3 item 5): CPU float64 and GPU float32 round that near-integer value to opposite sides, `floor()` picks a different vis cell (255 vs 0 nearby), and the whole light flips on/off.
- **Fix (minimal, both paths):** biased `sampleVis`'s floor by a small fixed epsilon, `VIS_FLOOR_EPS = 1e-3` (comfortably above the ~1.8e-4 worst-case float32 absolute precision at world coordinates in the low thousands, far below a 1 m cell) - `Math.floor(x - ox + VIS_FLOOR_EPS)` in `engine/render/lighting.js`, `floor(px - box.x + VIS_FLOOR_EPS)` in `engine/render/gpu/glsl/light.frag.js` (same constant name/value in both, per the story's ask). This makes both paths commit to the same side of a near-boundary sample regardless of which precision computed it, without changing any real (non-boundary) vis-grid lookup.
- **Tests added:** `engine/render/lighting.test.js` (`sampleVis`/`VIS_FLOOR_EPS` now exported for testability) - a value exactly on a vis-grid cell boundary and a value +/-1e-7 off it read the same cell (the boundary-flip regression), plus a sanity check that the epsilon is small enough not to blur a real one-cell-away difference (LOS cell vs a cell behind a wall). 120/120 pass (116 -> 120, +4).
- **Verify:** `?gpucompare=1` and `&sun=0` both **14/14 PASS**, OWN-001 row now `light: OK  dLViol 0` on both (previously `MISMATCH  dLViol 3`, `dLMax 0.0363`); every other row's `light:` line unchanged (`OK`, `dLViol 0`). No console errors. Node: `lighting.test.js` 120/120, `gpuCompare.test.js` 35/35, `sectorCaster.cap.test.js` 100/100, `physics.test.js` 187/187, `jump.test.js` 111/111, `eyeFeel.test.js` 18/18, `playerLook.test.js` 10/10, `check-deps.mjs` clean (127 files). `node --expose-gc tools/bench-cast.mjs --gc`: every checksum/geometry/GC check OK; only the `v2 timing` vs 3.5 ms escalation trigger flickered FAIL from machine load - the same known noise flagged in the BUG-CAST-001 tester report, not caused by this change (this fix is float-math-only, no extra work per cell). Browser: own server, ports 9015-9020 (each stopped after use), owner's port 8000 untouched.
- **`compareLight` gating:** left as reported-only (`pass` field computed but not read by `ok`/`overallOk` in `game/js/main.js`) - the story's ask was to fix the flip and prove `dLViol` is 0 across every pose first; flipping the gate is a one-line change (`const ok = cmpCells.pass && cmpGeom.pass && cmpLight.pass;`) the architect may want to make explicitly rather than have it ride in with an unrelated fix.
- **Scope note:** this fix only touches the point-light vis grid (14.3 item 5). The sun DDA (14.3 item 4) is a separate boundary-flip surface with its own nudge/epsilon and was out of scope here (also already known-unaffected: `&sun=0` was 14/14 before this fix too).

**Architect review (2026-09-24, 451c821): ARCH OK -> `testing`.** Same constant/expression in both `sampleVis`s. Non-boundary safety: the bias only moves the floor boundary from k to k-1e-3; the 0.02 toward-light nudge always ends >= 0.019 m from an integer face boundary on either side, so a nudged sample never lands in the shifted band, and any other point in [k-1e-3, k) reads cell k on both paths (consistent, a 1 mm shift, well under a cell); 1e-3 is about 8x float32 ulp at 1500 m. Re-ran `lighting.test.js` 120/120, `gpuCompare.test.js` 35/35, check-deps OK. **Gate:** `compareLight.pass` (sunlit flips <= 0.5 % and dLViol 0) now gates `ok`/`overallOk` in `game/js/main.js` (14.3 item 7; one-line change made by the architect). Tester: `?gpucompare=1` and `&sun=0` must still be 14/14 ALL PASS with the gate on; every row `light: OK`.

### US-045 WebGL2-required screen + software-renderer warning  [Priority: P0] [Status: done]

PO OK (2026-09-24): AC1-3 verified in `webgl2Gate.js` + `main.js` (gate blocks before `createEngine`'s rAF branches, `?gpu=0`/`force2d=1` bypass, software path unchanged). PO OK - US-045 ready for testing.
Tester notes:
- Use `?webgl2gate=none` and `?webgl2gate=software` (real hardware won't show these); confirm no console error on the blocked screen and exactly one `console.warn` on the software path.
- Check AC4 by hand at 240x90 and 320x120 with F3 overlay and pause menu open (DOM overlay is viewport-anchored, not grid-anchored, per programmer notes - not yet eyeballed).
- Confirm `?webgl2gate=none&gpu=0` still runs the game normally (gate skip precedence).
As a player whose browser can't run WebGL2 (or is falling back to a software renderer), I want a clear message instead of a broken or silently slow game, so that I know what to do.
(D-017: no playable CPU fallback; this replaces the old auto-fallback-to-CPU behaviour.)
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] No WebGL2 support, or `gl2` context creation fails: a full-screen message replaces the game canvas ("WebGL2 required to play" + a short how-to: update your browser / enable hardware acceleration in browser settings) styled with `uiStyle`; no crash, no console error, no game loop running underneath.
- [ ] A software renderer is detected (`UNMASKED_RENDERER_WEBGL` contains SwiftShader, llvmpipe or Basic Render Driver): the game still starts on the GPU path, with a one-line dismissible warning ("Performance may be poor on this device") shown for a few seconds (or until dismissed) and logged once to the console; no other behaviour change.
- [ ] `?gpu=0` still shows the JS reference in-browser as a dev/debug switch (unaffected by this story).
- [ ] Works the same at 240x90 and 320x120; no layout overlap with the F3 overlay or pause menu.
Design needed: no (uses `uiStyle` text/panel styling already established).
Notes / dependencies: D-017. No architect tech notes needed (UI/game-only, no engine render/world/physics/core/entities changes).

Programmer notes (2026-09-24): `game/js/ui/webgl2Gate.js` (new, game-only) probes a throwaway canvas at the very top of `game/js/main.js` (before `createEngine`), using the already-public `engine/index.js` export `isSoftwareRenderer` (no engine changes). `?gpu=0`/`?force2d=1` skip the gate entirely (dev switches, D-017 items 3/4). No real WebGL2 -> `showWebgl2RequiredScreen` hides `#screen` and shows a full-screen DOM message (palette colors, not a canvas draw - no `uiStyle` layout coords needed since it isn't scene text), and the bottom `runGame`/`runBenchmark`/etc. dispatch in main.js is skipped so no rAF loop starts (`createEngine` still runs harmlessly on the hidden canvas, same as the old fallback path, just never rendered/looped). Software renderer -> `showSoftwareRendererWarning`, a dismissible top-center DOM banner (auto-hides 6s), `console.warn` once; game proceeds unchanged. Added a test-only `?webgl2gate=none|software` override (not an AC) so both screens can be exercised without swapping GPUs - real owner hardware is GPU-backed so this is the only way to see the AC-1 screen locally. Verified: `node tools/check-deps.mjs`, `node tools/check-deps.test.mjs`, `physics.test.js`, `jump.test.js`, `eyeFeel.test.js`, `playerLook.test.js` all pass; browser-checked on a fresh port 8983 (stopped after) with `?webgl2gate=none` (full-screen message, no console errors), `?webgl2gate=software` (game runs, warning banner, one console.warn), and `?webgl2gate=none&gpu=0` (game still runs normally, gate skipped). Not checked: real hardware with no WebGL2 at all (owner has a GPU) and 240x90/320x120 grid layout overlap with F3/pause (the DOM overlay is viewport-anchored, not grid-anchored, so it shouldn't scale with grid, but a human eyeball pass is still worth it).

### US-019 Dust motes in the sun shaft  [Priority: P2] [Status: todo]
As a player, I want to see tiny specks of dust drifting in the sunlight, so that the light feels volumetric.
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] 40-80 particles within the sun shaft volume, drift slowly (0.05-0.15 m/s) with gentle noise, drawn as `.` / `'` in sun color, visible only while in sunlight.
- [ ] Costs <= 0.5 ms per frame.
Design needed: minor (glyphs/colors, designer confirms).
Notes / dependencies: US-007, US-011.

### US-020 Sound  [Priority: P2] [Status: todo]
As a player, I want to hear the fire crackle, my steps, the boulder and the wind, so that the space feels real.
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] WebAudio procedural synthesis only (noise-based crackle, wind, clicks/thuds); no audio asset files, no `game/assets/audio/` folder in M1 (D-004).
- [ ] Audio context starts after the first user input (no autoplay errors in the console).
- [ ] Sounds per GDD 7.5: brazier crackle attenuates with distance; footsteps on stone with slight pitch variation; boulder roll + thud; lever clunk; grate rattle; wind grows near the breach.
- [ ] `N` key toggles mute. (Was `M`; `M` now opens the map card, US-015/D-011. In M2 mute also moves into Settings, US-038.)
- [ ] (D-011) The brazier crackle is the *Kestrel* burner's crackle-and-hiss; the lever adds a gear ratchet.
Design needed: no.
Notes / dependencies: picked up only when every P0 is `done` and US-022 is done or explicitly deferred. Scope cap: one story; if it grows, cut sounds rather than extend. Not an M1 exit criterion; testers do not fail M1 on audio.

### US-021 Relay-keeper's log (was: readable wall scrawl)  [Priority: P2] [Status: design]
As a player, I want to find an old relay-keeper's log scratched into the stone near where I came to, so that I learn someone out here heard the signal long before me.

**D-011 reskin (PO, 2026-09-23):** `KEEP THE LIGHT` is replaced by the relay-keeper's log (`docs/story.md` section 4, 5 lines, each <= 60 chars). 60-char lines cannot be read as a wall decal at the M1 grids, so the log is a **visible decal plus a read panel**.
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] Decal material: rows of scratched tally marks and letters on one wall panel near the wake spot (about 1.5 m wide), clearly "writing" from 2 m, fading into the stone texture at > 5 m. It does not need to be legible as text.
- [ ] An interactable on the decal (US-012 rules) shows `[E] Read`. Pressing E opens the log text in the US-015 panel overlay (same dim and fade, style `uiStyle.logPanel`: scratched-stone plate, pale grey text); any key closes it. Readable at 160x60 and 240x90.
- [ ] The text comes from level/UI data, not code. It matches `docs/story.md` section 4 exactly and does not name Wick (D-013). It keeps "the SOS has been sending for years" vague (D-013 item 3).
- [ ] Reading is optional: nothing else depends on it. Restart resets nothing here (the log can be read any number of times).
Design needed: yes – decal glyph pattern + log panel style (low priority for designer).
Notes / dependencies: US-004, US-010, US-012 (interaction), US-015 (panel overlay).

### US-022 Wake the relay with the lamp (was: light the summit beacon)  [Priority: P1] [Status: todo]
As a player, I want to wake the dead relay with the *Kestrel*'s lamp before I step out, so that the slice ends on a moment of wonder and shows the core loop.

**D-011 reskin (PO, 2026-09-23) – "light the beacon" becomes "wake the relay".** Same interaction, conditions, one-way rule, performance rule and restart rule. The following replace the matching ACs below:
- Prompt `[E] Wake the relay` (was `[E] Light the beacon`), shown only while carrying the lamp.
- Effect: no fire. The relay's crystal cells switch from the dead to the woken state (US-011 reskin art) and grow in over 1.0 s from `mounts.glow`, with emissive teal cells and a `* + .` sparkle (4 frames at 6 fps).
- Light: an **aether teal** point light (palette `aether`, designer sets the exact colour) at the bowl centre, about 0.8 m above it, ramps 0 to 1.0 over 1.0 s, radius 12 m target (minimum 8 m, same budget rule). A slow shimmer of ±5% replaces the fire flicker. It must read clearly as not-fire next to the orange burner and the amber lamp.
- The lamp is not consumed. The signal tower and Ferrum's lights (US-016) do not change in M1.
- End text uses the woken variant (US-017).
- This is the first hint of magic (GDD pillar 6): no text explains it.
Acceptance criteria (original; read "beacon" as the relay, "fire/flames" as the glow, per the block above):
- [ ] Targeting the beacon bowl (US-012 rules, 1.8 m / ~20 degrees) while carrying the lantern shows `[E] Light the beacon`. Without the lantern: no prompt (the bowl is not interactable).
- [ ] Pressing E ignites the bowl: flames grow from 0 to full over 1.0 s using the delivered `beaconFire` model (3 brazier flame units, 10 fps) and its `grow` rule (`design/README.md` section 4). The fire is anchored at `beaconBowl.mounts.fire`, and flame cells are emissive.
- [ ] A new point light starts at the bowl centre (about 0.8 m above the ash): warm orange (brazier `#ff9a3c` family), intensity ramps 0 to 1.0 over 1.0 s, radius 12 m target, same flicker model as the brazier (8-12 Hz, ±15%).
- [ ] Performance: at the summit view (looking out the breach with the beacon lit) the US-018 budget holds (>= 58 fps). If not, reduce the beacon radius (minimum 8 m) until it does; the feature is never cut for performance. The final radius is recorded in the tuning config.
- [ ] The lantern is not consumed: the player keeps it and its light after lighting the beacon.
- [ ] One-way: once lit, the bowl shows no prompt and stays lit until restart.
- [ ] Optional: the breach end trigger (US-017) works whether or not the beacon is lit; end text uses the lit variant `One beacon burns. The others are dark.` only when lit.
- [ ] The distant second tower (US-016) stays dark after lighting; nothing in the far view changes.
- [ ] Restart (R) resets the beacon to unlit.
Design needed: no new story. The relay dead/woken art and the `aether` palette come with the US-011 reskin and the US-016 addendum (D-011); the old `beaconFire` model is dropped.
Notes / dependencies: US-011, US-012, US-016, US-017, US-018. Picked up only after every P0 story is `done`. Not an M1 exit criterion.

### US-023 See-through grate (masked walls)  [Priority: P2] [Status: todo]
As a player, I want to see the upper stair through the bars of the grate, so that the portcullis looks like real ironwork and hints at the way up.
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] Wall texels flagged `hole: true` in a material (the `grate` material) are transparent. The ray continues behind the masked face, and the geometry and sky behind it are drawn through the gaps.
- [ ] Works while the grate animates (US-014): the masked face shrinks as `ceilH` rises, with no tearing at the moving edge.
- [ ] Sprites behind the grate are occluded only by the bar texels, not by the gaps.
- [ ] Cost: at most 0.5 ms extra per frame when the grate fills the view; US-018 still holds.
Design needed: no (the `grate` material with `hole` texels was delivered with US-011).
Notes / dependencies: US-004, US-011, US-014. Picked up only after every P0 story is `done`. Not an M1 exit criterion. Until then the M1 fallback draws the gaps dark (US-011).

### US-024 Engine/game split (D-006)  [Priority: P0] [Status: po-review]
As a player (and as the future editor's first user), I want the engine to be a clean, reusable library underneath the game, so that the world can grow and an editor can later build on it without rewrites.
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] **Layout per D-006:** `engine/{core,render,world,physics,entities,ui}/`, `game/{index.html, world-test.html, js/main.js, js/quest/}`, `design/` unchanged, and `tools/check-deps.mjs`. Everything built so far is moved (git-less: move plus fixed imports):
  - RenderTarget*, CellBuffer, glyphMetrics, DepthBuffer, the US-004 caster as `sectorCaster.js`
  - loop, input
  - Level.js (plus `MAP_FORMAT.md` next to it)
  - debugOverlay
  - US-008 physics
  - `test_room` moves to `design/levels/` or `engine/world/testdata/`. PO preference: `design/levels/test_room.js` as a content-pack level. It must stay loadable.
  - Nothing engine-level stays under `game/js/` (only `main.js` and `quest/`).
- [ ] **Public API:** `engine/index.js` is the only public entry. It re-exports the D-006 rule 5 API:
  - `createEngine({ canvas, assets, cols, rows })` returns `{ renderTarget, world, input, loop, camera, events }`
  - `AssetRegistry`, `loadLevel`, `World` (a stub until US-025), `castSectors`
  - `castTerrain` and `drawSprites` stubs (throw "not implemented" until US-016 / US-011), `drawText`
  - `moveCapsule`, `moveSphere`, `integrate`, `Entity`, `Camera`, and `serialize` / `deserialize` stubs (US-025)
  - `game/` imports only from `engine/index.js`, never from deep engine paths.
- [ ] **No globals in the engine:**
  - `engine/` never reads `window.ASSETS` (or any `window.*` other than DOM/timing APIs it needs: `requestAnimationFrame`, `devicePixelRatio`, events).
  - `game/js/main.js` builds `new AssetRegistry({ palette, models, levels, terrain })` from the `design/` classic-script globals and passes it in.
  - `AssetRegistry` documents the palette, model, level and terrain-recipe shapes as JSDoc typedefs (`engine/core/assets.js`) and throws a clear error for an unknown key.
- [ ] **`tools/check-deps.mjs`** (Node, no dependencies, run manually: `node tools/check-deps.mjs`):
  - Scans `engine/**/*.js` imports and exits non-zero, listing file and line, if any import resolves outside `engine/`.
  - Also flags `window.ASSETS` inside `engine/`.
  - Reports OK on the moved codebase. The tester runs it for every later story.
- [ ] **Nothing regresses:**
  - `game/index.html` (default, `?debug=1`, `?bench=1`, `?glyphs=1`, `?shadetest=1`, `?force2d=1`, `?demo=1`) and `game/world-test.html` work as before.
  - `?level=test_room` still loads.
  - The US-004 view looks pixel-identical before and after the move (screenshot compare at the same camera).
  - No console errors. `?bench=1` numbers are unchanged within noise.
- [ ] Docs: a short `engine/README.md` (purpose, folder map, public API list, "engine never imports game/design" rule, how to run `check-deps`). `CLAUDE.md` layout section updated. Coordinate that edit with the manager, since CLAUDE.md is project instructions.
Design needed: no.
Notes / dependencies: after US-004 and US-008 reach `po-review`, before US-006 starts (D-006 rule 6). New files created before then should already go to the new paths. The designer's previews must keep working; they load `design/*.js` directly and do not depend on the engine.

**Tech notes (architect, 2026-09-22).** Reusable parts (layers, API typedefs, conventions, allocation rules, check-deps rules) are in `docs/architecture.md` sections 2, 3, 5, 6; this note is the move plan.

*Principle: move, do not refactor.* US-024 changes paths, import lines, one rename (`castScene` -> `castSectors`, kept internally as an alias until US-004b/US-016 adopt `FrameBuffers`), and adds `index.js`, `assets.js`, `check-deps.mjs`, `README.md`. No behaviour changes; the `CellBuffer.glyphIdx` checksum at a fixed camera is the regression test.

*File moves (old -> new):*
| Old | New |
|---|---|
| `game/js/render/RenderTarget.js`, `RenderTargetGL.js`, `RenderTargetCanvas2D.js`, `CellBuffer.js`, `glyphMetrics.js`, `DepthBuffer.js` | `engine/render/` same names |
| `game/js/render/raycaster.js` | `engine/render/sectorCaster.js` (export `castSectors`; `castScene` alias, not exported from index) |
| `game/js/render/shadeTest.js` | `engine/render/shadeTest.js` (takes the palette as a parameter already) |
| `game/js/render/palette.js` (placeholder) | **deleted**: the registry requires a real palette; `getDefaultRamp` callers use `assets.palette.ramps.default` |
| `game/js/render/demoScene.js`, `glyphsScene.js`, `benchScene.js` | `game/js/dev/` (page harnesses; import only `engine/index.js`) |
| `game/js/engine/loop.js`, `input.js` | `engine/core/loop.js`, `engine/core/input.js` |
| `game/js/engine/debugCamera.js` | `game/js/dev/debugCamera.js` (dev harness, replaced by US-005/US-008; not engine API) |
| `game/js/ui/debugOverlay.js` | `engine/ui/debugOverlay.js` |
| `game/js/world/Level.js`, `MAP_FORMAT.md` | `engine/world/Level.js`, `engine/world/MAP_FORMAT.md` |
| `game/js/world/levels/test_room.js` | `design/levels/test_room.js` as a classic script (`ASSETS.levels.test_room`, plus `module.exports` under Node like `palette.js`) |
| `game/js/world/worldTestMain.js` | `game/js/dev/worldTestMain.js` |
| `game/js/physics/config.js`, `capsule.js` | `engine/physics/config.js` (export `PHYSICS_DEFAULTS`; `PHYSICS` alias kept for tests), `engine/physics/capsule.js` |
| `game/js/physics/physics.test.js` | `engine/physics/physics.test.js` (imports stay relative inside engine; `test_room` via `createRequire` of `design/levels/test_room.js`) |
| `game/js/physics/physicsTestMain.js` | `game/js/dev/physicsTestMain.js` |
| `game/js/entities/Player.js` | `engine/entities/Player.js` (first-person capsule controller; US-025 splits it into `Entity` data + `integrate`) |
| `game/js/main.js` | stays; imports only `engine/index.js` and reads `window.ASSETS` once |
| new | `engine/index.js`, `engine/core/assets.js`, `engine/core/events.js` (tiny emitter), `engine/core/behaviours.js`, `engine/render/OpenSpans.js`, `engine/README.md`, `tools/check-deps.mjs` |
| stubs (throw `not implemented (US-xxx)`) | `engine/world/World.js`, `engine/world/Terrain.js`, `engine/world/serialize.js`, `engine/render/terrainCaster.js`, `engine/render/sprites.js`, `engine/render/textDraw.js` (this one is real: 20 lines over `setCell`), `engine/physics/sphere.js`, `engine/physics/integrate.js`, `engine/entities/Entity.js`, `engine/entities/Camera.js` |

*HTML pages:* `game/index.html` adds classic `<script>` tags for every `design/` file the registry needs (`palette.js`, `levels/test_room.js`, later `levels/tower.js`, `levels/overworld_far.js`, `levels/world_m1.js`, `models/*.js`) before the module. `world-test.html` -> `js/dev/worldTestMain.js`, `physics-test.html` -> `js/dev/physicsTestMain.js`, both also load `design/palette.js` + `design/levels/test_room.js` classic scripts. `?level=name` reads `assets.level(name)`.

*API signatures introduced by this story (normative, architecture.md 5-6):*
- `createEngine({ canvas, assets, cols=160, rows=60, force2d=false, physics? , inputTarget? })` -> `{ renderTarget, depthBuffer, openSpans, world: null, input, loop, camera, events, assets, physics, loadWorld(def), run({update, render}) }`. `loop` is created but not started; `run` starts it.
- `new AssetRegistry({ palette, models?, levels?, terrain?, worlds?, uiStyle? })`, `AssetRegistry.fromGlobals(window.ASSETS)` (splits `ASSETS.levels` into `levels` (has `rows`) and `terrain` (has `util.heightAt`)), `assets.palette`, `assets.level(key)`, `assets.model(key)`, `assets.terrain(key)`, `assets.world(key)`, `assets.uiStyle`; unknown key throws `AssetRegistry: unknown level "x" (known: test_room, tower)`.
- `castSectors(fb, level, cam, origin)` with `fb = { rt, depth, spans, palette, lights: null, timeSec }`; `beginFrame(fb)`; `fillSky(fb, cam)`; `renderWorld` is a stub until US-025. `main.js` for `test_room` calls `beginFrame`, `castSectors(fb, level, cam, ORIGIN0)`, `fillSky(fb, cam)`, `rt.present()`. That sequence replaces `skyFallback: true` and produces the identical image (sky is still painted into whatever is open).
- `drawText(rt, x, y, text, fg, bg?)`.
- `registerBehaviour(name, fn)` (+ aliases `registerInteraction`, `registerTrigger`), `getBehaviour(name)`.

*Sequencing so it does not collide with US-005 (programmer #1: `game/js/engine/input.js`, `debugCamera.js`, `main.js`) and US-008/US-009 (programmer #2: `game/js/physics/*`, `game/js/entities/Player.js`):*
- **Phase A (can start now, one programmer, ~half a day, no in-flight file touched):** create `engine/index.js`, `core/assets.js`, `core/events.js`, `core/behaviours.js`, `render/OpenSpans.js`, `render/textDraw.js`, all stubs, `engine/README.md`, `tools/check-deps.mjs`. Move the files nobody is editing: `render/*` (except the dev scenes), `ui/debugOverlay.js`, `core/loop.js`, `world/Level.js` + `MAP_FORMAT.md`, `test_room.js` -> `design/levels/`. Leave **forwarding shims** at every old path that in-flight code imports (`game/js/world/Level.js`: `export * from '../../../engine/world/Level.js';`, same for `levels/test_room.js` exporting the global as default, `render/RenderTarget.js`, `render/DepthBuffer.js`, `render/raycaster.js`, `engine/loop.js`, `ui/debugOverlay.js`). Shims are legal (game -> engine) and keep `physics.test.js`, `physicsTestMain.js`, `worldTestMain.js` and the two programmers' working copies running untouched. `main.js` is **not** edited in Phase A (US-005 owns it); it keeps working through the shims. `check-deps` already passes on `engine/`.
- **Phase B (after US-005 reaches `po-review`):** move `input.js` -> `engine/core/input.js`, `debugCamera.js` -> `game/js/dev/`, rewrite `main.js` to `AssetRegistry.fromGlobals` + `createEngine` + `beginFrame/castSectors/fillSky`, remove `render/palette.js`. Delete the render/loop/ui shims.
- **Phase C (after US-009 reaches `po-review`):** move `physics/*`, `entities/Player.js`, `physics.test.js`; fix their relative imports; delete the world shims. `node engine/physics/physics.test.js` passes unchanged.

Story goes to `po-review` only after D.
